# UX spec

What every surface looks like, how it moves and what it does, with the measured value and its source for each. Decision 6 sets the rule: Arc's structure, behavior and timings, Dia's command bar and motion, and ilo's own look. [ux-parity/](ux-parity/README.md) is the checklist built from this file, and [ux-principles.md](ux-principles.md) says why Arc's values are what they are. The last section is the plan for measuring Arc before it shuts down on 2026-12-22.

## Attribution

Parts of this file and of `ux-parity/` are adapted from Ardia (https://github.com/mantrakp04/ardia), from `docs/dia-spec.md` and `docs/dia-feature-parity.md`, with supporting values from `docs/omnibox-ux.md` and `apps/browser/src/lib/profileTheme.ts`. They are taken at tag `v0.2.40-nightly.5`, commit `fce3a02ffb917d2902af4ce47b398ce8b03a440d`. Copyright 2026 Mantra Patel. Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0). Changes from the originals: the material is reorganized by our features, Ardia's implementation is removed, and it is merged with our decisions and with Arc notes. The adapted parts keep their Apache-2.0 terms inside this GPL-3.0-or-later repository. Ardia's authors measured the values from Dia, a browser by The Browser Company. No Dia or Arc assets are included here.

## Reading this spec

**Precedence.**

1. Decision 5 overrides everything else. A Space holds tabs, pinned tabs and a colour. A profile holds cookies, history, logins and extensions. Dia and Ardia merge the two, so where a Dia value below says "profile", read it as "Space".
2. Structure comes from Arc: what exists, where it sits, layout sizes and behavior. That covers the sidebar, Spaces, favorites, pinned and Today tabs, archive, split view, Peek, Little Arc and Boosts.
3. The command bar and motion come from Dia. Materials, colours, radii, type and icons are ilo's own (decision 6); a Dia or Arc look value below stands until its ilo-look issue replaces it. Dia's surfaces built around its assistant, such as its New Tab page, aren't copied (decision 6). Interactions that only Arc has (sidebar reveal, Clear, Peek, Little Arc) keep Arc's motion.
4. Where an Arc value hasn't been measured yet, the Dia value is the target and is marked *interim*. The Arc capture plan at the end lists what will replace it.
5. Values marked **Ardia** are Ardia's own choices, not measurements of Dia or Arc. Treat them as placeholders.

**Sources.** `D›X` is section X of Ardia's `docs/dia-spec.md`. `P§n` is section n of `docs/dia-feature-parity.md`. `O` is `docs/omnibox-ux.md`. Dia versions are 1.49.1, 1.50.1, 1.52 and 1.53.0. *bin* means read from Dia's or Arc's binary or asset catalogs, *cap* a 2× screen capture, *rec* a 60–240 fps recording. **Arc (Ardia)** marks Arc values Ardia recorded: Ardia's owner's Arc recordings and measurements of 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-22, a user's screenshot, and Arc's binary (Arc 1.167 for themes). **Chrome** means Chromium's own behavior. **Flecha** marks behavior read from Flecha's site and code at v0.1.0 (https://flecha.dev, MIT). **Helium** marks Helium's. **Ours** marks our decisions.

**Units.** Sizes are points. Times are ms or s. Colours are sRGB unless marked P3. "white 0.16" means white at alpha 0.16. Where two values appear, dark comes first, then light. Arc and Dia were measured in dark mode, so light values are their asset-catalog tokens and no light capture exists yet. Springs are given as response and damping ratio ζ (mass 1, stiffness (2π/response)², damping 4πζ/response). Curves are given as `cubic-bezier(x1, y1, x2, y2)` and a duration.

**Assets and names.** SF Symbol names identify a shape only. We draw our own icons, and they have to work off the Mac too. The names Little Arc, Top Apps and Dia's mark are references, not UI text. A feature named Peek is Arc's link preview. Ardia uses "peek" for the hidden sidebar sliding in, which this spec calls the *sidebar reveal*.

## Shared rules

### Window material (Dia 1.50.1 bin, D›Window translucency)

Layers, bottom to top. There is no grain: 1.50.1 measures 0.05–0.09 levels of noise.

| Layer | Dark | Light |
|---|---|---|
| Blur behind the window: emphasized, follows the window's active state | private material 29 | material 13 (`.hudWindow`) |
| Base tint, full opacity | black 0.4 | white 0.8 |
| Space gradient: the Space colour at the top, the same colour at HSL lightness + 0.25 (clamped) at the bottom, each stop at 0.36 (0.12 for a neutral colour) | in a layer at 0.5 | in a layer at 0.75 |
| Content card | #121212 0.5 | white 0.7 |

- Dark composite: `out = 0.18·tint_y + 0.82·0.6·blur`. Light: `out = 0.27·tint_y + 0.73·(0.8·255 + 0.2·blur)`. Here `tint_y` runs down the gradient.
- When the window is inactive, AppKit swaps the blur for an opaque fill tinted by the wallpaper. Reduce Transparency and Increase Contrast do the same. Dia adds no fallback of its own. Compare captures only in the same key state.
- With no Space colour set, the gradient uses the system accent colour and follows its changes (Dia).
- Fitted Space colour (Dia's pink): P3 (177.6, 91.1, 106.8), which is #B25B6B in P3 and ≈ #BF556A in sRGB (HSL 349°, 0.36, 0.53). The other colours are unmeasured.

Material output measured over solid backdrops: black, sRGB grey levels 20 and 60, and white. Ports without AppKit materials approximate these (sRGB, active state):

| Material | Over black / grey 20 / grey 60 / white | Inactive fill |
|---|---|---|
| 29, dark | 24 / 32 / 45 / 104 | (52, 43, 41), tinted by the wallpaper |
| 29, light | 192 / 197 / 208 / 255 | 248 |
| 13, light | 137 / 146 / 165 / 243 | (239, 237, 236) |
| 13, dark | 33 / 44 / 72 / 182 | (58, 54, 53) |

- Ours (*interim*):
  - The window is light where its Space's theme asks for it or, automatic, where the system's appearance is light; a private window stays dark. Light is the table's light layers, material 13 under white 0.8, with text, glyphs and plates in Ink at dark's alphas. Where the window has to be opaque, light's grounds are Dia's composite over black and white, #E7E7E7 and #FDFDFD, and its stand-in is the composite over grey 20, #E9E9E9, until a check VM measures ours. The theme and icon pickers and tooltips stay dark over a light window.
  - A colour under HSL saturation 0.1 counts as neutral.
  - Where the window's look has to be opaque, it stands the material in with Dia's composite over grey 20 before the gradient, #131313, and puts the gradient over that. That's the reveal's panel over the page and a dragged row.
  - Base tint black 0.25, not 0.4. Material 29 reads darker in our window than in the table above: under black 0.4 our sidebar read 11 over black and 51 over white, the material at 18 and 85 against 24 and 104, two thirds of it in linear light at both ends. Under black 0.25 a check VM reads 23 / 27 / 34 / 70 over black / grey 20 / grey 60 / white, against Dia's 24 / 29 / 36 / 69. Ours is darker than Dia's over mid grey than at the ends, so no one tint puts all four within 2 levels; the `window` check fails past 3. The opaque fill of an inactive window and of Reduce Transparency is under the same lighter tint, unmeasured.

### Colour tokens (Arc 1.170 and Dia asset catalogs, D›Color tokens)

Arc's tokens beside Dia's (unchanged 1.49.1 → 1.50.1), and the one ours uses, which the section named gives in full. Arc's whites and blacks are often P3 #FAFBFF and P3 #0E0F10 rather than pure, and it composites in P3. *guess* marks a match by value or name that no capture confirms. The token names are Arc's, kept to find them again in its catalogs; they never become ours.

| Tab unselected | no fill (cap) | white 0.06 / black 0.06 | Arc's: a row has no fill until it's hovered or selected |
| Tab title selected / unselected | — | white 1 / 0.78; black 1 / 0.68 | Dia's (Tab rows) |
| Favorite tile resting fill / stroke | fill as Tab hovered, *guess*; no stroke | white 0.10 / 0.14; black 0.05 / 0.18 | Dia's |
| Favorite tile default selection ring | — | white 0.45 / black 0.48 | Dia's |
| Menu item hovered | — | white 0.07 / black 0.07 | Dia's |
| Menu hairline border | — | white 0.30 / black 0.25 | Dia's |
| Command bar row hover | white 0.05 / black 0.05: (31, 31, 31) on the (19, 19, 19) panel (rec) | rgba(120,125,134,0.32) / rgba(15,24,44,0.08) | Arc's |
| Command bar Go pill | — | as Dia's row hover | Dia's |
| Command bar placeholder | white ≈ 0.55 (cap) | white 0.53 / black 0.40 | Dia's |
| Command bar title / dim text | white 0.8 / 0.35 (cap), white and 0.5 on the plate; black 0.80 / 0.33 | white / rgba(239,244,255,0.6); #0F182C / #0F182C 0.6 | Arc's |
| Command bar caret | the system accent (cap) | #4A77D4 / #6395FC | Dia's (Command bar) |
| Command bar selected text | — | rgba(120,125,134,0.7) / — | Dia's |

| Subtle fill | — | white 0.12 / black 0.12 | Dia's |
| Separator, sidebar | white 0.15 / black 0.15 (`SidebarSeparator`; cap 0.14–0.16) | P3 white 0.09 / P3 black 0.09 | Arc's (Clear divider) |
| Separator, panels | white 0.10 / black 0.10 (cap) | P3 white 0.09 / P3 black 0.09 | Arc's |

Controls Arc wasn't recorded with take the sidebar rows' steps as one ladder (`[palette]` in `app/design/tokens.toml`): white 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed, laid over a control's own resting plate where it has one and capped at the selected row's 0.20. Where a section below says "the ladder's", it means these.

Light values are tokens only, never captured. Arc's tokens with no row here are in its asset catalogs, which stay readable after Arc's servers close.

### Motion

Every curve used in this spec. Each feature's section says where it applies.

| Name | Value | Used for | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Row motion | spring 0.13 s / ζ 0.9 (CSS ease ≈ 115–120 ms): within 0.75 pt of rest 83–92 ms in | rows making or closing room; a closed row fading, its opacity the slide's remaining fraction; a new tab's plate fading in; rows Undo brings back fading in | Arc 1.170.0 rec 120 fps: ⌘T then a pick, ⌘W, Undo after Clear |
| Row hover | one frame in and out. Only the selected row eases back from its hover fill to its selected fill, over about 170 ms (ours: ease-out, `cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.58, 1)`). A shortcut, with the keyboard in the UI or the page, takes the hover away until the pointer moves (ours), so the row the pointer rests on never reads as selected beside the tab a key showed | a row's hover fill under the pointer, and a tile's (ours); selection moves at once (Tab rows › Behavior) | Arc 1.170 rec at 2×: one frame both ways from row to row; the selected row 115 → 80 over 145.27–145.45 s |
| Favorites room | critically damped, ω ≈ 25 rad/s: 38 % in 50 ms, 73 % in 100 ms, settled in about 240 ms | the tiles making room for a dragged tab, and the list's top with them; ours: a dragged tab turning between its row and its tile | Arc 1.170 rec at 2×, 2 runs (ω 24–27) |
| Hover card in | opacity cubic-bezier(0.25, 0.46, 0.45, 0.94) 100 ms; scale 0.92 → 1 spring 0.25 s / ζ 0.75 | tab and favorite hover card | Dia bin, D›Tab hover card motion |
| Hover card out | opacity same curve 100 ms; scale → 0.92 spring 0.2 s / ζ 0.6 | same | same |
| Split fold | CSS ease, cubic-bezier(0.25, 0.1, 0.25, 1), 120 ms; the title keeps its width and fades with its share | a split row's segments trading widths as another pane takes the keyboard (Split view › In the sidebar) | ours: Arc's row doesn't fold; the length is the row motion's |
| Scroll edge | cubic-bezier(0.25, 0.46, 0.45, 0.94), 250 ms | the list's edge lines, under the favorites and at its foot, fading in and out | Arc 1.17x (0.2–0.3 s); the curve Dia 1.53.0 bin |
| List to a row | 0.22 s, `1 − (1 − t)^18`: most of the way in the first frame, still at the end; a scroll of the user's ends it where it is | the list scrolling to a row it shows | Arc 1.171 at 120 fps; the curve ours |
| Sidebar hide | ease-out cubic, 110 ms: within 0.75 pt of rest 67–83 ms in | ⌘S hiding a docked sidebar | Arc 1.170.0 rec 120 fps, 3 runs |

| Sidebar reveal in | no fade: spring 0.175 s / ζ 0.80, ≈ 4 pt past rest, starting ≈ 100 ms after the pointer reaches the edge | sidebar reveal at the left edge | Arc 1.170.0 rec 120 fps, 3 runs |
| Sidebar reveal out | spring 0.175 s / ζ 0.76, starting as the pointer leaves the hold zone | same | Arc 1.170.0 rec 120 fps, 3 runs |
| Space settle | critically damped, 0.19 s (ω ≈ 33), from the moment the fingers lift | Space pages after a swipe, a switch's slide, the pull's spring back | Arc 1.170 rec at 2×: 6 swipes, ω 30–33 in the 4 tracked best, and 3 pulls springing back, 0.18 s. Dia 1.50.1 bin has 0.25 |
| Space colour | the whole window at once: to the Space next door exponentially, τ ≈ 30 ms; a longer jump with the Space settle; a swipe with the pages | the window's colour as the Space changes | Arc 1.170 rec at 2× |

Clear is the Space's, so a tab switch in the Space moves its arrow too. The command runs on the release, as before, and a release off the button runs none | back, forward and reload (stop while loading) held; not the sidebar button or a dimmed one | ours, decided by the owner (2026-10-29): Arc's isn't recorded, so it takes Row press's distance and time, a press that reads the same across the sidebar (Quiet controls, loud thresholds). A recording of Arc's replaces it |
| Check draw | the check's stroke draws along its path, from its left end down to its point and up to its right end, round-ended, while the link leaves as in Glyph change. Ours: 250 ms, ease-in-out | Copy URL after a click | Arc, the owner's observation 2026-10-26; the timing isn't recorded |
| Page elsewhere: step back | the picture of a page another window took shows at the card's size and shrinks to 0.8 of it over 200 ms, ease-out cubic, its veil (black 0.12 at 0.8) fading in with it; with its window hidden it shows at 0.8 at once. It starts at once with the picture the window taking the page showed, if it showed one, else once its own comes. A fresh picture, taken as the page leaves, whenever the window comes to the front and when the page finishes a load or navigates, replaces the old where it is | the window a page left | ours: the page is seen going (Everything has a place) |
| Intro | the wordmark drawn as a hand writes "ilo", on the check draw's ease-in-out: the i's stem from its foot over 0.2–0.6 s, the l's over 0.32–0.8 s, then the ring from its top, anticlockwise, round-ended, over 0.48–1.1 s; the dot lands in the ink, growing about its middle, over 1.1–1.34 s, ease-out `cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.58, 1)`, and takes the Signal green over 1.34–1.64 s, ease-in-out; "a good tool." fades in over 1.64–2.04 s on the scroll edge's curve. It holds until 3.4 s, then the first step comes. Silent. Under Reduce Motion the whole wordmark, its dot green, and the line fade in together over 0.4 s and hold until 2 s | the intro of a new profile's first launch (Onboarding) | ours: Arc's published intro is a scored film of about 20 s (a recording of it runs 22 s), and Dia's is a few seconds that a reviewer found too quick to follow. Ours sits between, its last part readable by 2 s and the whole of it skippable (Delight on rare paths only; Readable at once) |
| Onboarding step | the step comes 32 pt from the right as the one before leaves 32 pt to the left, both over 200 ms, ease-out `cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.58, 1)`, fading with it; under Reduce Motion, the fade alone | going on from one step of the onboarding to the next | ours: the icon picker's tabs' 200 ms ease-out, the steps read as a row (Everything has a place) |
| Onboarding end | the flow fades from over the first Space over 200 ms on the scroll edge's curve, leaving the welcome where it is | leaving the onboarding's last step | ours |
| Page elsewhere: come back | none: the page is taken back at the press, as the row's click takes it, and covers the picture, at its last frame, stretched to the card until it draws at the card's size | a press on the picture | ours: Readable at once; a 120 ms grow before the move was a wait the user saw (#292) |

Arc plays a shimmer as its window first appears. It is not carried over (orchestrator's call, 2026-11-04, #669, for the owner's review): launch is a hot path, and the window shows its final frame at once, so nothing draws over the first frame to hide it. Revisit only with launch numbers from the performance gate showing a shimmer costs nothing.

Under Reduce Motion, Dia skips the New Tab intro and the PiP throw. Ardia also drops the sidebar slide and turns the panel grow into a fade. Ours: nothing moves or scales, and fades stay. Reduce Motion is the system's: the Mac's, Windows' "Show animations in Windows" turned off, and on Linux the desktop's animations turned off (GNOME) or their speed at 0 (KDE), read at launch and each time the app becomes active.

### Overlays

The command bar, the Space icon picker, menus, hover cards, Peek, find and every prompt over the page are child windows (decision 4). AGENTS.md › Over the page has the rules: a click outside closes the overlay and still does its own thing, and the host draws the overlay before showing it. Panels anchored to the URL field share one grid, except the command bar over the field, which keeps Arc's measures (Command bar › Over the URL field). **Ardia** set it on the owner's request; it is not measured from Dia:

- Content inset 16 on every side, corner radius 12. Rows 32 tall, with the hover fill inset 6 at radius 6.
- A 20 pt icon slot holds 13 pt glyphs, with text 10 pt after the slot, so titles start at 46. Separators sit 16 in, with 5 above and below.
- Header: the icon box is 16 from the top and left, with title and subtitle at 13/16 and 11/14 pt centred on it, and 8 pt below it.
- Placement: 8 from the card's side and 8 below the page's top. A panel taller than the page scrolls inside an 8 pt bottom margin.

### Toast

Arc's note at the window's top right, saying what was just done where the result can't be seen, such as a copy, even when the control used shows it too (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×, two Spaces deleted and ⇧⌘C; the URL field's Copy URL, Arc in use, the owner). It's a child window the mouse goes through.

- 46.5 tall, 8 inside the card's top right corner: 18 from the window's top and right edges. In a split view, 8 inside the focused pane's, as Arc shows a toast over its panel (Arc help), and it moves with the focus while it shows. Radius 10, with 0.5 pt of black 0.3 around it and a faint shadow below. Its fill goes down from the window's colour to darker (Arc's from (40,22,80) to (31,17,62) in a purple Space).
- White medium text, 12.5 in from its left and 13.5 from its right. A key's chip sits 8 from the words each side of it: 26 tall at radius 6, white 0.1, with the key semibold 9 in from each side.
- It comes and goes on Motion › Toast. After a Space is deleted it says "Space deleted! Use ⌘Z to undo." from about 0.1 s after the Space switch until 5 s after it. Ours: it goes with the ⌘Z it names, or the next edit, which ⌘Z would undo instead, as an Undo button's toast does, so it can't prompt a second ⌘Z (#1375). A new toast takes the words of one showing. Asked for while one is in place, it stays where it is, takes the new words and time, and bumps: 1.044 of its size in 40 ms, easing out, and back by 0.2 s, about the middle of its top edge, as it grows coming in, rather than come in again (Arc 1.171, measured by an outside reverse-engineering with a second ⌘↩ in the command bar while New Tab Created shows, *interim*, #1404). Ours: any toast asked for in place bumps, new words or the same; under Reduce Motion it doesn't.
- Ours: any other edit that can be undone and isn't plain to see offers its Undo the same way, "Removed from Favorites", "Tab Removed" (a pinned tab's trash), "Tab Moved to Space", "Folder Moved to Space", "Folder Deleted", "Tabs Closed" (Close Tabs Below), "Deleted from Archive" and "Archive Cleared", with an Undo ⌘Z button after the words for 5 s, or as long as the pointer is over it. A click on the button is ⌘Z. The offer goes when the next edit has no toast of its own, and when the edit is undone or redone some other way.
- A tab opening in the Space shown while the sidebar is hidden, and its reveal isn't out, is told of, as its row can't be seen (Arc's release notes). It says Arc's "New Tab Created", or "3 New Tabs Created" for several at once, for 4.8 s after the last one asked for, or as long as the pointer is over it, with a Show button, as Arc's is clickable and the Spaces' toast has one (ours, #1389): Show brings the sidebar back at the top one's row, as Reveal Tab in Sidebar does. A Space being filled, as at launch, opens nothing, and a tab moved from another Space isn't new (*interim*: its time is #1404's, from an outside reverse-engineering of Arc 1.171; Arc's plural: measure). A Space made with the sidebar hidden is told of too, as Arc's is (release notes), when it's made where it isn't seen, as by an import or an agent: ours says "Added the Space “Work”", or "Added 2 Spaces" for several made while the toast still tells of others, with a Show button for New Tab Created's time that shows the first with the sidebar back. New Space… brings the sidebar back for its form, so its own Space says nothing, and neither does one ⌘Z brings back, nor the window's first Spaces, as at launch. Only the browser window in front tells, of tabs or Spaces, as the toast shows over it (*interim*: Arc's words and time: measure).
- Ours (*interim*): 13.5 pt text, which makes Arc's 256.5 wide, as measured; the fill's 0.78 darkening at the bottom and the shadow (black 0.25, 4 down, blur 12) are estimates. It shows as soon as it's asked, rather than Arc's 0.1 s after the switch.

### Keyboard

- Focus rules are in AGENTS.md › Keyboard with the page focused. Chrome's reserved commands reach the browser before the page sees them: New Tab, New Window, New Incognito Window, Close Tab, Close Window, Reopen Closed Tab, Next/Previous Tab and Quit. Other browser keys reach the browser only if the page doesn't keep them. A page keeps ⌘F, for example. Pressed again within a second of one the page kept, the key runs the browser's command, as Arc's second press does; a key repeating as it's held isn't a press (the Mac tells; Linux and Windows don't yet, so a held key counts there). Per command, Settings › Shortcuts has Prefer Websites, the default, or Prefer ilo, which sends that command's keys to the browser before the page, as Arc's Prefer Arc does (Arc 1.170 Settings). Arc's Notify me when shortcuts are conflicting isn't built (owner call, 2026-11-01: the second press works without it, and a toast on every kept key would nag). Extensions can't take the reserved keys (Chrome).
- Off the Mac (Linux, Windows) every key is pressed with Ctrl for ⌘ and Alt for ⌃: ⌘T is Ctrl+T, ⌃2 is Alt+2, ⌃⇧= is Alt+Shift+=, ⌥⌘↓ is Ctrl+Alt+↓. Where ours and Chrome's differ, ours wins, as ⌘S does on the Mac, so someone using both learns one set: Alt+1–9 select Spaces (Chrome's Linux tabs; tabs stay on Ctrl+1–9), Ctrl+Shift+C copies the URL (Chrome's Inspect), Ctrl+S toggles the sidebar (Save Page), Ctrl+J jumps to the selection (Downloads) and Ctrl+Shift+J shows the downloads (Console). Chrome's actions there stay on their other keys, with none added: DevTools on F12 and Ctrl+Alt+I, inspecting from DevTools' picker, downloads on Ctrl+Shift+J and in the Library, and Save Page on Ctrl+Shift+S. ⌃Tab stays Ctrl+Tab, since Alt+Tab is the desktop's. Chrome's Alt keys stay Chrome's, and no ⌃ key takes them: Alt+← / Alt+→ go back and forward, Alt+D edits the URL and Alt+F4 closes the window, as in Chrome, so ⌃D, Developer Mode's, has no key there; Alt+Home, Alt+E and Alt+F do nothing yet. Menus, tooltips, the command bar's hints, Settings, toasts and notes show the keys this way, named in words as Chrome names them on Linux and Windows: Ctrl+Shift+T, Esc, Enter, Backspace, Del, Tab, Page Up, Left Arrow (the owner's call, 2026-10-29). The UI's font off the Mac has no keyboard symbols, and these are the names the platforms use; the Mac keeps its symbols.
- Where Arc and Dia bind different keys, Arc's key wins, because Arc refugees are the first users (decision 7). Chrome's key stays as a hidden alternate where it is free. *The owner should confirm this rule.*
- Every command's keys can be changed in Settings › Shortcuts (Dia, P§22): the user's keys replace the command's in this table, and a reserved command stays reserved on its new keys. A user's key beats the other commands' (the command that had it loses it) except a reserved command's and those that can't be rebound, which keep theirs, as do Hide, Minimize, the Edit menu and full screen. Quit (⌘Q, every Mac app's way out), Undo (⌘Z, which falls back to the page's own undo), the ⌃Tab walk (it lasts while ⌃ is held) and ⌘1–⌘8 can't be rebound. Each of ⌃1–⌃9's Spaces can, as Arc's Space shortcuts can, in its own row, Go to Space 1 to Go to Space 9, and keeps its keys across a relaunch. The keys are the user's own setting, with no agent path.

| Action | Arc (recorded) | Dia | Ours |
|---|---|---|---|
| New tab / window / incognito window | ⌘T / ⌘N / ⇧⌘N (menus); Blank Window ⌃⌘N | ⌘T / ⌘N / ⇧⌘N | ⌘T / ⌘N; ⇧⌘N comes with incognito |
| Command bar on the current URL | ⌘L (menus) | ⌘L | ⌘L |
| Close tab / window | ⌘W (Archive Tab) / ⇧⌘W (menus) | ⌘W / ⇧⌘W | ⌘W / ⇧⌘W. ⌘W in a window with no tab selected closes the window, as ⇧⌘W does. In the last window that closes it, and like the close button it quits at once, past the Quit warning |
| Reopen closed tab | ⇧⌘T (menus) | ⇧⌘T | ⇧⌘T |
| Clear Today tabs | ⇧⌘K | ⇧⌘K (Close All Tabs) | ⇧⌘K |
| Pin / unpin tab | ⌘D | — (⌘D bookmarks) | ⌘D, *owner to confirm* |
| Back to pinned URL | none: its Reset Tab to Original URL has no key (Settings › Shortcuts) | ⌘↩ | ⌘↩ |
| Toggle sidebar | ⌘S | ⌘S (Auto-Hide Tabs) | ⌘S |
| Library | ⇧⌘L (menus) | — | ⇧⌘L, which closes it too |
| Little Arc / open it in the main window | ⌥⌘N / ⌘O | — | Arc's, in Little ilo (⌘O does nothing in other windows, until Open File) |
| Switch Space 1–9 / next / previous | ⌃1–⌃9 / ⌥⌘→ / ⌥⌘← (menus) | ⌃1–⌃9 (profiles) | Arc's |
| Next / previous tab | ⌥⌘↓ / ⌥⌘↑ (menus), ⌘} / ⌘{ (Settings › Shortcuts) | ⇧⌘] / ⇧⌘[, ⌥⌘→ / ⌥⌘←, ⌥⌘↓ / ⌥⌘↑ | Arc's |
| Select tab 1–8 / last | ⌘1–⌘8 / ⌘9, pinned tabs first, counting those in an open folder (ax) | ⌘1–⌘8 / ⌘9 in sidebar order | Dia's |
| Tab used last, then further back | ⌃Tab (rec) | ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab (switcher) | ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab, no switcher (owner, 2026-10-29) |
| Search tabs | none: Arc has no Search Tabs (menus, bar, Settings › Shortcuts) | ⇧⌘A | ⇧⌘A, with Search Tabs |
| Split: add pane / close pane / focus next / previous | ⌃+ / ⌃⇧- (menus) / none; its ⌃⇧] / ⌃⇧[ switch to the next or previous split view, and ⌃⇧1–⌃⇧4 to one (Settings › Shortcuts) | ⌃⇧= / — / ⌃⇧] / ⌃⇧[, and ⌃⇧1–⌃⇧4 to the page at that place | Arc's; focus Dia's, *interim* |
| New note / in a split | ⌃⇧N / none (Settings › Shortcuts) | — | Arc's (Profiles › New Note) |
| Find / next / previous | ⌘F / ⌘G / ⇧⌘G (menus) | ⌘F / ⌘G / ⇧⌘G | ⌘F / ⌘G / ⇧⌘G |
| Use selection for find / jump to selection / find and replace | — / ⌘J / ⌥⌘F (menus) | — (⌘E is Chat) / ⌘J / ⌥⌘F | ⌘J; ⌘E and ⌥⌘F come with their features (⌘E writes the system's find pasteboard) |
| Copy URL / as Markdown / as Quote | ⇧⌘C / ⌥⇧⌘C / ⌃⇧⌘C (menus) | ⇧⌘C / ⌥⇧⌘C | ⇧⌘C / ⌥⇧⌘C; Quote needs the page's selection |
| Paste URL as new tab | ⌥⌘V (help) | — | ⌥⌘V |
| Downloads | ⇧⌘J (menus) | ⇧⌘J | ⇧⌘J |
| Save page / print | ⇧⌘S / ⌘P (menus) | ⇧⌘S / ⌘P | same |
| Capture a portion of the page | ⇧⌘2 (tooltip) | — | ⇧⌘2, which leaves capture mode too |
| DevTools / console / inspect / view source | ⌥⌘I / ⌥⌘J / ⌥⌘C / ⌥⌘U (menus) | ⌥⌘I or F12 / ⌥⌘J / ⌥⌘C / ⌥⌘U | same |
| Developer Mode | ⌃D (Settings › Shortcuts) | — | ⌃D, which a page or text field that uses it keeps (Developer Mode) |
| Back / forward | ⌘[ / ⌘] (menus) | ⌘[ / ⌘], ⌘← / ⌘→ | same |
| Reload / force reload / stop | ⌘R / ⇧⌘R / ⌘. (menus) | ⌘R / ⇧⌘R / — | ⌘R / ⇧⌘R / ⌘. and Esc |
| Zoom actual / in / out | ⌘0 / ⌘+ / ⌘- (menus) | ⌘0 / ⌘+ / ⌘- | same, plus hidden ⌘= |
| History / clear browsing data | ⌘Y / — (menus) | ⌘Y / ⇧⌘⌫ | ⌘Y, which opens Chrome's history page in a tab; ⇧⌘⌫ and the Archive menu's and command bar's Clear Browsing Data…, Chrome's Clear browsing data dialog in a tab for the Space's profile, not in a private window; a page may keep ⇧⌘⌫, as Chrome's isn't reserved |
| Full screen | ⌃⌘F (menus) | Globe-F | Globe-F, plus ⌃⌘F, which AppKit's Enter Full Screen in View binds |
| Settings | ⌘, | ⌘, | ⌘, |

Arc's other default keys (Arc 1.170.0, Settings › Shortcuts) come with their features: ⌘E Open Extensions, ⇧⌘O Little Arc's, ⇧⌘D Show/Hide Toolbar, ⌥⌘F Find and Replace, ⇧⌘V Paste and Match Style, ⌃⇧⌘C Copy URL as Quote, ⇧⌘2 Capture…, ⌃⌘N Blank Window, ⌃⇧E New Easel. Its ⌃Space Reveal Current Tab has no key here: macOS takes ⌃Space by default to switch input sources, which anyone typing Japanese or another language through an input method relies on, so the key wouldn't reach the browser. Reveal Tab in Sidebar is that one command, with no key until Settings › Shortcuts gives it one. Its Help › Essential Keyboard Shortcuts is a panel of five (⌘T, ⌃Tab, ⇧⌘C, ⌃⇧=, ⌥⌘N) whose View All opens Settings › Shortcuts.

Mouse (Dia and Chrome, P§ Keyboard shortcuts):

- ⌘-click opens a background tab. ⇧⌘-click opens a foreground tab. ⇧-click opens the link in Peek (the owner, 2026-11-02, #1370), or with Peek off in a foreground tab, as the core opens every new window a page asks for (decision 3), where Chrome's opens a window. Middle-click opens a background tab.
- ⌥-click opens a link in a split pane, as Arc's does. The owner's call (2026-10-30), over Chrome's download, which the link menu's Save Link As… keeps. ⌥⌘-click opens it in Little ilo (the owner's call, 2026-10-29, over Chrome's split), and in a private window in a pane, as its links stay private (Little Arc). Dia's ⌥⇧-click split isn't ours: ⌥⇧-click does what ⇧-click does.
- ⌘-click or middle-click on back, forward or reload opens the result in a background tab under the tab (Arc), at the page back or forward goes to, or the tab's own page for reload. It loads once it's shown, as ilo's background tabs do, where Chrome's loads at once.
- Double-click a tab to rename it.

### Menus

Arc 1.170.0's menu bar, with the items the browser has so far. An item runs the same command as its key and shows that key. Each is in the command bar by its name too. Their agent paths are the core's own calls: `bc_tab_open` for View History, `bc_window_show_space` for the Space list, `bc_space_set_name` for Rename Space and `bc_space_set_profile` for Change Profile. Reveal Tab in Sidebar moves only the user's view, so agents get none; they open folders with `bc_folder_set_open`. Search Tabs switches the user's view too: an agent lists its own Space's tabs, `bc_space_pinned` and `bc_space_today`, and names the one it acts on in each call, as the prototype's `list_tabs` and `tab` argument do. Titles that follow the window's state, such as Hide Sidebar and Pin Tab, change once that state does, and the menu bar is made again only then, never on a switch between two Today tabs. Essential Keyboard Shortcuts, About ilo and Import from Other Browsers… only show our Settings window, which an agent has no use for, and agents get no import: it reads the user's other browsers.

- **Order.** App, File, Edit, View, Spaces, Tabs, Archive, Extensions, Window and Help, as Arc's.
- **File.** New Tab…, New Window (⌘N), New Little ilo Window (⌥⌘N), New Incognito Window (⇧⌘N), Blank Window (⌃⌘N), Restore Last Closed Tab, Open Command Bar, Close Window (⇧⌘W), Archive Tab (⌘W), then Share…, Capture… (⇧⌘2), Capture Full Page and Capture in Portrait Mode (Capture), Save Page As (⇧⌘S) and Print (⌘P), in Arc's places. Save Page As shows Chrome's save panel and Print its print preview, over the page. Share…, also a command in the command bar, opens the system's share sheet for the page shown, as Site Controls' Share tile does, hanging from the URL field, or from the page's top while the sidebar is hidden (ours); it isn't offered where the system has no sheet, as on Linux, or for a page with no link. No agent path: the share services are the user's. Arc's Blank Window (⌃⌘N) goes after New Window. New Profile follows Open Command Bar, as Change Profile's is, dimmed unless the Space shown is empty, and not in a private window. Arc's New Easel goes after it with its feature.
- **Archive.** Go Back, Go Forward, View History (⌘Y), View Archive… and Clear Archive, as Arc's. View History opens Chrome's history page in a new tab, and does nothing on that page, as Chrome's ⌘Y shows the one it has (*interim* until a history page of ours).
- **Edit.** Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All go to the first responder, so a focused page or text field does them itself. Blink on Mac leaves those keys to the menu, so without them a page can't undo, copy or paste. Copy URL and Copy URL as Markdown sit between Copy and Paste, as in Arc. Paste and Match Style (⇧⌘V) follows Paste, to the first responder as Paste is, so a page pastes as plain text in its own style; a field of ours has no style and pastes as Paste does. Paste URL as New Tab (⌥⌘V) follows it, where the menu shows its key (owner, 2026-10-29). Find is a submenu: Find…, Find Next, Find Previous and Jump to Selection. AutoFill follows it, a submenu of Contact…, Passwords… and Credit Card… (Passwords and autofill › Filling), in the system's words and place, in place of the system's own, which AppKit would add and which fills a field from Contacts and the Passwords app, not from the profile. Like Undo or Paste, they act on the field with the keyboard, so they aren't in the command bar, which takes it.
- **App.** About ilo, which opens Settings on its About section, then Check for Updates… in a build that updates itself (Updates), then Settings…, Import from Other Browsers…, which opens Settings on Import & Export, and Privacy Policy, which opens ilo's site's /privacy in a tab (`app/branding/site`, a default service URL, principle 2) and is in the command bar too, then the system's Services, Hide (⌘H), Hide Others (⌥⌘H), Show All and Quit (⌘Q). Arc's About Arc is the system's panel and its import item is Import from Another Browser…; ours are in ilo's words, and About is a Settings section so it's one view on every OS.
- **Spaces.** New Space…, Rename Space, Change Profile, Next Space and Previous Space, then each Space by its name, after its emoji if it has one (a glyph can't show in a native item), the first nine with ⌃1 to ⌃9, as Arc's "Space 1" (its capture had one Space, so the names are an inference). The list is made again when a Space is made, renamed, deleted or moved, never on a switch. Change Profile is a submenu that does what the Space menu's Set Profile does: the profiles, the Space's checked and the others dimmed while it has tabs, then New Profile, dimmed then too. A private window has none. Edit Theme… follows New Space…, as Arc's, and opens the theme picker as the command bar's does. Arc's Manage Spaces… goes after the list, dimmed with one Space, with its feature.
- **Tabs.** New Tab…, Search Tabs… (⇧⌘A, ours: Arc has none), Pin Tab (⌘D), Unpin Tab for a pinned tab and Pin/Unpin Tab with none shown, as Arc's, New Folder…, Next Tab, Previous Tab, Reveal Tab in Sidebar, Focus Sidebar (ours, Pinned tabs), then Clear Today (⇧⌘K) and Reset all tabs in this Space, as Arc's. Reveal Tab in Sidebar shows a hidden sidebar docked, opens the closed folders the tab shown is in and scrolls the list to its row. Reset all tabs in this Space, which has no key, as in Arc's Settings › Shortcuts, and isn't in the command bar, as Arc's "space" lists only the Space commands (Command bar › Commands), sends each pinned tab of the Space shown that's away from its pinned URL back, as Back to Pinned URL does, in its folders too. An unloaded one keeps its page until it's shown (ours), as loading it unseen would cost what showing it does (UX principles › Pay when it's looked at). Its agent path is Back to Pinned URL's core call, on each tab.
- **View.** Appearance (Automatic, Light, Dark) first, as Arc's (Themes › Appearance), then Hide Sidebar, or Show Sidebar while it's hidden, as Arc's (the command bar and Settings › Shortcuts keep Show/Hide Sidebar, as Arc's Settings does), and Collapse Pinned Tabs, or Expand Pinned Tabs while they're collapsed (Pinned tabs), then Stop Loading, Refresh the Page, Force Refresh the Page, Clear Cookies and Refresh and Clear Cache and Refresh, then the split view items, zoom, Cast…, which opens the Cast card (Sidebar › Cast; ours after zoom, as Arc's place for it isn't recorded), and Developer, as Arc's. Arc's View also has Show Toolbar (⇧⌘D) after the sidebar's item, Expand Current Split (ours, after Developer, enabled in a split view), and Enter Full Screen (⌃⌘F) last; its Developer adds Network Inspector and Allow JavaScript from Apple Events, and ends, with a page, with Turn on Developer Mode for this site (⌃D), as ours does (Developer Mode). Its two Clear items show only with a page. The two Clear items, also in the command bar, delete the profile's cookies or cache for the page's site, its registrable domain or IP address, then load the page again, the cache's bypassing the cache. Pages that aren't http(s) have no site, so the command bar leaves the items out and they do nothing (*interim*: what Arc clears beyond the site isn't measured).
- **Window.** Minimize Window (⌘M) and Stay On Top (Window › Stay On Top), then Open Library… (⇧⌘L), View Downloads… (⇧⌘J), View Media… and View Boosts…, as Arc's, whose View Easels… goes with them. View Boosts… is there only where the window lists Boosts: not in a private window or an agent's Space, nor with Settings' switch off (Boosts › Seeing and turning off). AppKit adds its window items (Fill, Center, Move & Resize, Full Screen Tile) above them, Enter Full Screen to View, and the list of windows below. Each window is named there, and in Mission Control, by its shown tab's title as the sidebar shows it, or by its Space's name with none shown, as Arc's; a private window with none shown is Incognito. The name changes when the tab shown or its title does, never on a timer. The menu bar is the key window's: its items act on that window, and Extensions lists its Space's profile's.
- **Help.** Welcome to ilo (ours), which plays a new profile's first launch again (Onboarding), then Help Center and What's New, Arc's Visit Help Center and What's New, which open ilo's site's help and release notes in a tab (`app/branding/site`, a default service URL), then Essential Keyboard Shortcuts, which opens Settings on its Shortcuts section. After a separator, Report a Problem…, ilo's for Arc's Contact the Team: a new issue on the repository's bug report form in a tab, its version, Chromium and system fields filled with the build's details, and nothing sent until the user submits it (`app/branding/issues`). Then Restore Earlier Sidebar, then Troubleshooting › Copy Build Info, as Arc's later Copy Arc Info, which copies the same details (the version and build, Chromium's, the system's and the chip) with a toast, and Open Task Manager, as Arc's, where Chrome has Window › Task Manager, and Record a Trace (DevTools › Record a Trace). All but Essential Keyboard Shortcuts' Help items are in the command bar too, Restore Earlier Sidebar as Restore Earlier Sidebar…, which lists the backups in ilo's menu where the bar was, newest first, offered once there's one: Linux and Windows show no menu bar, so it's their way in. AppKit adds its search field. Arc's other Help items are its services' (Export Arc Notes) or put its data in Finder (Reveal Arc Data), and stay out.
  - **Restore Earlier Sidebar** (Arc's Restore Data, decided by the owner on 2026-10-29): the core backs up each profile's sidebar, its Spaces with their folders and tabs and its favorites, in the profile's directory, as it saves the Spaces anyway, so no timer wakes an idle browser for it. A backup is taken when the newest is an hour old (ours: Arc's spacing isn't known). Arc's schedule keeps 10 from today, then one a day for 10 days, one a week to a month back and one a month to a year back. The submenu lists the shown Space's profile's backups, newest first, each by its weekday, date and time, and is dimmed until there's one. A backup chosen asks first, then its Spaces take the place of the profile's, and the window shows the Space it showed if the backup has it. Agents' Spaces and other profiles' stay, as do favorites shared by every profile. The sidebar it replaces becomes the newest backup, so choosing that one undoes it. Not in a private window, and agents get no tool for it. The backups stay on the computer; with sync on, the restored Spaces reach the account's other devices as any change to the Spaces does.
- **Page menu.** Arc's (Arc 1.170.0 ax) is Chrome's with no icons or key hints. A link's: Open Link in New Tab, in New Window, in Split View, in Little Arc, in Incognito Window, then Save Link As… and Copy Link Address. An image's adds Open Image in New Tab, Save Image As…, Capture Image…, Copy Image, Copy Image Address and Search Google for Image. The page's: Back, Forward, Reload, then Save As…, Print…, Cast… and Translate, then View Page Source and Inspect. Selected text: Look Up, Search Google, Copy, Copy Link to Highlight, Print…, then Inspect, Speech and Services. A field: the system's edit items, Writing Direction and AutoFill. It has none of Chrome's Lens, Reading Mode, Listen, QR Code, Send to Your Devices or Copy Link Text.
  - Ours: Chrome's context menu cut to Arc's set, with Chrome's items working as in Chrome: a link opens in a tab under the page, Save As and Save Link As show the save panel, Print shows print preview and Inspect docks DevTools at the element. A link's items are Arc's, in its order, with ilo's Open Link in Peek after Open Link in New Tab for a web link (the owner, 2026-11-02, #1370), left out while Peek is off or in an agent's Space (Peek › Which clicks open it). Open Link in New Window opens the link in a window as ⌘N's, on the Space the page is in, Open Link in Incognito Window in a private window as ⇧⌘N's, and a private window's link opens in a private window either way, as Chrome's does. Open Link in Split View opens it in a pane on the right, as ⌥-click does. Lens, Reading Mode, Listen, QR Code, Send to Your Devices, Copy Link Text, Chrome's reading list and Gemini's items are left out, as Arc leaves them out. Chrome's image search stays for an engine that has one, under Chrome's name, Search Image with <engine>. Open Link in Little Arc and Capture Image… wait for their features. Translate is ilo's own row where Arc's is, after Cast…, as Chrome's item would run Chrome's translate, which needs Google's servers: Translate to English for a page offered, Use Original once it's translated or on its way, each as the URL field's card runs it (Sidebar › Translate), and none for a link's, an image's, a selection's or a field's menu. Cast… opens the Cast card, as a video's does. A video's keeps Chrome's Picture in Picture, which floats it, as Arc's second right-click on a video shows Chrome's. Agents get no path: the menu is the user's.
- **Tab dialogs**, such as print preview, Chrome's, as in Chrome's window: centred over the page, with their top at the page's, and gone while another tab shows.
- **Names.** Arc's, where they're plain words: Restore Last Closed Tab, Archive Tab (⌘W), Refresh the Page, Force Refresh the Page, Go Back, Go Forward, Zoom to Actual Size, Zoom In, Zoom Out, and View › Developer's View Source, Developer Tools, Inspect Elements and JavaScript Console.
- **Case.** Title case on the Mac, as Arc's. Off the Mac, ilo's own menus are in sentence case, as Chromium's menus and the Windows and GNOME guidelines are there, so one window shows one style: Add split view, Archive tabs below, Copy link (ours). ilo's nouns Space and Library keep their capital, as Chrome's Incognito does: New Space…, Move to › Space 1. Names users give, such as Space names and tab titles, never change, and Chromium's page menu keeps Chromium's case on each OS. The case is picked in one place, `MenuEntry::new` (shell/menus.rs).

## Phase 3 features

### Window

| Item | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Card inset, sidebar docked or hidden | 10 on every side. Docked, the card starts at the sidebar's width (228), so the 10 on its left is inside the sidebar | Arc 1.170.0 ax and cap |
| Card inset (reference) | left = sidebar width, top 6, right 7, bottom 7. With the sidebar hidden, 6 / 6 / 6 | Dia 1.49 cap |
| Card corner radius | 6, circular; the page in the card is clipped to it. Dia's is 10, continuous | Arc cap, Dia cap |
| Traffic lights | Arc: 16 × 16, centres (20, 24), (43, 24), (66, 24), a 23 pt pitch. Dia: centres x 24.75 / 47.75 / 70.75, y 26.75 | Arc 1.170.0 ax, Dia 1.50.1 bin |
| A page before its first paint | black while websites are told the appearance is dark, white while it's light (View › Appearance's, else the system's), whatever the Space's theme: a new tab, a page never shown and a tab restored unloaded alike. Switching between loaded tabs shows no gap: the next page's frame follows straight after (*interim*, #1404). Ours: a page that fails to load draws its words on the same colour (Pages that break) | Arc 1.171, measured by an outside reverse-engineering |

- **Dragging the window.** The empty sidebar below the rows, the header row outside its buttons, and the margins around the card all move the window (the left margin only while the sidebar is hidden). A double-click there does the system's title-bar action from System Settings › Desktop & Dock. The exception is a double-click on the empty sidebar, which opens a new tab (Dia 1.50; Arc: confirm). Rows, tiles, buttons and the URL field keep their own presses. The outer ~3 pt is the resize edge (Dia and Arc, P§1). Arc's Settings › Advanced "Allow window dragging from the top of webpages" is not carried over (orchestrator's call, 2026-11-04, #669, for the owner's review): web apps put their own tabs, menus and toolbars there (Docs, Figma, Gmail), and a strip that takes their presses breaks them, so the page is the page's to the top of its card.
- **Card, no page** (*Ours*: no recording of Arc's empty card, so this follows the principles, [ux-principles.md](ux-principles.md)). A window that shows no tab, which is a fresh ⌘N window, a Space with no tab shown and what's left after the last Today tab closes, keeps the card's fill and puts ilo's mark over one line in its middle, the pair centred as one. The mark is the app icon's lowercase i (`app/branding/ilo.icon`), drawn from its 1024 pt canvas at a sixteenth, so 39 tall: an 11.5 dot 5.1 over an 8.3 × 22.4 stem with round ends. The dot is the icon's green, #0E9F55, the one colour on the card and the only place the brand shows in the window; the stem is white 0.14, a step over a tile's 0.10, so it reads as pressed into the card rather than printed on it. It keeps those colours on every Space's tint and in a private window, as the icon does in the Dock. No wordmark, title, second line or art: the mark says whose window this is, once, at the weight of the card's chrome, and the line says the one thing to do next. The line is "New Tab" and its key, ⌘T, in a chip, 24 under the mark's foot, so the two read as a sign and its caption, not one block (Teach the keys where they're used). The words are 14 pt, as the sidebar's New Tab row, in secondary text (white 0.6); the chip is the command bar's key chip (24 tall, 7 in, radius 6.5, white 0.08, 13 pt) 8 after the words, in the same colour, since the line is one quiet unit. It shows the key the user gave New Tab in Settings › Shortcuts, and the words alone if there's none. Light mode: the stem, the name and the line take the window's ink at the same alphas, as the icon's light tile draws its stem in ink.
  - A click anywhere on the card opens the command bar as ⌘T does, centred on the window, on release so that a press that drags the window by it isn't a click (Forgive rather than forbid: the card says where to start, and any click on it starts). A double-click there does nothing more, where on the margins it does the title bar's action. The mark and the line take no hover, since the whole card is the button. With the bar open, a click on the card only closes it, as one outside the bar does anywhere else; the sidebar's New Tab row and URL field do the same, since the press closes the bar before the click can tell it was open.
  - While the command bar the window opened is up over it, ⌘N's and ⌘T's and ⌘L's with no page, the card draws nothing: its one offer, New Tab, is the bar, and the two would read as one thing stacked on another. Once the bar closes with no page shown, the mark and the line fade in again as below (ours, #669).
  - The mark and the line fade in together over 100 ms from nothing, on the scroll edge's curve (Motion), from the moment the card shows no page, with the URL field's words (Sidebar › Header and URL field), and go in the frame a page is selected, without fading. A window that shows a page a frame later never shows them, and a page's first frame doesn't wait on them. Under Reduce Motion the fade stays (Motion).
  - Nothing native lies over it: the core lays out no page view with no tab shown (`Window::ShowNothing`), so GPUI draws it and takes its clicks. It costs nothing while a page is shown: it's drawn only with no tab selected. The state of a window whose tab is selected but whose page another window shows (Several windows) is not this card, and stays blank until the frozen frame.
- **Pages that break** (*Ours*: Arc and Dia weren't recorded with a page that broke, so this follows the principles and the empty card). Each says what happened in one line on the card's colour, or a page's before it paints (Window), with one or two buttons, and nothing to read past it.
  - *Died.* A page whose renderer crashed, was killed, ran out of memory or never started leaves its pane to the card: "This page stopped" in 14 pt medium white, the empty card's line size, centred with Reload 12 under it, a button of the page question's plate (28 tall, white 0.1, with the ladder's 0.20 under the pointer and 0.16 pressed, 13 pt medium, 14 in from each side). The pair fades in as the empty card's line does. Reload, ⌘R and ⇧⌘R load the page again, as ⇧⌘R does Arc's Sad Tab. In a split view only the dead page's pane shows it. A tab behind whose renderer dies shows nothing: it's unloaded, as Chrome unloads a tab for memory, and loads once it's shown, so the card is only ever seen on a page the user was looking at (Chrome keeps its sad tab instead). A renderer ended on purpose, normal termination, as when the core closes a page or moves a tab to another engine, never shows it.
  - *Hung.* A page that hasn't answered input for Chrome's hang timeout, 15 s, gets the toast with "Not responding" and two buttons, Wait and Reload, in place of Chrome's Page Unresponsive dialog. It stays until a button is pressed, the page answers or another tab shows. Wait gives the page Chrome's timeout again; Reload ends its renderer first, as Chrome's Exit page does, since a reload would wait on it.
  - The row of a page that died or hung shows Lucide's circle-alert in the speaker's place, 16 in 18, in secondary text, and the speaker goes while it shows. Split segments and favorites show none (*interim*).
  - *Load failed.* The core draws its own error page from Chromium's strings, in place of the neterror page with its game: Chromium's heading, Reload 12 under it in the card's look, and Chromium's error code (ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED and so on) 12 under that at 11 pt in white 0.32, on the colour a page shows before it paints (Window): black, or white. In Light, as websites are told it (View › Appearance), its words, plates and the game's rows take the palette's ink at the same alphas, as the pane is light then, the way the empty card's line does (#1567). Offline it reads "You're offline" over "Reloads when you're back" in 13 pt secondary text, with no button but the game's line (Offline game), and Chromium's own auto-reload loads it once the network is back, and retries a refused or failed connection while the tab is shown, with no timer of ours. Certificate and Safe Browsing interstitials stay Chromium's (*interim*). The row shows no mark: the page says it.
  - Agents read the same state from the core (`bc_tab_state.trouble`, with the net error's code), and may reload or wait through the same calls.
- **Offline game** (*Ours*: Arc has none and Chrome's game is Chrome's, so this is ilo's own, after Delight on rare paths only and the sidebar's rows). The offline error, ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED or Chromium's DNS probe finding no internet, offers it, and so does `chrome://dino`, which is that error. Other errors don't: they have something to do instead.
  - *The offer.* "Press Space to play" 12 under the note, in the error code's style, 11 pt white 0.32, with the code 12 under it. It never starts by itself. Space or ↑ starts a run and hops; a click or a tap hops during a run but doesn't start one, since the line says Space and a click on the page may only be focusing it. The page keeps Space and ↑ only without modifiers, so ⌘L, ⌘T and ⌘W stay the browser's.
  - *The field.* The pane itself, with no frame or floor. The player is a 12 pt dot in the icon's green, #0E9F55, at 28 % of the pane's width, 64 to 200 in. Columns of the sidebar's tab rows, each 64 wide, 36 tall on a 41 pitch at radius 12, in a row's hover fill, white 0.12, come in from the right 210 apart. Each misses three rows, a 128 pt gap. The band holds as many rows as fit in the pane's height less 96, 5 to 11, centred. A gap never takes the band's first or last row and lies at most three rows from the one before; the first is in the middle, where the dot starts. The row hit takes the selected row's fill, white 0.20. A resize mid-run fits the band again and keeps the run.
  - *Motion.* Gravity 1300 pt/s². A hop sets 360 pt/s upward, a 50 pt rise over 0.28 s, and the dot falls at most 640 pt/s. The band's top and bottom hold the dot: only a row ends a run. The columns start at 150 pt/s and ease toward 300 as 150 + 150 (1 − e^(−t / 45 s)), so 180 at 10 s and 220 at 30 s, and the first reaches the dot 2.4 s in. It steps at a fixed 1/120 s, so it plays alike at 60 and 120 Hz, and moves at most 0.1 s a frame.
  - *Score.* A column passed counts one. The corner, 14 from the top and 16 from the trailing edge, shows the score in 11 pt tabular figures, white 0.6, then "Best" and the best score in white 0.32, 12 apart. The best lasts as long as the page.
  - *The end.* A hit stops the field with the row lit for 600 ms, then the field fades out and the words in over 200 ms, the line now "Press Space to play again". Space within 0.4 s of a hit starts nothing, so keys mashed as a run ends don't start the next. The page losing the keys ends a run: the command bar or another overlay, another window or app, or another tab shown. It listens for blur, not visibilitychange or pagehide, which would keep the page out of Chromium's fast shutdown at a quit.
  - *Back online.* While a run is going, Chromium's auto-reload waits (the core holds it, `Tab::GameRunning`), the corner says "Back online" in the green once the page hears the network come back, and the page loads as soon as the run ends. With no run going it reloads as before.
  - *Cost.* It draws with `requestAnimationFrame` only during a run and sets no timer, so an idle offline page runs nothing and a hidden one draws nothing. No sound. Agents get no tool for it and read the offline error as before.
- **New window.** It appears at its final frame, already showing its content, with no empty frames. A closed window leaves the screen at once (Dia, P§1). ⌘N's window, and ⇧⌘N's private one, opens with ⌘T's command bar, centred, so ⌘N and typing go straight to a page (Arc's release notes). The bar opens once the window's first frame is on screen, so the window comes no later for it; Esc closes it to the window's Space with no page, the card's mark and line. From a window full screen of its own, not for a page, the new one goes full screen too, and its bar opens once it's there and macOS shows its desktop (Arc); AppKit takes it there itself. Ours (*interim* until Arc's ⌘N is recorded): it's as big as the key window, down and to the right of it as AppKit cascades windows, or of the regular window in front when the key one is private, whose frame never sets a regular one's, and shows its Space with the same sidebar width.
- **Several windows.** Every window shows the same Spaces, as in Arc, and a tab's page is live in one window at a time: the one that showed it last. A window's own picks, on a Space switch or a tab closing, pass over pages another window shows. A click on a row whose page another window shows moves the page here, live, and the window it left keeps the row selected and draws a picture of the page in its card (ours; Dia's frozen frame is washed, ours is dimmed and set back, so it reads as not live): the page's own shape, fitted to 0.8 of the card and centred, over the card's empty fill, with the card's corner radius, a 0.5 pt rim of white 0.10 and a shadow of black 0.24, radius 16, 4 down (the popovers' rim and shadow), under a veil of black 0.12 that goes in one frame under the pointer, which becomes the pointing hand. It has no caption. A click anywhere on the picture, or on the row, takes the page back, live (Motion › Page elsewhere: come back). Once the window showing the page lets go of it, as it shows another tab or Space or closes, the page goes back, live, to the window with its picture, to the one the user was in last if several have one, without taking the keyboard; focusing a window moves no page (ours). The page stays drawn while it moves, so the window taking it shows its last frame at once, stretched to its card until it draws at the new size. The window the page left shows at once the picture the window taking it showed, if it showed one, and asks the core for a fresh one, after the move and off its path: raw pixels, copied off the UI thread, with no PNG; with no picture to start from it shows the card empty until it comes (Motion › Page elsewhere: step back); it asks again whenever that window comes to the front while it shows one, and when the page finishes a load or goes to another URL with no load under way, as a same-page navigation does, from the core's reports and never on a timer, so it does nothing while idle. A page in Safari's engine is pictured the same way, from WebKit's snapshot of its view, in which protected video is black. It keeps one picture, let go when the page comes back, the window selects another tab or the tab closes. Agents get no picture. Why: the page seen stepping back is told where it went, a picture that is dimmed and clicks through to the page says what a click does without words, and a capture only when it's looked at or the page has changed costs nothing the rest of the time, while a picture of a page no window shows would be a dead end. It isn't a live mirror: on Windows a DWM thumbnail of the page cost about ten times the window server's CPU while the page drew, more with each window, and on the Mac a second layer of the page's compositor was within noise on CPU but not measured on the GPU (#359). A page in Safari's engine gets WebKit's picture, not the empty card: with its video black it still says where the page went and that a click brings it, where an empty card reads as a page that broke. Closing a window gives its tabs to the window in front before it, live. Closing the last quits. A quit keeps every window but the private ones, and a launch brings them back stacked as they were, the front one key (*interim* until Arc's launch is recorded): each at its frame, on its display or moved onto the main screen if that display is gone, with the Space it showed, the tab it showed there and its sidebar's width. The first window opens as a lone window does, and the others behind it after its first frame, so launch waits for none of them. Only the front window comes back full screen; one behind comes back at its frame from before full screen, so a launch never takes the user to another macOS Space. A window closed before the quit doesn't come back. Closing the last regular window keeps it as a quit does, so the next launch, from the Dock or a link, opens at its frame. With Settings › General's Reopen your windows and tabs off (Arc's Restore windows from last session), a launch opens one window, at the front one's frame and out of full screen, showing the Space it showed with no page, as ⌘N's window does; the Spaces, their folders and pinned tabs and the favorites come back, and the Today tabs go in the archive, so nothing is lost. Without a picture of a sidebar that won't come back, the launch window shows none.
- **What a relaunch and an unload keep** (ours, as Chrome keeps them when it continues where it left off, #1934). A quit, an update's relaunch and a crash alike keep each profile's cookies, the session cookies with no expiry that a sign-in often lives in too, so a site signed in before the quit is still signed in after it; before, only a crash kept those. A tab the launch puts back, and one that let go of its page (unseen 2 hours, the oldest when memory runs short, or a pinned tab or favorite closed with ⌘W), gets its own sessionStorage back with its next page, so a site that keeps its sign-in there, as Microsoft's sign-in does, doesn't ask again. A tab closed for good, or one whose Space or window goes for good, takes its sessionStorage with it, and once a launch has put the tabs back it deletes what no tab came back for, the Today tabs' that go in the archive with Reopen your windows and tabs off included. Each profile's stay its own, an agent's Space's tabs have only their own sessionStorage, never one of the user's tabs', a tab moved to another profile or copied while it has no page starts with none, and a private window keeps nothing past its close. Clear browsing data clears both, the unloaded tabs' sessionStorage included. Agents have no path to either.
- **Close confirmations** (Dia, P§1). Closing a window with 2 or more tabs, or pressing ⌘W on the last tab, asks "Close 3 tabs?" with a breakdown per Space and "Don't ask me again". Arc's equivalent: measure. Ours: none (*interim* until Arc's is recorded). Spaces are shared, so closing a window closes no tab and loses nothing, and ⌘W archives a tab, which ⇧⌘T brings back (Forgive rather than forbid).
- **Warn before quitting** (Arc 1.170.0 bin and cap; Arc 1.170 rec at 2×). The Quit menu item and ⌘Q ask "Quit Arc?" in the dialog (Spaces › Renaming and deleting), unless Settings › General › Ask before quitting is off. Its card is 451 × 249 with the app's 64 pt icon over the question and no detail, the buttons 46 under the question's line: "Quit, and don't ask again" at the left, text only in Cancel's look, its label 13 in from each side, then Cancel with its ESC chip and Quit with its ↩ chip at the right, Quit in blue, #3338F2 on a ledge of #393F92. ↩ quits, Esc cancels, and the left button turns the setting off and quits. A quit from the Dock or at logout doesn't ask. Ours: "Quit ilo?", on by default as in Arc's capture, with no icon, so the card is 172 tall; Arc's shows the app icon the user picked, which isn't carried over while ilo has no alternate icons (orchestrator's call, 2026-11-04, #669, for the owner's review), so the dialog stays without one; Quit darkens under the pointer, to #2E32DA, as the red action does. With no window open it quits without asking. With downloads running, Chrome's alert asks after Quit, as Arc's quit asks again for them.
- **Page full screen** (Dia, P§18). The page fills the window and the sidebar goes away. Esc or the page's `exitFullscreen` brings the layout back. Switching or closing the tab ends full screen. The window enters macOS full screen for the page and leaves it only if it entered for that page. No frame of the transition may show the other layout.
- **Window full screen** (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×, four entries). The sidebar stays docked and the card keeps its 10 pt inset; Arc's window changes to its full-screen layout in one frame. Once macOS's transition is over, a note in the toast's look and motion says "To exit full screen, press ⎋.", off the Mac Chrome's "Press Esc to exit full screen": 41 tall, 4 from the window's top, its right edge on the card's, in the Space's colour, for 4.8 s. ⎋ goes to the page first, and one the page doesn't use leaves full screen when there's no load to stop; two within 500 ms, or one held 600 ms, leave even when the page keeps it, so a page that uses ⎋, a game or an editor, never keeps the user there (Arc's release notes; the times ours). Off the Mac a held key's repeats come as presses, so a held ⎋ leaves there as one pressed twice. Arc's Settings › Shortcuts can give the browser ⎋ first; ours has no such switch yet. Ours says the note again for each Space shown in full screen (*interim*: the recording's one Space switch was windowed, so it isn't seen in Arc's). Leaving full screen takes it away (*interim*: Arc's stayed). A page's full screen doesn't show it. It holds up as Arc's release notes say Arc's does: once the window leaves full screen its page draws again at its new size, never blank or a frame from before, and a display plugged in or taken out while it's full screen leaves it full screen at its size, its page drawn (#669).
- **Stay On Top** (Arc's Window menu). Window › Stay On Top keeps the window over other apps' windows and the app's own, and its overlays still over it: the command bar, menus, Peek, the toast. The menu ticks it while it's on, and the command bar offers Stay On Top, or Don't Stay On Top while it's on, the way to see it and the way out. Off the Mac, with no menu bar, the command bar's row is both. Ours (*interim*, Arc's not measured): it's the window's own, not kept across a quit; full screen sets it aside, and it's on top again after; Wayland gives an app no way to stay on top, so there it isn't offered. Agents get none.
- **Blank Window** (Arc's File › Blank Window, ⌃⌘N, and the command bar's New Blank Window). A window for tabs the user doesn't want kept or shown in other windows. It shows one Space of its own, in the profile of the Space shown, which no other window shows and nothing saves or lists, holding Today tabs alone; the tabs its pages open stay in it. Its sidebar is a private window's: no favorites, pinned tabs, folders, Space switcher, ••• or Library. Its name row is a Space's, so it lines up: the square glyph in the icon's column, in the name's ink and no colour, and "Blank Window" on the titles' column, the way to see what it is. It keeps the look and the extensions of any window of its profile. A tab's menu offers Move to › the user's Spaces of its profile, the way out: the tab goes to that Space's Today, live, in the regular window showing it, else the one in front, and the Blank Window stays. Closing the window puts its tabs in the archive, as closed Today tabs go (ours, Forgive rather than forbid: Arc's go unsaved), though not among those ⇧⌘T reopens, and a launch brings none of it back. ⌃⌘N is the Mac's; off the Mac Ctrl+Alt+N is ⌥⌘N's Little ilo Window, so there it has no key and the command bar opens it (ours). Agents never reach it: bc_windows leaves it out, and its tabs are in no agent's Space (#669).
- **Other window commands** (Dia, P§1). Merge All Windows, Move Tab to Window. Moving keeps the live page: no reload, history kept.

### Sidebar

Arc's layout: the URL field and back, forward and reload live in the sidebar, and the card has no toolbar (decision 6). Dia's card toolbar, its top tab strip and the toolbar that hides on scroll are not ours.

A click opens the bar over the field with the same words in the same place, so the field reads as that bar, folded. 0.32 is to the host's 0.60 as the bar's placeholder is to its text, and is Clear's resting white, the sidebar's quietest words. Like all text it's raised to read on the window, 4.5:1, as the bar's placeholder is on its panel, so where the window needs that it's as bright as a host. - Ours: Site Controls 1 pt past the field's end and Copy URL 28 before it, their plates overlapping by 2 as Arc's do, with Lucide's sliders-horizontal and link glyphs at 14 pt, the header's glyph colour and hover plate. Site Controls opens its panel on the press, as a pop-up button does, and a second press closes it (› Extensions › Site Controls). A click on Copy URL draws Lucide's check in the glyph's place as the link leaves (Motion › Check draw, Arc's), keeps it for 2 s, then brings the link back (Motion › Glyph change). It shows ⇧⌘C's toast too, as Arc's does. The host truncates before them.
- **A page's question** (Chrome's rules; the look is ours, *interim* until Arc's is measured). When a page asks for a permission, such as the camera or managing windows, a card hangs under the URL field, where Chrome's bubble hangs under its address bar (in Little ilo, under its bar: Little ilo › A page's question): 6 under the field, as wide, radius 12, in the window's fill with a white 0.10 edge and a shadow. It reads "<site> wants to" in 13 pt medium white, Chrome's phrase for each thing asked in the dialog's detail colour, then Block and Allow side by side, 28 tall, on the URL field's plate, with the ladder's 0.20 under the pointer and 0.16 pressed; × at its top right dismisses it, on the ladder's white 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed. The site is Chrome's, with the scheme shown unless it's https. Block and Allow are kept for the site, in Chrome's site settings until Site Controls lists them; × keeps nothing, and the site may ask again. The card stays with its tab: another tab takes it down, and coming back brings it again. With the sidebar hidden, a question brings it out and keeps it out until it's answered. Chrome's quiet prompt, for sites that ask too much, shows nothing. Agents can't answer: an answer could give a site the camera or where the user is.
- **Cast** (Chrome's media router; the look is ours, *interim*, as Arc's dialog isn't recorded). View › Cast…, the command bar's Cast…, the page menu's Cast… and a page asking to cast, such as YouTube's Cast button, open the tab's Cast card where a page's question hangs, under the URL field on the question's plate, bringing a hidden sidebar out and keeping it out until it closes. It has Chrome's header, such as "Cast tab", over Chrome's devices, each a 40 tall row with Lucide's cast glyph at 16 pt, its name in 13 pt over Chrome's status in 11 pt secondary, the ladder's 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed; with none yet, Chrome's "Looking for devices…". A click on a device casts the tab, or the page's presentation where it has one, as Chrome's does, and one casting stops; one connecting, disconnecting or unavailable is at half opacity and takes no click. It closes on ×, View › Cast… again, another tab shown, or casting starting, as Chrome's dialog closes then. A mirrored tab's row shows the screen-share mark (Sidebar › Calls). The browser finds devices itself, on the local network, with no Google service: ungoogled-chromium builds without mDNS, so Chrome looks with DIAL alone, as the card opens, and finds the Cast devices that answer it. Agents can't cast: it shows the page on a screen others can see.
- **Protected-media note** (Ours: Arc and Dia have nothing to explain, as their engines carry Widevine). ilo's Chromium has no Widevine module, since ungoogled-chromium ships none, so Netflix, Disney+, Spotify and Prime Video fail with an error of their own that says nothing of why. When a page asks for a key system Chromium can't play, Widevine or PlayReady, a card of the question's look hangs where the question does. Where the Mac can show the page in Safari's engine (decision 1; macOS 14 and later), it reads "This site's video needs Safari's engine. Play it in this tab." in 13 pt medium white, with one button as wide as the card, Play in This Tab, on the question's button plate. The words say where the video plays, in this tab, and nothing of signing in, as the user stays signed in: the site's cookies and localStorage go to Safari's engine before its page loads, and come back to Chromium when Site Controls turns it off (decision 1). It's named for Safari, which people know, rather than WebKit (the owner's words, 2026-10-27). The button loads the tab's URL in Safari's engine, and the profile keeps the site for it: from then on the site's pages open there with no note, in every tab of that profile, until Site Controls turns it off. A link to another site goes back to Chromium, in the same tab. A private window's tab uses a store that keeps nothing, and its session keeps the site only until it ends. Where Safari's engine isn't there (macOS 13), the note reads "This site's video needs a module ilo doesn't have yet." and its button is Open in Safari, which opens the tab's URL there and leaves the tab and the note as they are. In a private window it reads "Open in Safari, not private", as Safari's window keeps history. On Windows, while ilo isn't the system's default browser, its button is Open in Default Browser ("Open in Default Browser, not private" in a private window), named for what it does, which opens the tab's URL there through the system's http handler: Edge and Chrome play what ilo can't on Windows 10, on ARM or without the graphics' hardware DRM. Once ilo is the default it has none, as the page would come back (ours, #2468). Linux has none: a Widevine page's note offers Google's module instead, no Linux browser has the other DRMs, and a Flatpak can't tell whether it's the default. Where the platform can do nothing, the note shows without a button. Its ×, shown under the pointer with the tip "Don't Show for This Site", and escape while the UI has the keyboard close it for the site in the profile, across launches, and Site Controls then offer Safari's engine for the site; otherwise it goes once the tab is at another site, and shows only for the tab that asked. In Safari's engine, a protected video the display connection's protection (HDCP) falls short for plays its sound over a black picture, as it does in Safari, and the site says nothing: a dock's link can be the cause, on every display while it's plugged in (the owner's Disney+, 2026-10-28). Once the page's key reads "output-restricted", the note reads "This video needs a protected display connection. A dock or adapter may be limiting it." with no button, as the fix is the user's cable. It shows once a tab and site; its × (tip "Close") and escape close it for that tab alone, as the cause is the desk's, not the site's. A page's question takes its place while it's up. It doesn't bring out a hidden sidebar, as a question does, since it asks for nothing (*interim*). ClearKey, which plays, brings none, and a page is told once for each key system it asks for. Agents get no tool for it, as the button hands the user's page to an engine the browser can't enforce their rules in; the note shows for an agent's tab like any other.
- **Which engine** (Ours). A tab showing its page in Safari's engine shows Lucide's compass at 12 pt, 5 after the host in the URL field, in the host's colour at 0.6. It's quiet: the Site Controls name the engine and turn it off. The rest of the browser treats the tab like any other: it has the same sidebar row, title, favicon, back and forward, reload, sound and mute, and the same full screen and session restore. Find, zoom, DevTools, Capture, print, downloads, file pickers and the page's permission questions don't reach it yet. The page's own popups open as Chromium tabs.
- **The key** (ours, for Chrome's key icon). While Chrome's toolbar would show its key for the page, as when the site's saved logins were filled in or one was just saved, or an offer to save one waits, the URL field shows Lucide's key-round after the host, and after the compass, drawn as the compass is, 12 pt, 5 after it, in the host's colour at 0.6. A press on it brings back an offer that showing another tab closed, or else opens the Site Controls, whose Passwords line lists the site's logins. Agents get no path to it: no call reads it, and they hear none of its callbacks.
- **Translate** (Arc's URL bar offers to translate a site that isn't in the user's language and, once it's translated, Use Original; on the device, the owner, 2026-10-29, #671). A page that declares a language the user doesn't read (its html lang or Content-Language, as Chrome's language detection finds it), neither the one pages are translated into nor one in the system's Preferred Languages, as Arc offers none for a preferred language, shows Lucide's languages after the host and the key, drawn as the key is, 12 pt, in the host's colour at 0.6; ours, *interim* until Arc's prompt is measured: no card opens on load. A press opens a card where a page's question hangs: "Translate this page?", "From Spanish to English, on this Mac.", Not Now and Translate in the accent, and ×; escape closes it. Translating, it says "Translating from Spanish…" and "The page doesn't leave this Mac." with Use Original, and the mark is the accent at 0.5; translated, the card closes and the mark is the accent, and its press says "Translated from Spanish", "To English, on this Mac." with Use Original. Under the answers, on a row of its own as its words alone, as Always Allow is on an agent's card, Always Translate Spanish translates the page and every page in Spanish as it opens, as Chrome's Always Translate does, which Arc keeps; translated, the card says "To English, on this Mac, as every page in Spanish is." with Stop Always Translating, which leaves the page as it is and has the next ask again. The languages are the computer's, as its translator is, so every window, profile and private window translates them, and Settings › Features lists them under Always translated, each with Remove. The command bar has Translate Page and Use Original, and the page menu has them after Cast… (Menus › Page menu). The page's title and each run of its text are translated in place, its spaces kept, but not script, style, code, fields, editable regions or what the page marks translate="no" or notranslate; text the page adds after stays in its own language, *interim*, and so do frames, attributes such as alt and placeholder, and a page that declares no language, as there's no model on the device to tell its words' language, *interim*. The translator is the system's, with the models it keeps: Apple's Translation from macOS 26, into the first language the Mac prefers. A language whose model isn't on the Mac says "Spanish isn't on this Mac yet", with Settings…, which opens Language & Region where Translation Languages adds it, and Translate. Before macOS 26, and on Linux and Windows for now, nothing's offered. Agents get no path, and an agent's Space offers none, so an agent reads a page in its own words. Arc's Never Translate and Settings' reset of translated-site prompts aren't built, as no card opens on load for them to quiet. Nor is a Settings switch that stops the offer, as Arc's Languages › Google Translate does, for the same reason (orchestrator's call 2026-11-10, #671, for the owner's review).
- **Copy URL** (Arc 1.170.0 menus). ⇧⌘C copies the tab's URL, and ⌥⇧⌘C a Markdown link, `[title](url)`. Arc also has Copy URL as Quote (⌃⇧⌘C). ⇧⌘C's toast (Shared rules › Toast) says "Copied Current URL", with a share button after the words (Arc 1.170 rec).
