Ours: the title's brackets are escaped, the URL is copied without its tracking parameters (› Command bar › Copy links without tracking, Dia's), and the keys, menus and command bar show the toast, "Copied Current URL" or "Copied URL as Markdown", for 2 s (*interim*: Arc's stayed while its share menu was open, so its time isn't recorded, and its Markdown words aren't either). The share button is the capture toast's Share, its glyph, tokens and place (Capture › What the rows do), and opens the system's share sheet for the page, hanging from where it was pressed; Linux has no share sheet, so there the toast has none, as the capture toast hasn't. Copy URL as Markdown's toast has none. The sidebar's button shows the toast and a checkmark (Sidebar › URL field).
- **Paste URL as New Tab** (Arc help). ⌥⌘V, and the command of that name in the command bar, open the clipboard's link as a tab at the top of Today and show it. Ours: the clipboard is read only when it runs, and only one http(s) link opens, as a drop on the sidebar takes, so text, `javascript:` and `file:` open nothing. The page keeps ⌥⌘V if it handles it. No agent path: the clipboard is the user's.

**Width**

| Item | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Default | 228 on a fresh install, at 1280 × 800 and at 1512 × 949. Dia's is 190 | Arc 1.170.0 ax and preferences, Dia 1.50.1 bin |
| Minimum / maximum | 164 / 500. The width stops at each, and dragged on past the minimum the sidebar hides in one frame and keeps the width it had | Arc 1.170 rec |
| Persistence | kept per window; double-click on the handle resets it | Dia, P§4 |

- Ours: 228 at first. The recording starts at 260, but that is the owner's width, not Arc's default, which the fresh install's 228 is. The handle is the 6 pt margin right of the rows, with the left-right resize cursor, or the left or right one at a limit. The width stops at 164 and 500. Arc's hide happens off the recording's pointer, so ours hides once the pointer is past half the minimum, where AppKit's split view collapses a pane (*interim*), and the sidebar comes back at its width from before the drag, as Arc's reveal did. Arc's width came back a few points from past the maximum in one take; that small bounce isn't built. A double-click resets it to 228 on the Space pages' spring. The core saves the width with the Spaces. Back, forward and reload keep to the sidebar's right edge; where one would come within 13 of the sidebar button, reload goes, then forward, so reload goes under 228 and forward under 194 (ours, *interim*: Arc's narrow sidebar isn't measured).

**Sections, top to bottom.** Header, URL field, favorites, then the Space's pinned tabs, the Clear divider, the Today tabs and "+ New Tab". At the foot, the window's tasks (Agent task) over its media players, and under them Restart to Update while an update waits (Updates), all of which stay where they are as the Spaces change and the list scrolls. The footer holds the Space switcher (Arc structure). The Space's name starts at x 41 after its icon, 2 before the titles (Arc 1.170.0 ax, on a Space with an icon); with no icon it starts on the favicons' left edge, x 18 (ours), and at 41 again while the icon picker's placeholder holds the icon's place (Spaces › Icon). Measure what the footer holds. The favorites stay put while the list scrolls. A favorites block taller than 40 % of the sidebar scrolls with the list instead (Ardia).

**Favorites** (Arc's favorites are Dia's Top Apps dock, so the values without an Arc source are *interim*)

| Item | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Tile height / gap | 47 / 8, at every width. Dia's are 41 / 6 | Arc 1.170 cap 2× (outside measurement, #1399) |
| Side inset | 8, the rows' plates' and the URL field's. Dia's is 6 | ours |
| First row top | 90 below the window top, 9 under the URL field. Dia's is 54 under its header, and Arc's favorites area starts at 82 (ax): re-measure with favorites in Arc | ours |
| Columns | the most, up to 4, that keep a tile at least 47 wide; then the fewest that need no more rows, so 5 go 3 + 2, 6 3 × 2, 7 4 + 3, 9 3 × 3, and 4 at 200 go 2 × 2. Every tile is as wide, a short last row keeps that width and starts on the left, and a tile's x is on the half point, its width not rounded. At 228 that's 4 across, 47 wide | Arc 1.171 ax, 41 cases of 1–9 favorites at 347, 230, 200, 140 and 100 (outside measurement, #1399) |
| Radius | 10 | Dia bin |
| Resting | fill white 0.10, 0.5 pt stroke white 0.14. Under the pointer white 0.20, the ladder's hover over it, coming and going in one frame as a row's does (ours) | Dia 1.50.1 bin |
| Favicon | centred, as a row's: 16 in an 18 box, at half opacity while unloaded. Size: measure | Dia cap |
| Badge for a tile away from its URL | the return mark, as on a pinned tab's row (Pinned tabs). Dia has none | ours, #2338; Dia 1.53.0 bin |
| Hover | hover card with title and URL (see Motion) | Dia, D›Tab hover card motion |
| Double-click | goes back to the favorite's URL, like ⌘↩. A tile already there doesn't reload | Dia, P§2 |

The selected tile is themed by its icon (Dia 1.50.1 bin, D›Sidebar). Draw the icon at no more than 32 px on its long side. Average its pixels with alpha ≥ 0.975, then take their mean RGB distance from that average:

- **Distance ≥ 0.055 (colourful).** The tile is white 0.20 (dark) or white (light). Over it goes the icon at 22 % opacity, blurred with radius 5 and aspect-filled to 2.5× the tile. The ring is a 3 pt border showing the same blurred icon over black (dark: at 75 %, saturation 2, brightness −0.1) or white (light: at 100 %).
- **Distance < 0.055 (one colour).** The tile is filled with the mean colour, the icon is drawn as a white template, and the ring is white with soft-light compositing. A near-white icon (relative luminance > 0.88) gets a black fill and a white 0.30 ring instead.
- **No usable icon.** Fill black (dark) or white (light), with the default selection ring, white 0.45, 0.5 pt.
- A secondary selection (multi-select) gets the secondary fill and no ring. Emoji icons draw at 16 pt and are themed the same way.

Ours (dark only, `app/src/shell/tile_theme.rs`): the theme is made from the favicon's pixels the first time its tile is drawn selected, and kept until the favicon changes. Where it differs from Dia's:

- GPUI has no blur or blend mode, so the blur is a Gaussian of sigma 5 on the icon at ≤ 32 px, cropped to the icon's own extent with nothing beyond it (Dia's crop isn't known), and the ring is an image baked for an 88 pt tile and stretched between its corners to the tile's width. Its saturation and brightness are applied in the image.
- The soft-light ring is the fill's colour with white soft-lit over it (W3C's formula), drawn as a plain border, which is the same where the fill is opaque. All themed rings are 3 pt (Dia's `itemStrokeWidth`; the one-colour and near-white rings' width is assumed).
- An emoji icon has no theme yet and shows as no usable icon: GPUI can't give the pixels of text, so it needs the core to hand over the emoji as an image.
- Light mode: not themed. A light window's selected tile is Dia's white one with the default ring, whatever its icon, until the light theme is built.

On every tile and row, shown or not, a one-colour favicon that would barely show on the sidebar, under 3:1 against black in a dark window or against white in a light one (WCAG 1.4.11), is drawn in the ink instead, as the shown tile draws a one-colour icon in white (ours: Arc and Dia weren't recorded with a black favicon). A black logo reads on a dark sidebar, a white one on a light sidebar, and a colourful one is left as it is.

What a favorite does (ours, *interim* until Arc's are captured):

- **Scope** (ours: the owner's calls, 2026-10-27; Arc's favorites follow the profile, confirm in the capture). Settings › Tabs & Sidebar › Share favorites chooses who shares them, and the core keeps the choice with the Spaces:
  - *In Each Profile's Spaces*, the default and today's behaviour: every Space of a profile shows the same tiles, and another profile's have their own.
  - *In Every Space*: the same sites in every Space, whatever its profile. That is the sites, not the signed-in session: each profile opens a site in a tab of its own, made unloaded and loaded when it first shows, so a Space never shows another profile's login.
  - *In Each Space*: every Space has its own tiles. A new Space starts with none.
  - A favorite is a site (URL, title, icon) in an ordered list at that scope. The tab behind a tile belongs to whoever shows it: the profile's own tab, in no Space, under the first two, and the Space's under Each Space. Click, ⌘1–⌘9, Back to Favorite URL, close-to-unload and a Space remembering the favorite it showed work on that tab, as they did.
  - Add, remove and reorder act on the list at the scope, so under Every Space a site added, removed or moved is so in every profile, and Remove from Favorites closes the site's tabs in the other profiles. The tab of the Space it happened in goes to the top of that Space's Today.
  - Changing the choice never loses a favorite. Narrowing copies each list to every narrower holder (every Space → each profile → each Space); a loaded tab keeps its page in the Space or profile that had it, and the copies are unloaded tabs. Widening merges by URL: the list of the Space the window active last shows comes first, then each other list's extras in the order of the Spaces; a duplicate's extra tabs close, keeping a loaded tab over an unloaded one.
  - The tiles stay put while the Spaces' pages swipe under them, and the tiles of a Space that has other ones swap in at once. Selecting a favorite that the Space shown doesn't show, as an agent can, shows the first Space that does.
  - The user's favorites never move into an agent's Space, and favoriting a tab of an agent's Space does nothing under Each Space. Agents have no tool to change the choice; their favorite operations follow it through the calls the UI uses.
- **Tooltip.** A folder's row shows its name after a moment (Arc 1.170.0 cap), but a closed one with tabs in it, which has its preview instead (Sidebar › Folders). Arc's tiles name their favorite the same way, but ours, and every tab's row, have the hover card instead (#430, ours): 0.5 s after the pointer rests on the row, Dia's delay, sooner than the tooltip's, it opens beside the row with a picture of the page as it last looked (none for the tab shown, its favicon until there's one), the title and host, and Pin or Unpin, Add to or Remove from Favorites, Split, and Share This Tab Instead while a page shares another tab (Arc shows it on a row's hover), the calls the row's menu and its drag make. It comes in and goes out in Dia's motion (Motion › Hover card in, out), growing and shrinking about its top left corner, the one beside its row (ours), drawn by the UI from its window's first frame on screen, and its window hides as the fade out ends. While it shows, the next row's swaps in at once; leaving the rows, scrolling, pressing or any key, in the page or the UI, closes it, as in Dia. The footer's Library button names itself with its shortcut's keys on chips: View Library ⇧ ⌘ L (Arc 1.170 rec, 2026-10-26, at 2×). Ours (*interim*: Arc's other buttons aren't recorded): the header's sidebar button, back, forward and reload (stop while the page loads) and Copy URL do the same, each with the menus' name and the keys the menus show for it, the user's own from Settings › Shortcuts, so a command with no key shows its name alone. The sidebar button says Hide Sidebar docked and Lock Sidebar in the reveal panel, Arc's labels for it. A dimmed button names itself too, as the system's do.
  - Look (Arc 1.170 rec, at full opacity, one style for every tooltip): a capsule 27 tall, round at half its height but for the corner toward what it names, which is nearly square (radius about 2), in #090C4B with a 0.5 pt rim of white at about 0.12. Its text is 12 pt semibold, #E2E5FF, inset 11. Then chips 18.5 wide at radius 3.5 in #D2D3FC, each a body 16.25 tall, centred on the capsule, over a 2 pt lip of #8486E3, on a pitch of 22, the first 8 after the text and the last 10 from the end, their glyphs 10 pt semibold #1F2AFF. View Library ⇧⌘L is 161.5 wide. Arc's square corner is the top left, for the tooltip beside the Library button, and it is the pointer: it sits at what the tooltip names. Ours (*interim*, only that one is recorded) is the same under a tile or a header button, where the top left corner sits under the middle of it and the capsule goes right from there, or the top right corner when it goes left from there (see Place); the bottom corners when it goes above. A row's and the footer's buttons' is the top left, which already sits at what they name: a folder's under its icon, a footer button's level with it and 3.5 past its plate. A key named in words, as Space is, widens its chip to 3 each side of it. Whether it follows the Space colour isn't recorded. A name past 320 truncates, and the keys stay whole.
  - Place: beside the Library button, 3.5 past its plate and centred on it top to bottom (Arc). Off a header button or a tile, it hangs from the bottom right of a 16 pt box around the glyph: the capsule's square top left corner 8 right of the glyph's centre and 7 below it, over the plate's lower right, whatever the tooltip's width, and the capsule going right from there, over the page if it reaches past the sidebar (Arc 1.170). Arc's row tip follows the pointer at +(13, 14); our rows have the hover card instead. Ours (*interim*): if that would cross the window's right edge, 8 in, it is mirrored: the square top right corner 8 left of the centre, the capsule going left, if it fits that side. A folder's has its text lined up with the row's title, so its plate hangs 10 outside it, under the row. A tooltip stays inside the window, moved sideways to keep 8 from its left and right edges (ours), the left one first, and goes above with the bottom corners if it doesn't fit below: a button's 7 above its glyph's centre, a folder's 6 above its row. Site Controls' extension tiles and Clear on the divider follow the tile rule.
  - The command bar's remove button names itself and its key: Remove from History ⌥⌘⌫, and Shift+Del off the Mac (ours: a MacBook has no ⌦, and ⇧⌦ is the same removal). It follows the button rule inside the bar rather than the window, so it goes above only when the bar has no room below, and its window goes over the bar's, though an overlay otherwise goes over a tooltip. The bar closing ends it.
  - Timing (Arc 1.170 rec): it shows in one frame, at full opacity, with no fade in, and fades out over about 0.27 s, ease-out (Motion › Tooltip out). It shows 1.6 s after the pointer's last move over the tile, row or button (Arc 1.170 rec, twice), so a pointer passing on its way shows none. It runs on one timer that each move starts again and the pointer leaving cancels, and the next shows at once if another went less than 0.5 s ago (ours, the system's), as the pointer crosses the gap to the next tile. The fade is its window's alpha, which AppKit animates without waking the UI, and a tooltip shown while one fades goes back to full at once.
  - It's a child window the mouse goes through, never key, hung from what it names and not from the pointer, so it sits in the same place wherever the pointer is on it. A click, a right-click, a scroll, a Space switch, a drag, a menu, the window losing the keyboard and the app going to the background end it. A folder with no name has none.
- Add to Favorites in a tab's menu makes the tab the last favorite, at its pinned URL if it's pinned, else at its page, and takes it out of its split. Remove from Favorites in the tile's menu puts it at the top of Today in the Space shown (Scope, above, has the lists they act on).
- A favorite's tile marks its page as its row does (Sidebar › Calls), 12 pt, 4 in from the tile's top right, in secondary text, and with an eighth note while it plays sound, unmuted, where Arc animates notes on a music favorite and badges one shared into a call (Arc 1.170 release notes). Ours is still and the same for every page (*interim*: Arc's isn't measured).
- A Space shows at most 12 favorites, as Arc's profile keeps 12 (Arc release notes, up from 8). With 12, Add to Favorites in a tab's menu is dimmed, the command bar's Favorite Tab isn't offered, the hover card has no Add to Favorites, and a row dragged over the tiles stays a row, its drop line in the list, so nothing marks a place it can't take. A tile still moves among them. A list longer already, from before the cap or an import, is kept whole: none is taken away.
- Dragging moves a tab between the tiles and the rows. Above the bottom of the tiles it would have with the dragged tab among them, a dragged row is drawn as a tile centred on the pointer, and the tiles make room for it at the place nearest its centre. A dragged tile below that is drawn as a row centred on the pointer, and the drop line marks its place as for a dragged row. The list stays put either way: a row dragged over the tiles stays in its place until it's let go (Arc 1.170 rec). Crossing that line, the row turns into the tile on Favorites room (*interim*: Arc's turn isn't measured): the plate's width, height, corners and fill go to the tile's, the title fades out over the first half and the favicon goes to the tile's middle. Crossing back reverses it from where it is. The point the pointer holds goes from where it held the row to where it holds the tile, so the plate grows and shrinks around the pointer. Let go mid-turn, the drop waits for the turn to end. A split view's row turns into its tile, its pages one favorite (Split view › As a favorite). Dropped on the tiles, a row becomes a favorite there, and dropped in the list a tile goes there, pinned at its favorite's URL among the pinned tabs. Between the tiles, a tile reorders. A tile passing another clicks the trackpad as a row does.
- The tiles and the list's top move on Favorites room while they make room (Arc 1.170 rec), and on Row motion when they settle, as the favorites change and a dropped tab lands. A new tile is in its place at once. Nothing moves while the Space shown is being filled, as at launch.
- A click shows it. ⌘1–⌘9, next and previous tab and ⌃Tab count the favorites first. Include Favorites in ⌘1–⌘8 in Settings › Shortcuts, on by default, turned off has ⌘1–⌘8 start at the Space's first tab after them, as Arc's Include Favorites in Ordering does (owner, 2026-10-29). Last Tab Goes to the Ninth Tab, off by default, has ⌘9 show the ninth tab, counted the same way, and nothing in a Space with fewer, as Arc's choice of ninth or last tab does. A Space remembers the favorite it showed, across a quit too, and forgets one it stops showing when the choice of scope or its profile changes.
- The tile's menu: Back to Favorite URL (⌘↩, away from it), Replace Favorite URL with Current (away from it, as a pinned tab's, Pinned tabs below), Duplicate Tab (a copy at the top of Today, shown), Remove from Favorites, Change Icon… and Remove Icon once it has one, as Arc's favorite changes its icon, then Close Tab (⌘W). Change Icon… opens the icon picker as for a pinned tab (Tab list › menu), under the tile's middle, and the tile draws the emoji in the favicon's place, untinted, as Favorite selection theming has no theme from an emoji yet. The icon is the favorite's tab's, so its profile's, and is kept across a quit as a tab's name is. Closing unloads it and keeps the tile, as for a pinned tab, and shows the Space's top Today tab, else its lowest pinned tab still loaded. No Rename, Edit… of its URL or Move to Space yet: Arc's favorite has Edit… beside Replace, and the field Rename and Edit… type in is in a row, which a tile hasn't.
- Links a favorite opens go at the top of Today in the Space shown.
- Later: themes for emoji icons, and Arc's measured turn between a dragged row and its tile.

**Pinned tabs** (Arc: per Space. Dia 1.52 has the same section.)

- They are rows that look like tab rows, sitting between the favorites and the Clear divider, 6 pt under the tiles (Ardia).
- Closing one (⌘W or its row's −) unloads its page and keeps the row (Dia rec, P§2), as Arc's Close and Keep Pinned does. Closing the tab shown, a pinned tab or a favorite, shows the tab shown before it that's still open in the Space: a Today tab, or a pinned tab or favorite with its page, so closing one loads no other. The list glides to its row, which wears Focus Sidebar's ring until another tab shows or 2.5 s pass (Arc 1.170 rec: Arc selects the tab used before, scrolls the sidebar to it and flashes its keyboard ring around it). The 2.5 s is *interim*: Arc's ring was still there 2.5 s after it was drawn, in the one recording of it, which ended there. With no such tab, the core's pick stands: the top Today tab, else a pinned tab still loaded, else no page. An agent's close takes the core's pick too, as what was shown before is the user's. Closing the last tab closes the window (Dia rec, P§2).
- Away from its pinned URL, the favicon turns into a back arrow under the pointer and the title reads "Back to Pinned URL". A click goes back, and so does ⌘↩ (Dia 1.53.0 bin). At rest, a pinned tab or favorite away from its pinned URL carries a return mark on its icon, on its row, its tile and its split segment alike: Lucide's undo-2 at 9 pt in the secondary ink (0.62), in a round notch 11 across, cut out of the icon's bottom right and reaching 3 past the 18 pt favicon box's corner. What's under the icon shows through the notch: the window, the selected plate or the tile. It dims with the favicon while unloaded. Under the pointer on the favicon, the back arrow takes the icon's place and the mark goes with it. The mark comes and goes on Glyph change (Motion), the notch opening and closing with it. It shows at once on a row just drawn and on a tab only switched to. Ours (#2338): Arc and Dia show nothing at rest, so a click that goes back surprised anyone who didn't know the tab had a home. It's on the icon, not the title, because the title is what's read to find the tab and dim already means unloaded. It's at the bottom right because the tile's top right holds its speaker and capture glyph. The notch is the one form that still reads as going back at 18 pt on a 1x screen: a badge's knocked-out arrow blurs, and a dot already means loading or unread. Ours: that click shows the tab, after it starts going back, and the press doesn't, so its old page never loads. A ⌘-click goes back too and keeps the page it was at in a copy with its history at the top of Today, shown, as Arc's ⌘-click on a pinned tab's icon opens it in a new tab (Arc's help centre; whether Arc's shows it: measure), with Duplicate's and Back to Pinned URL's core calls as its agent path.
- Pinned URL, in a pinned tab's menu, holds Replace Pinned URL with Current, away from it, which makes the page the tab's at the URL it goes back to, and Edit…, as Arc's. Edit… turns the title into a field holding the pinned URL, all selected, as Rename does: ↩ makes what's typed, read as the address bar reads it, the URL the tab goes back to, Esc cancels, and kept empty nothing changes. Either is one edit on the window's ⌘Z history, Change Pinned URL, which gives the old URL back. Their agent path is the core's bc_tab_set_pinned_url, on a tab of the agent's own Space. Arc's look for a navigated-away pinned tab: measure.
- A URL typed into a pinned tab (⌘L) doesn't replace its page: it opens as a new Today tab at the top, selected, and closing that tab goes back to the pinned one (Arc cap). Ours does the same, for an address other than the page's, from the command bar and from agents, who go through the same core call, so a pinned tab stays what it was pinned as. The same address loads in place. Favorites load what's typed in place (*interim*: Arc's isn't recorded).
- A pinned tab has one live page. Selecting it in a second window moves the page there, with history and playing media intact. The first window keeps a frozen frame under a wash: white 0.40 in light mode (Dia cap), black 0.25 in dark mode (Ardia). Clicking the frozen frame pulls the page back (Dia, P§2).
- Links ⌘-clicked in a pinned tab gather in a group just below the pinned tabs (Dia rec, P§2). Measure what Arc does: Peek covers some of these links.
- ⌘D pins or unpins. Dragging a tab above the divider pins it, below unpins it, and onto the favorites makes it a favorite.
- Collapse Pinned Tabs, after the sidebar's item in View and in the command bar, where "hide" finds it, hides the Space's pinned rows and folders but the tab shown's, as a closed folder hides its rows, and Expand Pinned Tabs, the same item while they're collapsed, shows them again (Arc's menus and bar). ⌘1–⌘9 and Next and Previous Tab skip what's hidden. Each Space keeps its own, over a quit, in the core (`bc_space_set_pinned_open`). Arc 1.171 keeps it per window instead, which isn't carried over: a Space shown in two windows at once is rare, and per window would put the window's state into the core's stored windows for little gain (#1400). The Space's name row does it too (Arc 1.171): a press anywhere on it but its ••• collapses or expands them, and under the pointer a chevron takes the icon's slot, down while they show and turned a quarter to point right while they're collapsed. It turns over 0.12 s (ease-in-out, ours), comes and goes with the row's plate in one frame, and stays while they're collapsed, so the way back shows; the icon comes back only once they're expanded. While they're collapsed the Space's name lights a step, to 0.81, where Arc's turns bold (Arc's release notes; ours is semibold already, the type scale's heaviest; whether Arc's is only while the tab shown is a pinned one: measure). Ours: a Space with no icon has the chevron after its name, 12 pt in a 20 pt box 2 after it; with no pinned rows to fold the row has no chevron and its press does nothing. A press acts at once, as a folder's does, and the rows move on its fold.
- Focus Sidebar, in Tabs after Reveal Tab in Sidebar and in the command bar, where "keyboard" finds it, gives the sidebar the keyboard, showing it if it's hidden, and rings the tab shown's row, else the first. ↑ and ↓ move the ring over the rows ⌘1–⌘9 count and the folders' rows, the favorites' tiles first and past what collapsed pinned tabs and closed folders hide, stopping at the ends, and the list scrolls to it. On a folder → opens it and ← closes it, and on a row in a folder ← goes up to the folder, as a tree's keys do (#1384, ours: Arc's aren't recorded). Return shows the ringed tab and gives its page the keyboard, as a click on its row does, and on a folder opens or closes it with the ring staying on it; escape gives the page the keyboard back without showing another. Close Tab (⌘W) closes the ringed row as its ✕ does, its split with it, rather than the tab shown, the ring going to the row after it or, on the last, the one before; on a pinned row it unloads and the ring stays, and on a folder it does nothing, as a folder isn't a tab and its menu deletes it. ⇧F10 or the menu key opens the ringed row's menu under it, as a right-click on it does. A press anywhere, a key in the page, a field taking the keyboard, or the sidebar hiding ends it (Arc 1.170's release notes). Its key is F6, Chrome's for moving the keyboard out of the page, as Arc's old ⌘. is Stop Loading in 1.170 and here; Settings › Shortcuts can give it another. Like Chrome's, it isn't reserved: a page that uses F6 keeps it. On a Mac keyboard whose top row is media keys, it's fn-F6. Ours (*interim*, Arc's look isn't measured): a 2 pt ring in the ink at 0.45 inside the row's plate or the tile. Agents get none, as it moves only the user's view and keyboard.

**Folders** (Arc: in the pinned tabs, per Space. Arc 1.170.0 ax and cap, sessions 10–14)

| Item | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Indent | 16 per folder: in one, the favicon is at x 33, the title at x 59 and the plate from 24 | Arc 1.170.0 ax, cap |
| Row | a tab row's, with the folder's icon in the favicon's place, open or closed, and its name as the title | Arc 1.170.0 cap |
| Closing and opening | Arc's accessibility call moves the rows below in one frame; a click: measure | Arc 1.170.0 rec |
| Hover | the row plate, and a tooltip with the name after a moment | Arc 1.170.0 cap |
| ⌘1–⌘9 | count the tabs in an open folder | Arc 1.170.0 |
| New Folder… | in the Tabs menu, under Open Little Arc or Peek | Arc 1.170.0 menus |

- Ours (*interim* until Arc's folders are captured):
  - The icon is Lucide's folder or folder-open at the favicon's size, in the secondary colour.
  - New Folder…, from the Tabs menu, the command bar, the footer's + or a right-click on the sidebar's empty space, makes an open folder named "New Folder" at the bottom of the pinned tabs, with its name in a field, all selected. ↩ keeps what's typed, and Esc or nothing typed keeps "New Folder".
  - Add to Folder › in a tab's menu (ours, *interim* until Arc's menus are recorded, #433, #1088), as Chrome's Add Tab to Group, lists New Folder, then the Space's folders, each named by its path, so a nested one follows its parent ("Docs / Old"). New Folder puts the tab in a new folder in its place: a pinned tab's folder takes the tab's spot, and a Today tab is pinned first, its folder at the bottom of the pinned tabs. A folder takes the tab at its bottom. With several rows chosen, their menu's Add to Folder › does the same with them all, in their order, its New Folder at the bottom of the pinned tabs as Arc's New Folder with Tabs. Each is one step to undo. Agents have the same through Move to and New Folder (`bc_tab_move`, `bc_folder_create`).
  - A folder made from tabs is named for their site when every one is on the same one: the site's name as a part of its titles spells it, as "Pull requests · GitHub" names github.com's "GitHub", else the host without "www.". Tabs on different sites, or a file, make "New Folder". Either way the name is in the field, all selected, so typing replaces it.
  - A press opens or closes it, before the release, as Arc's does (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×: closing at 166.97 s, let go at 167.09; opening at 169.94, let go at about 170.12). Held, it keeps its hover fill, without the selected fill a tab's row takes. The rows under it move on Folder open and Folder close (Arc 1.170 rec), and the rows it shows fade in together, as rows Undo brings back do.
  - A double-click renames it, after its first press has opened or closed it, and so does Rename Folder in its menu. The menu also has Copy All Links, which copies the link of every tab in it and in the folders in it, one a line and without tracking parameters, which reads Copy All Links as Markdown while ⌥ is held, in its place as the tab menu's Copy Link as Markdown is, and copies each as a `[title](url)` line (Arc's); Paste URL as New Tab, which opens the clipboard's link as a tab at the folder's top, opening the folder, as ⌥⌘V does at the top of Today; and Delete Folder, which leaves what was in it where it was, in the folder that held it. Change Icon… hangs the icon picker's Emoji tab from the folder, whose emoji then takes the folder glyph's place, with Remove Icon after it once there's one, each undone with ⌘Z; Duplicate makes a copy under the folder of it and everything in it, its tabs with their history, names and icons, unloaded until shown, which isn't undone, as a tab's Duplicate isn't (Arc's menu; ours how each looks). Move to Space › follows Duplicate and lists the user's other Spaces as a pinned tab's does, never an Agent Space, and takes the folder whole to the bottom of that Space's pinned tabs: the folders in it with their names, icons and whether they're open, and its tabs in their order, pinned at their URLs, in their split views, their pages as they are; a window showing one goes with it, as with a tab. It's one step for ⌘Z, whose toast says "Folder Moved to Space". Into another profile's Space it goes as a tab does, after the one-time warning: its tabs there are new ones, which load again, and nothing is undone (owner, 2026-11-02, #1353). Agents have it through the same core call (`bc_folder_move_to_space`).
  - Closed, it hides its rows but the tab shown's, which stays listed under it after another tab shows, as Arc keeps it in each window (Arc 1.171, from an outside measurement, #1405). A click on the folder then closes it over the tabs it keeps, for good, their rows sliding away as the rows a closing folder hides, and the next click opens it. While the tab shown is in it, a click opens it, as on any closed folder, and lets go of the tabs it kept. Ours: Arc's click leaves the folder closed while its tab is shown, so the click seems to do nothing and no click opens it; ux-principles › A change shows that it happened, and a press on a folder opens or closes it (#1405). ⌘1–⌘9 skip what a closed folder hides, so each row keeps its number.
  - The tab shown in a closed folder inside another closed folder, or an open one inside a closed one, is listed one under the outermost closed folder, and the folders between stay hidden; that folder's glyph then has three dots in it, 2 pt at a 4 pt pitch, to say there's more between them (Arc 1.171, from an outside measurement, #1405). The dots come and go with the tab shown at once, as a glyph that differs because something else is shown does, and turn in with the folder's glyph as it closes. The Library's card lists it the same way. Ours: the dots are Lucide's folder with three of ours in it; a drop beside that tab lands at its real depth. A tab kept listed after another shows keeps its dots. Its row slides in from under the outer folder's row over 0.15 s, fading in, and the rows under it make room on the same curve, as Arc's does; the curve, the folder's opening ease-out, is ours.
  - Preview (Arc's Folder Preview). The pointer resting on a closed folder's row for 0.45 s shows a list of the tabs in it and in the folders in it, by their rows' titles, the one a window showed last first, each over how long ago that was, under a search named for the folder. It's 240 wide, 4 right of the sidebar, centred on the folder's row and kept 18 inside the window: the search 48 tall, a 1 pt line, then rows from 4 below it on a 45 pitch, 56 + 45 a row and at most 320, past which it scrolls. It comes in over 90 ms, fading in as it grows from 0.85 about the middle of its left edge, and fades out over 80 ms, 40 ms after the pointer has left it and its row. It has the keyboard, with the first row selected: typing narrows the list by title or address and selects the first match, ↑ and ↓ move the selection, and ↩ or a click shows the tab, which peeks under the closed folder without opening it. The row under the pointer has the selected row's fill, both at once, and the pointer doesn't move the selection. With nothing found it says "No tabs matching that search". Once something is typed or an arrow pressed, it goes only with Esc, a choice or a click outside it. Arc 1.171, from an outside measurement (#1405); ours: the look, the hover card's, so 3 more under the last row than Arc's 1 to keep its corners concentric, a date past five weeks for the age, no emoji after the words, and staying once typed in. Arc's magnifying glass on the row isn't built.
  - Dragging. Where a dragged row would land at the end of folders or after one, the pointer's travel right of where it pressed takes it in, a level for each 16 pt, between the levels that place allows: from the row below's to one inside the row above, if that's a folder. A folder drags as a row, among the pinned tabs only, never into itself. What's in it stays while it's dragged and moves with it when it lands.
  - Folders come back after a quit with their names, icons and whether they're open.
  - Collapse All Folders in the command bar closes every folder of the Space shown, on one fold, and Expand All Folders opens them, as Arc's Settings › Shortcuts has, with no key. Neither is undone or has an agent path: they only change the view, as Reveal Tab in Sidebar does.
  - Later: making a folder inside one, and Arc's folder drag, captured.

**Clear divider and Today tabs** (Arc bin and a user's screenshot, D›Arc note: Clear)

- Today tabs are the Space's unpinned tabs. They are archived (see Archive).
- The divider shows with the section while the Space has Today tabs. Its line has no hover state.
- Clear shows only while the pointer is over the sidebar (Arc 1.170.0 cap). Ours, *interim* until Arc's fade is recorded: it fades in and out over 150 ms on the scroll edge's ease-out curve, takes clicks only while shown or fading in, and a reversal starts from the opacity it had. The reveal's panel, which shows only under the pointer, always shows it, with no fade.
- The line is one device pixel of white 0.15 (Arc's separator token; the capture reads 0.14–0.16), 18 pt in from each side of the sidebar (Arc 1.170.0 cap). While Clear shows, it ends 9 pt before the word. Its centre is 25 pt above the centre of the first Today row.
- Clear is the word alone while it would keep the tab shown: the captures with the sidebar hovered show it 29.5 × 15 (ax), with nothing between it and the line's end 9 pt before it. While the tab shown is the Space's only Today tab, or its split is all of Today, so Clear would close it too, an arrow down 8 pt tall comes 9 pt after the line and 3 pt before the word (Arc 1.170.0 cap of a Library card; Arc in use, the owner, 2026-10-26). A Library card follows the same rule for the tab its Space shows. The label is semibold, with a cap height 0.75 of the tab titles' (≈ 10 pt beside 13 pt titles), and it ends where the titles end.
- Ours: the arrow comes and goes on Glyph change (Motion) while the Space stays, and shows at once on another Space's Clear or a divider just drawn. The word stays at the titles' end, and the arrow's room opens before it over the change's 80 ms, ease-in-out, so the line's end slides instead of jumping 11 pt. Arc's motion isn't recorded.
- The word and the arrow are white 0.32, and under the pointer they light up to white 0.81, with no plate (Arc in use, the owner's captures, 2026-10-26). Ours: the change is instant, as the header's hover is (Arc's timing isn't recorded); Clear takes clicks 5 pt past the word and the arrow each way; fading out, it takes no click and doesn't light up.
- The button's tooltip is "Clear unpinned tabs in this space". Arc 1.170.0's Tabs menu and command bar call it Clear Today (⇧⌘K), and Settings › Shortcuts Clear Unpinned Tabs (ax); ours says Clear Unpinned Tabs, *interim*. The command bar finds it by "close unpinned tabs", "clear tabs" and "clear today".

**Tab rows** (Arc 1.170.0 ax, cap and colour tokens; Dia 1.50.1 bin and cap where noted)

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Pitch / row height | 41 / 40 |
| Plate | 36 tall, centred in the row, from x 8 to the width − 8 (from 24 in a folder), radius ≈ 12 |
| Favicon | 18 × 18 at x 17, vertically centred |
| Title | at x 43, 14 pt, ending 17 from the sidebar's edge (x 211 at 228) and cut with an ellipsis (Arc 1.170.0 ax and cap). Dia fades it out at the trailing edge instead |
| Host | under the title, a second line naming the page's host as the URL field does, without "www." or the port, at 11 pt in the secondary ink (0.62), the two lines 16 and 14 tall and centred in the row together. A pinned tab's row has it while its page is on another host than its pinned URL's, as Arc's navigated pinned tabs do, but not at another page of the same host (Arc 1.168 cap: Phantom at /signin shows none), and a tab's row has it in Developer Mode, as Arc's Today tab on a developer-mode host does (Arc 1.170 ax). *Interim*: a renamed pinned tab at its pinned host has none, as Arc's rule for those isn't known and one of the two seen has none; a pinned tab in Developer Mode has it too; the line heights are ours |
| Selected | white 0.20 (Arc's p3 (0.98, 0.984, 1)), and white 0.36 under the pointer (Arc 1.170 rec: (113–116) over the dark Space's (37, 35, 53)), with no line or shadow in dark mode. Light mode: white 0.85 with a black 0.2 shadow (tokens); ours: the shadow 1 down with blur 2, and white 1.0 under the pointer. White title (Dia), ink in light mode |
| Hover | white 0.12, and a close button 26 × 26, 5 inside the plate's right edge, that the title truncates before. The selected row shows it too. Its glyph is two round-ended bars 11.4 long and 2 wide in a 14 pt box, white 0.6 (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×). One shape per action, so ✕ closes, − unloads and the trash removes (owner, 2026-11-02, #1349). On a Today row the bars cross as ✕, which closes the tab for good. On a loaded pinned row, selected or not, in a folder or a split too, they lie level as −, which unloads the tab and keeps its row, as ⌘W does. Arc shows ✕ there and turns it into − only on the selected row |
| Pressed | the selected fill at once, as Arc selects a row on the press; the plate shrinks (Motion › Row press). The token, white 0.065, never shows on a row (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×) |
| Audio | a speaker after the favicon while the page plays sound and for 2 s after (Chrome), crossed out while the tab is muted. A click on it mutes or unmutes the tab, as Mute/Unmute Tab in the command bar does. Arc mutes the tab (Arc 1.170.0 bin: `setWebContentsMuted`, `isTabMuted`); Dia mutes the site in the profile (P§2). Arc's is 17.5 wide with the title 8 after it (Arc 1.17x). Ours: Lucide's volume-2 and volume-x at 16 pt, white 0.6, in an 18 pt square with the ✕'s hover fill, 6.5 before the title, so the title is 8 after the glyph; the crossed-out speaker stays while the tab is muted, playing or not, so a muted tab always shows it; ⇧⌘T and Duplicate Tab keep a tab muted and a quit doesn't |
| Calls | a mark after the favicon, before the speaker, while the page captures (Chrome's media capture indicator, told as it changes): Lucide's screen-share while it shares a screen, a window or a tab, or another page shares it; else video while it uses the camera; else mic for the microphone alone. 16 pt in the speaker's 18 square, secondary text, 4 before the speaker or title. It goes once the capture ends, and VoiceOver reads it after the title, "using the camera", "using the microphone", "sharing", "being shared". Arc animates it (Arc 1.170 release notes); ours is still (*interim*), as a call would draw every frame. Arc's ⌃Tab switcher shows it too; ilo's ⌃Tab walks with no switcher. Arc also tints the shared tab's row yellow; ours marks it alone (*interim*). Agents can't move a share (Tab menu), as it would show a page to whoever the call reaches. A call floats while its tab is hidden, as Arc's does: Chrome's automatic Picture in Picture, for a page using the camera or microphone that asks for it through its media session, as Meet does, on https; showing the tab brings it back, and Site Controls' Picture in Picture switch turns it off for the site. Its window moves by its top bar and sizes by its edges, as Chrome's, where Arc's moves with a two-finger drag (*interim*, the owner's call, 2026-11-01): the page keeps its own input, as Meet's chat scrolls there, so neither a scroll nor a pinch nor a double-click in it moves, sizes or lands it, as they do the Mini Player. Arc's look: measure |
| Media player | a stack at the sidebar's foot, over the footer, of a card for each tab of the window's Spaces or favorites whose page has media Chrome can control (its media session) while no pane of the window shows it, playing or paused, as Arc's mini player and media tray: a window has the players of what plays in another, as Arc's, and a private window's tabs have theirs in it alone, as no other window lists them; an agent's Space's tab has one as any other the window lists. At rest only the newest card shows, 33 tall, 8 in from the sidebar's sides, its bottom where the list ends, and up to two older ones peek out behind it, each 7 higher, 5 narrower on each side and 0.2 fainter (Arc 1.171, an outside measurement, #700). The newest is the one whose player came last. The list ends 6 over the stack at rest (ours). Under the pointer every card grows to 65.5, 5 apart, the oldest on top, over 0.2 s on an ease-out both ways, closing as soon as the pointer leaves (Arc 1.171); ours: it opens once the pointer has rested on it 100 ms, as the reveal waits, and grows over the list's last rows, which don't move. Under Reduce Motion it opens and closes in one frame. A press on a card peeking out behind opens the stack and does nothing else (ours). A card's lower row, 33 tall, has its tab's favicon, which shows the tab (Back to Tab), the previous track, play or pause, the next track and Adjust Tab Volume, evenly spaced, the previous and next tracks dimmed with no plate under the pointer while the page has no handler for them, as Arc's. Adjust Tab Volume's glyph has full waves, one wave under half volume, or none at 0, as Arc's 3, 1 or none; ours is Lucide's speaker, with 2 full waves. It opens the volume drawer, and the stack with it: the card grows 33 with its bottom put, as Arc's, the drawer over the controls so they stay under the pointer (ours), over 0.2 s on an ease-out, in one frame under Reduce Motion (ours). A slider in it sets the tab's volume where it's pressed and follows a drag anywhere in the window. The volume scales the page's media elements, under the page's own volume and the mute, and stays across the tab's pages until the browser quits; Web Audio, calls and the Safari engine's pages keep theirs (*interim*). The drawer closes with the stack, or on a second click of its button. Open, a title row over it has the session's title in 12 pt (the tab's title without one) and its artist in secondary after a ·, cut with an ellipsis where Arc's scrolls, then 24 pt buttons 4.5 from the card's right and 2 apart: Picture in Picture while the page can float (its own handler or its one video, as Chrome's media controls offer it) and nothing of it floats, which floats it, as Arc's Back to PiP does without Arc's animation *interim*, and × to put it away until the page plays again, which folds and fades it over 0.2 s as the cards above close up (ours). A click elsewhere on a card shows its tab. Cards are opaque, a tile's fill over the window's colour, so one behind shows only its edge (ours). Agents have none: an agent plays and pauses in the page itself, and an agent's tab starts muted (#1080), and its volume, as its mute, is the user's |
| Loading | a 12 × 12 ring at width − 8 − 12, only in rows at least 48 wide, hidden under the hover's close button. The track and ring are 1.5 pt lines on a circle inset 1.25. The ring covers 72 % with round caps in the secondary label colour, the track is the same colour at 0.18, and it spins per Motion |
| Battery Saver | Chrome's (its Energy Saver), on below 20% on battery and off once plugged in or above, as Arc's, following the battery's own notices with no timer: IOKit's on the Mac, UPower's on Linux, Windows' power notices (#686). While it's on, pages draw at 30 frames a second, their wake-ups align, and a page busy out of sight freezes once Chrome has measured it, a minute at a time, and thaws as it's shown; Arc freezes a background tab that uses too much CPU, ilo only on Battery Saver, as Chrome does (#686). As it turns on, the toast in the window in front says "Battery Saver is on at 19%" with Turn Off, which turns it off until the computer's plugged in and says "Battery Saver is off until you plug in"; the command bar has Turn Off Battery Saver for Now while it's on. While it's on, Lucide's leaf sits among the URL field's buttons, before Copy URL, in the Space's accent, and stays as the tools fade; a press shows the toast again. A frozen tab's hover card has Lucide's pause and "Paused to save battery" in the host's place, keeping the picture it had, as a new one would thaw the page; its row has no mark (ours). Settings › Tabs & Sidebar's Battery Saver, on, Chrome's default: off, it goes at once and never comes on. Agents hear none of it |
| Sleeping tab | favicon at half opacity while unloaded (*interim*; Arc's alpha: measure) |
| Unloading | a tab unseen for 2 hours lets go of its page as another is shown, while Settings' Unload tabs you haven't looked at is on, and each time the system says memory is short the one unseen longest does, whatever its age (Arc frees memory when the Mac runs low); it loads again once shown. Never one in a call: using the camera or microphone, sharing a screen, a window or a tab, shared itself, or holding a WebRTC connection, even one that takes nothing and makes no sound (Arc never puts a tab using the microphone or recording to sleep, #686). Nor one playing sound or lately, asking before it's left, with typed form text, a device connected, DevTools open, shown or pictured, private, or an agent's (core/idle_unload.cc, Chrome's discard checks) |
| "+ New Tab" row | the first row under the divider, where new tabs open below it (Arc). Label white 0.55 (secondary), plus sign white 0.25 (tertiary); hover as rows (Dia) |
| New Tab tab's icon | our mark, 16 wide, white 0.28 in dark mode over any row state |

- Ours: titles are Dia's, white for the selected row and 0.78 for the others, since Arc's aren't measured. The close button's square is the subtle fill, white 0.12, at the header buttons' radius 6. A held row has the selected fill before the core answers its press; a dragged row doesn't. An unloaded pinned row has no page to close, so under the pointer it shows a remove button in the −'s place and plate: Lucide's trash, 14 pt, in the −'s colour, with the tooltip Remove Tab. A click takes the tab, with its split, out of the pinned tabs into the archive, not into Today, as Unpin and then ⌘W would, and ⌘Z and ⇧⌘T pin it again at its place, in its folder too (owner, 2026-11-02, #1349). On a row whose − or middle press just unloaded it, the button waits until the pointer has left the row, so a second click meant for the − never removes the tab. Arc's unloaded row shows a ✕ whose click isn't recorded.

**Behavior**

- **Showing a tab** (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×, two presses). A press on a row shows its tab: the page and the row's selected fill change in the frame its plate starts to shrink, before the release. The split row pressed at 144.562 s showed its pages in that frame and was let go at 144.870; Import Logins pressed at 146.187 s showed its page in that frame. A press that becomes a drag has shown its tab already (all the recording's drags were of the tab shown). Ours: a split view's segment shows its page focused on the press too. The press gives the page the keyboard, from the UI too, as a tab clicked in Chrome's tab strip does (Arc's not recorded), and so does a press on the row already shown and a click on a favorite's tile; a page still loading takes it as one shown from a focused page does, unless a click in the UI comes first. The window's other presses in the sidebar keep the keyboard in the UI. ✕, − and the speaker act on their own click, and a press on them neither shows the tab nor starts a drag. An away pinned tab's favicon shows the tab on its click (› Pinned tabs). A right-click, ⌃-click included, shows the row's menu without showing its tab. A press with ⌘ or ⇧ chooses rows instead (› Other row actions), and neither shows the tab nor starts a drag; ⌥ changes nothing yet (*interim*: the recording has none).
- **⌘T's command bar** (Arc rec 120 fps). While it's open, "+ New Tab" has the selected plate, and the tab shown loses it and its selected title, in one frame. A pick, Esc or a click outside the bar gives it back, in one frame. ⌘L's bar doesn't move the plate.
- **Opening a tab** (Arc rec 120 fps). The new row appears in its slot at once, already selected, and its plate fades in (Row motion). The old selection drops at once. Rows below slide down one pitch (Row motion), drawn under the new row and revealed from beneath its bottom edge (Dia).
- **Closing a tab** (Arc rec 120 fps). Selection moves to the neighbour at once. The closed row drops its selected fill and stays in place, fading as the rows below slide up over it (Row motion), all starting at the same moment. Hover follows the final layout at once. Several closes fade together.
- **Tabs coming back.** When several rows come at once, as after Undo, they fade in in place (Arc rec) and the rows below them slide.
- **Hover card** for rows and tiles (Dia bin). It is a child window that scales from its top-left corner. In and out per Motion; both start from the current values, so a leaving card comes back from where it is.
  - It shows after 0.5 s, or 0.1 s from one hover source that wasn't identified. It shows at once when another card is already up.
  - Moving from row to row moves the card without animation.
  - A click or key press dismisses it. The leave delay is unknown (Ardia uses 160 ms). It takes no clicks until it has faded in.
  - Actions (Dia): Pin, Bookmark, Split, Picture in Picture, and Reset for a pinned tab.
- **Scroll edges** (Dia 1.53.0 bin; Arc 1.170). A 1 pt full-width separator sits 2 over the scrolled rows' top, under the Space's name row; Dia's list starts 5 under its favorites with a 1 pt top inset, its separator 4 under. The separator fades in (Scroll edge curve) once the list scrolls past its top and fades out at the top or while rubber-banding. Another, 1 under the list's foot, shows while rows are hidden under it and goes once the last row is in view (Arc). After its last row the list has room, so at full scroll that row's plate ends 121 above the foot; the room doesn't bring the foot's line (Arc 1.170).
- **Only the tabs scroll** (Arc 1.170). The favorites and the Space's name row stay put, and the pinned and Today rows scroll under the name row, whose right-click and press stay its own wherever the rows are. Ours: while the favorites are tall enough to scroll with the list (Favorites), the name row goes with them, as the top of what scrolls.
- **The scroller** (Arc 1.170, which follows the system's Show scroll bars, as AppKit does). While the system shows scroll bars only as they're used, a knob 4 wide, 3 in from the sidebar's edge, in the ink at 0.49 (white 49 % on a dark theme), with no track, shows on the rows' part as the user scrolls them, and fades once they've stopped. With Show scroll bars: Always, a knob 5 wide, in the ink at 0.5, 3 in from the edge and 1 inside its rect, which is 3 in from the rows' part's top and bottom, stays while the rows overflow, and the rows give up 8 at the sidebar's edge for it, their plates ending 16 from it. The knob is as long against its track as the rows in view against all of them, the room after the last row included. Ours (*interim*, Arc's aren't measured): the knob holds 0.8 s after the last scroll and fades over 0.25 s; it's at least 18 long; the overlay knob's ends are the legacy rect's, 3 in; a glide to a row (List scrolls to a row) brings no knob, as AppKit's programmatic scrolls bring none; the setting is read at launch and as the app comes back from another, such as System Settings, so a mouse plugged in while ilo is in front changes it at the next; the knob doesn't drag and its track takes no click, so the wheel and the fingers scroll the list; the tiles keep their width.
- **Shown tab marker** (Arc's release notes; the look is ours). While the selected tab's row is scrolled wholly out past the list's top or bottom, as the list scrolls too, a 4 pt sliver of its plate, in the selected fill and rounded, lies along that edge across the plate's width. A click within 14 pt of the edge there scrolls the list the least that shows the row whole (List scrolls to a row), and the sliver goes. A tab with no row, a favorite, has none. Arc's look and motion: measure.
- **Reorder** (Arc 1.170 rec). A ghost of the dragged row follows the pointer within a frame and draws over the rows it passes. The row stays in its place, and nothing else in the list moves while the drag lasts: a line marks the gap it would drop into. On drop the old place empties, and the rows glide to their new places on Row motion, 41 pt in about 115 ms.
  - The drop line is 2 pt, white 0.9 (Arc's (232, 233, 249) over the dark Space), and starts with a ring 8 across, drawn in the same 2 pt. The ring's left is 7 in from the plate's, and the line ends 2 before the plate's end. It sits halfway between the plates on either side of the gap, as far in as the row would go.
  - Ours: a row comes away once the pointer has moved 4 pt, and a drag never shows its tab. The ghost follows the pointer up and down only, within the list's first and last places, and the line marks the gap nearest where the ghost is drawn. Arc's rule for the gap isn't settled: in the frame measured, its line was under the row its ghost covered. No line shows while the gap is where the row was. Above the divider it pins the tab and below it unpins it; a tab pinned this way is pinned at its page. Letting go anywhere drops it at its place, and the row lands from the ghost; escape, or letting go where it started, puts it back. Let go in the list with ⌥ held, a tab's row leaves a copy of the tab there, which shows, as Duplicate's does, and goes back to its place; one step for ⌘Z, which closes the copy. Not a folder, a split view or a favorite, and not into another Space (Arc does it from the release notes' inventory, #690; ours, *interim*: when Arc reads ⌥, and whether its copy shows, aren't measured). The trackpad clicks each time the place it would drop at changes, passing a row, crossing the divider or the favorites' edge, or going in or out of a folder, and not as it comes away, holds, drops or is put back (Arc; owner's observation, 2026-10-26). It's the Space swipe's detent, so it follows the same setting. The ghost shows its row's hover fill, made opaque, and the other rows none.
- **Dragging to another Space** (Arc's help centre: a tab dragged to the sidebar's edge pages to the next Space). A row held within 24 pt of the sidebar's left or right edge for 0.5 s pages to the user's Space on that side, with a detent, and on to the next after another 0.5 s there; the ghost stays with the pointer. Over that Space it drops where Move to puts a tab: the drop line marks the bottom of its pinned tabs or the top of its Today, whichever is nearer, a folder's only the bottom of its pinned tabs, and let go it moves there through Move to's core call, with its undo and the one-time warning for another profile's Space. Paged back to its own Space, it drops as any drag does. Only what Move to takes pages: a tab or a folder, which goes whole (owner, 2026-11-02, #1353), not a favorite, a split view or links dragged in, and never into an agent's Space, which no window lists (Agent Space). Ours, *interim*: the edge's width and the wait, and the places in the other Space, since Arc's aren't measured.
- **Dragging out of the list.** A tab dragged onto another window's list lights that list up and moves the live page there. Dropped on the desktop, it opens a new window under the pointer. Esc cancels a sidebar drag.
  - Ours (#1798): a tab's row dragged past its window's edge leaves as the system's drag, showing the row as it's drawn, and goes back to its place in the window. Over another window's list the rows mark where it would go, as for links dropped there. Let go there, it moves to that place, into that window's Space if it was in another, as Move to moves a tab, and shows there, its page moving live, as a click on its row there does. Into another profile's Space, where its page loads again as a new tab, it asks first with Move to's one-time warning and goes where Move to puts a tab. Let go anywhere else, it opens a window as ⌘N does, as big as the one it left, with its row under the pointer where it was held, kept on a screen, and shows there, its page moving live. Under Wayland no app picks where its window opens, so the compositor places that window. Either way the window it left shows the tab used before it, as when a tab closes. Esc cancels it, with nothing changed. ⌘Z in the window it went to puts it back where it was and shows it in the window it came from, and closes a window the drag opened, which holds nothing else, as every window shows the same Spaces.
  - Only a tab's row of a regular window: not a folder, a split view or a favorite, and not into or out of a private or a Little ilo window, whose Space is its own. An agent's tab has no row in the user's windows (Agent Space), and agents have no path that moves a tab between windows, so there's no rule for one.
  - Ours, *interim*: Arc's picture of a window as it leaves, and its timings (Drag targets › What the drag looks like), aren't built. It's built on the Mac, Windows and Linux. On the Mac and Windows the drag shows the row as the window draws it, held where it was pressed; on Linux it shows no picture of the row yet, only the pointer's.
- **Dropping onto the tab list.** Files and http(s) links dropped on the list open as tabs at the drop gap (Dia): the gap nearest the pointer, where the line shows, so under a row once the pointer is past its middle (Arc). A file opens in the page area like any tab, not in a split (owner, 2026-10-31). Dropped text that isn't a link opens a search for it with the default engine there, its spaces and lines run together, even when it names a site, as `example.com` does (Arc's release notes). Other links, such as `javascript:`, `data:`, `file:` and `mailto:` ones from dragged text, never open and aren't searched for (Ardia).
  - Files and folders in the sidebar (Arc: Finder's files and folders added to the sidebar, folders expanding in place, a file dragged onto a website to upload it; owner, 2026-10-31). A dropped folder becomes one of the sidebar's folders (Pinned tabs › Folders), named for it and open, at the drop gap among the pinned tabs, else at their bottom, as folders live there; files dropped with it open at the gap first. It holds its folders, closed, then a tab for each of its files, each by name, case aside, hidden ones left out, so it expands in place, moves, renames and is saved with the Space as any folder is. Its tabs load once shown, and none shows as it comes in. It's what the folder held then, not a live view of it. It brings in at most 100 files and folders, its own first, then its folders' in turn, and 8 folders deep (ours, *interim*: Arc's limits aren't measured), and is read away from the main thread.
  - A file's row, a tab showing a file on the disk alone in its row, dragged past the sidebar onto the page hands the file to the system's drag, as a download's row does (Library › Downloads), so the page can take it as an upload, in place of the split targets a row gets there; the row goes back to its place. Dropped where nothing takes it, nothing happens.
  - The +'s Add File or Folder… (ours, as Arc adds both from its +) asks the system's panel for files and folders: picked files open as tabs at the top of Today, the first shown, as new tabs do, and picked folders come in as dropped ones, at the bottom of the pinned tabs. Off the Mac the panel picks files or folders, not both, so the menu has Add Files… and Add Folder…. A private window's has neither.
  - Agents get none of this: an agent opens no page outside http(s) and reads no file (principle 3), so a file's tab or a folder from the disk has no agent path.
  - Ours (*interim* until Arc's is recorded): over the list, the drop line marks the place nearest the pointer, as for a dragged row, in the pinned tabs or Today, and in a folder when the row under the place is in it. The system draws what's dragged. Let go, what it carries opens in its order, the first in that place and shown, the others under it, loading once shown, and in the pinned tabs they're pinned at their URLs. A file's page with no title is titled by the file's name, as in Chrome's tab strip. Over the page, the page takes the drop, as in Chrome. Nothing takes one while the sidebar is hidden or the Library is open.
- **Opening rules.** Links opened behind line up after their opener in the order opened. A link opened in front goes right after its opener. Closing a page goes to a tab it opened, then a sibling, then the opener (Chrome). The Space forgets which tabs opened which when Chrome's tab strip does: when a link opens in front, before it records that tab's opener; when the user shows a tab that isn't the shown tab's opener, a tab it opened or a sibling; and when a tab goes somewhere typed or bookmarked rather than by a link. Opening behind forgets nothing, so a run of tabs opened behind keeps lining up. Tabs opened behind load only when first shown; until then the row shows the address and the site's icon (Dia). The list scrolls the least that shows a row whole: a tab opened behind's (Dia), the shown tab's, however it was shown (Chrome's tab strip), and "+ New Tab"'s as ⌘T's bar opens, so from the list's bottom it ends at the top (Arc 1.171). It glides there (Motion › List to a row).
- **Other row actions** (Dia). Double-click a row to rename it inline. Every name field in the sidebar, a tab's, a folder's, the Space's and the New Space form's profile, and Settings' profile name, ends once it loses the keyboard, not only at ↩ or Esc: at a click elsewhere in its window or in the page, as the command bar or another overlay opens, or for another window or app. A click away that closes the field's own menu ends it too, though the click goes no further, as one outside any menu doesn't; a click on the field or Esc in the menu leaves it open. Ending so keeps what's typed, a composition included, as ↩ does, an empty field changes nothing, and Esc still cancels; a pinned tab's Edit… field is undone instead, as a URL is only taken on purpose (owner, 2026-11-02, #1414). Change its icon to an emoji. ⌘-click and ⇧-click select several rows for bulk actions. Ours so far: ⌘-click chooses a row, with the one shown, or lets it go, and ⇧-click chooses the rows from the one ⌘-clicked last, else the one shown, through it; a pinned tab or folder that isn't in a closed folder, or a Today row, can be chosen, a split as one. A chosen folder stands for itself and what's in it: a row chosen in it goes with it, ⇧⌘C copies its tabs' links, and Add to Folder ›, New Folder and Move to Space move it whole, while ⌘W and ⌘D, which are a tab's, leave it be, and the menu's Close Tabs and Pin Tabs are dimmed with only folders chosen (ours, #1353). The rows chosen besides the one shown take the secondary fill. ⌘W closes them all, as one step for ⌘Z, and ⇧⌘C copies their links, one a line, as Arc's Copy URLs does; a plain click, Esc or another Space lets them go. ⌘D pins them, the Today ones if any, else unpins them all, and New Folder pins them into a new folder at the bottom of the pinned tabs and names it, as Arc's does, each keeping their order. A right-click on a chosen row shows their menu (ours, *interim* until Arc's is captured): Pin Tabs or Unpin Tabs (⌘D), Add to Folder › (Pinned tabs › Folders), Move to Space with the other Spaces of the profile, then Copy URLs (⇧⌘C) and Close Tabs (⌘W); a private window's has Copy URLs and Close Tabs alone. A right-click on another row lets them go and shows that row's menu. Each of these is one step for ⌘Z, which puts every row back in its place. The context menu shows shortcut hints. A haptic tick sounds while reordering, as a setting.
  - Arc's, for a Today tab (Arc 1.170.0 ax): Copy Link (⇧⌘C), Share, then Change Icon…, Rename…, Mute and Customize Page…, then Add Split View, Duplicate and Move to (its Spaces, then New Space…), then Archive Tab and Archive Tabs Below. A pinned tab's isn't captured.
  - Ours, for a Today tab: Arc's, with Customize Page… as New Boost…, or Edit Boost… once the site has one, where its page can have a Boost (Boosts › Making and editing), with Remove Icon after Change Icon… once the tab has one (Spaces › Icon) and Separate from Split View after Add Split View in a split. Mute reads Unmute while the tab is muted. Share This Tab Instead follows Mute and the Boost while a page shares another tab, on each tab Chrome's infobar would offer it, and moves the share to this tab (Arc 1.170 help centre; Chrome's Share this tab instead). Add to Folder › (Pinned tabs › Folders) comes before Move to, as ours. Copy Link copies the link as ⇧⌘C does, the only item with a key chip, as in Arc. Holding ⌥ turns it into Copy Link as Markdown (⌥⇧⌘C), in its place, as AppKit's alternates are (owner, 2026-10-29); ilo's menu shows an item's alternate while ⌥ is held from the press that opened it or later, and a choice then is the alternate. Share lists the system's share services for the link, dimmed with none. Add Split View splits the tab shown with this tab, showing it first if it isn't in the split shown. Move to lists the user's Spaces, the tab's checked, and New Space… makes a Space with the New Space form and moves the tab into it once it's made. A Space with folders is a submenu of Today, the Space as before, and its folders, each named by its path ("Docs / Old"), and a folder chosen takes the tab at its top, one step to undo (Arc reaches a folder in any Space; the submenu and the paths are ours). Another profile's Space lists no folders, as the tab goes there as a new tab, and Agent Spaces stay off the list. Archive Tab and Archive Tabs Below archive as Close Tab and Close Tabs Below did. Pin Tab and Add to Favorites aren't in it, as in Arc's: the row dragged onto the pinned tabs or the favorites does each. New Boost… opens the Boost's card, showing the tab first if it isn't shown. Copy Link, Share and the Boost have no agent path: the clipboard and the share services are the user's, outside the browser, and a Boost is the user's view of their pages (Boosts).
  - Ours, for a pinned tab (*interim* until Arc's is captured): Unpin Tab (⌘D), Add to Favorites, Back to Pinned URL (⌘↩, away from its URL), Pinned URL › Replace Pinned URL with Current and Edit… (Arc's, Pinned tabs above), Rename Tab, Change Icon…, Remove Icon (once it has one, Spaces › Icon), Duplicate Tab, Add to Folder › (Pinned tabs › Folders), Move to Space with the other Spaces in the switcher's order, then Close Tab (⌘W). The row keeps its hover fill while the menu is up, and a menu from the reveal's panel opens at the pointer there. Duplicate copies the tab with its history under it, or at the top of Today for a pinned tab, and shows it. Rename, or a double-click on the row, turns the title into a field with all of it selected, as for a Space's name: ↩ keeps what's typed and Esc cancels, and losing the keyboard ends it (Other row actions). The name shows in place of the page's title wherever the tab's title does, the archive too, and stays through a quit, ⇧⌘T and Duplicate. Kept empty with ↩, the tab shows its page's title again, and left as it was, nothing changes. Change Icon… opens the icon picker (Spaces › Icon) under the tab's favicon, its arrow at the favicon's middle, in from the row by the folders the tab is in. It waits until the row has stopped moving, as a Space's does, and docks the sidebar if only the reveal shows it. Only its Emoji tab is offered, with the search, the recents and the grid shared with a Space's, since a tab's icon is an emoji its row draws in the favicon's place; the Icon tab's glyphs are a Space's and the tab row and core have none, so the control is Emoji alone and the trash after it. The tab's emoji is on the plate. A click on an emoji, or a search and ↩, is the icon at once; the trash gives the favicon back, as Remove Icon does, and Esc closes with no change. The emoji draws at 13 pt, its glyph the favicon's 16, wherever the sidebar shows the favicon, and stays as a name does. Remove Icon, in a favorite's menu too, gives the favicon back. Dia's picker and Arc's menu: measure. Archive Tabs Below shows the right-clicked tab first if one below was shown, so none loads on the way out, and ⇧⌘T reopens them one at a time. Move to Space keeps a pinned tab pinned, at the bottom of the other Space's pinned tabs, and Move to puts a Today tab at the top of its Today. A window showing the tab goes with it, as Chrome's active tab goes to the window it's dragged to, and the old Space shows next what it would once the tab closed.
- **Hiding and showing** (Arc layout, Arc 1.170.0 rec).
  - ⌘S or the sidebar button hides the docked sidebar (Sidebar hide). The sidebar and the card's left edge leave together, and the page is laid out at every step. Hidden, the card fills the window with no traffic lights, header or nav.
  - ⌘S shows it again (Sidebar show). The window's buttons come back once it's at rest, so they never sit over the moving card.
  - The sidebar button's plate is at x 77, as Arc's (its glyph is ours). The View menu has Toggle Sidebar ⌘S.
  - ⌘S isn't reserved: a page that keeps it, as an editor's save does, keeps it (Chrome's rule for Save Page).
- **Sidebar reveal** (Arc (Ardia), Arc 1.170.0 rec).
  - The pointer kept at the window's left edge, at any height including the top-left corner, slides a floating panel over the page (Sidebar reveal in). The page doesn't move. A pointer passing the edge, on its way to another window or display, brings nothing out, and neither does one that jumps into the edge and stays: it comes out once the pointer has moved a few points up or down there (Arc 1.171; ours, *interim*: 3 pt, since Arc's distance isn't measured). Only the key window's edge does, so with two windows one behind leaves its edge to the pointer (Arc 1.171); ours: the window last made key keeps it while another app is in front.
  - The panel is inset 5 from the window's left, top and bottom edges and as wide as the docked sidebar. It's dark, at radius 12, with a 0.5 pt light border and no shadow; in a capture at 1× the border is one pixel of white 0.27 over the panel (Arc 1.170.0 cap). It holds the traffic lights, the header buttons, the URL field and the tabs.
  - It stays while the pointer is within 24 pt of its edge (Arc ≈ 21), then slides away (Sidebar reveal out).
  - Docking from the panel is instant: the page is laid out narrower in one frame.
  - Pressing ⌥ shows the hidden sidebar, and Settings can turn that off (Arc's release notes). Ours (*interim*: Arc's trigger isn't recorded): ⌥ pressed and let go alone, with the page or the UI holding the keyboard, slides the panel out as the edge does, and a key, another modifier, a press or a scroll before it's let go makes it part of a shortcut, a typed character or an ⌥-click, which bring nothing out. The panel stays out wherever the pointer is until the pointer has come over it and left, as from the edge, or ⌥ is tapped again. Settings › Tabs & Sidebar has the switch, on at first: "Show the hidden sidebar when ⌥ is pressed alone". Agents get none, as it moves only the user's view.
  - Ours (*interim* until the Arc capture): the trigger is the card's left margin, x < 10. The border is white 0.54, which gives that pixel at 1×; a 1 pt border of 0.27 would too, and only a capture at 2× tells them apart. The panel draws its own traffic lights, since AppKit's stay in the browser window under it.
  - Ours: the panel never takes the keyboard, so renaming a Space or making one from it docks the sidebar first. The command bar opening, page full screen and the app going to the background send it away.

**⌃Tab** (Arc 1.170.0 rec, sessions 10–12; Dia 1.50.1 bin, P§2)

- Arc: ⌃Tab shows the tab used last at once, in one frame, with no switcher, whether ⌃ goes up at once or is held. With no tab shown, it shows the tab used last.
- Dia: a switcher of recent tabs, most recent first, that appears after 140 ms (Ardia; source unclear) and switches when ⌃ goes up. → and ← move. Esc or a click outside cancels. Clicking a row switches to it, and the pointer highlights rows. Leaving the app switches. Other keys are swallowed while it's up.
- Ours: Arc's, among the tabs of the Space shown. While ⌃ stays down, each ⌃Tab goes one tab further back in the order they were shown, and ⌃⇧Tab one forward, so holding ⌃ reaches past the last two. ⌃ going up, a key or a click without it, or the app going to the background ends the walk, and only the tab it ends on counts as shown. Tabs not shown since launch come last, in sidebar order. Arc's walk with ⌃ held isn't recorded.

### Spaces

**Model** (decision 5)

- A Space has a name, a colour, its pinned tabs, its Today tabs and a profile.
- Several Spaces can share a profile. Switching to a Space of another profile switches the window's cookies, logins and extensions with it.
- Moving a tab to a Space of another profile reloads it in that profile, after a one-time warning that the tab loses its session (Dia warns when moving a tab to another profile, P§1). Its back and forward history, name and mute go with it, and it loads once it shows; the old tab closes without going to the archive or ⇧⌘T, since it lives on in the new one. *Interim*: the warning is our in-window dialog with Move and Cancel, as Dia's wasn't captured, and nothing turns it back on once it's been confirmed.
- Favorites follow Settings › Tabs & Sidebar › Share favorites (Scope, under Favorites): by default every Space of a profile shows the same ones (Arc, to confirm in the capture).

**Footer and switcher** (Arc 1.170.0 cap and ax)

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Footer | 50 tall. Home, our Library button, 32 × 32 at 8 in on the left, with the ladder's white 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed. A + 32 × 32 at 8 in on the right, its glyph a 14 pt cross in white 0.60, with the header's hover plate. Ours: the +'s menu has New Space…, New Folder… and New Split View (Arc makes a split from it), Arc's menus' names, as Arc's + menu isn't recorded, then New Note, which Arc's help centre puts there (Profiles › New Note), then Arc's New Boost, as New Boost… or Edit Boost… for the page shown where it can have a Boost (Boosts › Making and editing), then a line and Add File or Folder… (Dropping onto the tab list) |
| Switcher | between them, 42 to 186 in a 228 sidebar (ours: 42 in from each side at any width). One item per Space, 36 × 34 at a 34 pt pitch, centred; none with one Space |
| Dot | 8 pt, white 0.50 for the Space shown and 0.19 for the others, 0.30 under the pointer (ours); the Space shown's is in its colour, in a 1.5 pt ring of #F3F0E8, when it has one, as the status dots are (#321) |
| Click | shows that Space |
| Drag | ours, *interim* (Arc's isn't recorded; #1580): a dot pressed and moved comes away, its copy following the pointer and its place dimmed to half, and the rows' drop line stood up, 2 wide and 16 tall on the dots' middle in white 0.9, without its ring, which would be a dot's size, stands in the gap it would go into, never where it was. Let go over the footer, the sidebar's width and 50 tall, the Space goes there in every window, the switcher, the Spaces menu, ⌃1–⌃9 and the Library, saved with the Spaces, and the dots glide to their places on Row motion, the one let go from where its copy was; off the footer, or after Esc, nothing moves, and a click still shows the Space. ⌘Z puts it back. Agents get no path: the order is the user's, and an agent works in its own Space |
| More Spaces than fit | the cells share the span in half points, at most 34 each, and nothing scrolls; once a share would be under 22, the Space shown keeps 22 and the others share the rest: 17.5, 13 and 9 with 12, 16 and 22 Spaces in a sidebar 300 wide (Arc 1.171 ax). Ours: each share is rounded to the nearest half point of our span, which gives Arc's three; a squeezed item's ink keeps 4 clear of its neighbours and never goes under 4 (the cell less 1 below 5), a dot under the pointer growing 2 past its squeezed size, a glyph squeezed by its ink; the widths change at once on a switch, as the switch does.
