Dia: 12 pt per item, at most 7 before it scrolls, edge items scaled 0.75–1 (Dia 1.50.1 bin, D›Profile paging) |

Arc shows Space icons in place of dots when a Space has one: measure both looks. Ours (*interim*): a Space's icon (Icon) takes the dot's place, so the strip doesn't move. A glyph is drawn in a 16 pt box, 13 pt of ink: the Space shown's in its accent, at 3:1 on the footer in light and dark, or the ink with no colour; the others white 0.19, 0.30 under the pointer. An emoji is 13 pt text, as a tab's, the Space shown's at full strength and the others at the dots' ratio, 0.38, 0.60 under the pointer. The right-click menu and a click work as on the dot. Spaces with no icon keep their dots, mixed in one strip.

**Switching**

- Click a dot, press ⌃1–⌃9, swipe horizontally with two fingers over the sidebar, or press a mouse's back or forward side button over it.
- A mouse's side buttons (3 and 4, Arc's numbering; X's 8 and 9) over the sidebar, or anywhere else in the UI, show the previous and next Space, as Arc's do, never past the first or last (decided by the owner, 2026-10-29). Over the page, the page takes them and goes back and forward, as in Chrome.
- A Space shows the tab it showed last, not the top of its Today (Arc; decided by the owner, 2026-10-29). The core keeps each Space's selected tab, so a window, the command bar's Focus and an agent's switch all come back to it.
- Paging is Dia's (bin):
  - Tracking moves the pages at half the scroll distance (−0.5 × the horizontal delta), whatever the system's "Swipe between pages" setting says. Magic Mouse one-finger swipes count too. Ardia tolerates 12 pt of vertical wobble, then locks to horizontal.
  - Past the first or last Space, the pages rubber-band: `255·(1 − 1/(0.15·x/255 + 1))`.
- Past the first Space is the Library (Arc's release notes, June 2022: "Swipe all the way left to get to Library"). A 2023 video (Arc 1.x, 25 fps) shows a wheel's scroll there playing the Library's open, with the Space's rows gone within a frame.
  - Fingers going right on the first Space move the Library as its open moves it, the card at the fingers' way over the Library's width. The sidebar recedes under it with them: its rows, fields, footer and the window's buttons fade out, gone by 0.48 of the way, and shrink toward a point 128 right of its edge at the window's middle, so the top goes down and right and the bottom up and right (Library recede). The Library is one sheet, its column and what its section holds together, coming from its width to the left at the card's pace, so its right side comes first, and it fades in over the sidebar as the sidebar fades out, never cutting it (Library recede; Arc 1.170 rec at 2×: Spaces with one Space and with three, and Media; Downloads and Archived Tabs follow them, *interim*). The Library follows the card after the fingers let go too, and back the same way. Ours: the detent sounds at halfway. Let go, the Library opens on a flick toward it or past halfway, else goes back, on its spring from the fingers' speed. In the Library, fingers going left bring back the first Space the same way, as the Library sits left of it, though it was opened from another Space (Arc 1.170 rec, three Spaces), and so does a wheel's tick (ours). Going right, past where it opens, they take the card on a little, about as far as the pages' band on a short pull and half as far on a long one, and the Library with it (Library end), with the column's fill reaching back to the window's left edge and the window's buttons on it, and both spring back as the Library's push does when they let go. A wheel's tick opens it from the first Space and closes it from the Library. Fingers catch the Library while it moves. With the sidebar hidden, the reveal's panel rubber-bands as before.
  - A swipe from the second Space stops at the first, however far the fingers go, and the Library takes the next swipe from there (the owner: it's the only way one Space left, so it never overshoots). An Arc 1.170 recording, three Spaces, 245.3–246.8 s, was read as one swipe carrying on into the Library, with a 0.26 s rest on the first Space; the owner reads it as two.
- Past the last Space, a pull makes a new Space (Arc 1.170, recorded at 1× and 2×, three pulls; sizes in pt).
  - The Space's title and tabs move with the rubber band; the URL field and the favorites stay. A circle 30 across comes out from under the page's left edge, halfway down. Its centre starts 20 right of the edge and moves 3.1× as fast as the page, stopping 27 left of the edge once the page is 15 out; the page goes on.
  - It's a disc of the Space's colour with a white + (13 across, 1.5 thick), a soft shadow, and a white ring 2.5 thick on its edge, with a 0.5 line of the sidebar's colour inside it. The ring grows anticlockwise from 12 o'clock with round ends, `out / 22` of the way, and the white is tinted with 9% of the colour.
  - A white glow comes out with the circle: a cone centred on the edge halfway down, 80 wide and half the window tall, fading linearly from 0.18. It slides out from under the edge as the circle does.
  - At 22.7 out (the fingers 333 across) the circle readies a new Space, and the trackpad clicks. The + goes, the white closes in on the colour to a dot 13 across over 60 ms, and the dot shrinks into a + of the colour over 70 ms more, as the circle pops to 1.13× and back from 40 ms over 120 ms. Pulled back under 22.7, it un-readies at once and clicks again: the colour grows from the middle over 120 ms, the white + on it, and the white thins back to the ring. No other moment clicks.
  - Let go readied, the Space is made, "Space N", with no icon: the Create a Space form and the new colour show within a frame, with no slide, and the circle and its glow go back under the edge over 100 ms, ease-in-out. Let go before, the page springs back, critically damped with a 0.18 s response, and the circle goes with it.
  - Ours: the circle is the Space's accent with its label colour (`app/src/palette.rs`), ilo green for none. The glow is drawn in 4 pt strips across, as GPUI has no radial gradient, and the ring's round ends are dots.
  - Release velocity comes from the last 100 ms of samples. At |velocity| ≥ 5 pt/s the target is the next Space in that direction, otherwise the nearest. A swipe moves at most one Space from where it began. The settle carries the release velocity when it points at the target.
  - Settle per Motion.
  - Wheel mice: deltas sum while they come less than 0.049 s apart. Once |sum| > 1 pt, it pages one Space and then ignores the wheel for 0.25 s.
- Ours, where Dia and Arc aren't measured:
  - The first move decides the axis: across if it's more across than along. Momentum after a page swipe is ignored, and a wheel tick counts as 10 pt, so one tick pages.
  - A Space's page is its URL field, its favorites and its rows, and they page together, at its offset on every frame, on a swipe, a dot click, ⌃1–⌃9, ⌥⌘←/→ and a wheel's page (the owner, from use). The window's buttons, the footer with the switcher and the window's colour stay out of the pages. Past the last Space the URL field and the favorites stay (above). Ours: while the pages slide, after a swipe's fingers lift too, a press on the URL field, a favorite or a row acts on what rests under the pointer, and its release clicks that, so a click does what it does once they settle; a tile or row moving to its place is the same.
  - The window shows the new Space when the fingers lift, not while they move. Until then the page beside shows that Space as it will show, and takes no input: its URL field with the URL of the tab it would show, or the empty field's look, its favorites, which are the same tiles when the two Spaces share a profile, and its rows with that tab selected. Its field shows Copy URL and Site Controls while the shown one does, but not the pinned extensions, which are its profile's.
  - A dot click, ⌃1–⌃9 and ⌥⌘←/→ slide the new Space's page in from its side on the Space settle curve, pushing the old one out.
  - The trackpad clicks with the fingers down, since a haptic is felt only with a finger on the trackpad: each time where letting go would take the pages changes by the release's rule above. So it clicks as a swipe goes for the next Space, from its second move, as it goes for the Library from the first Space, and again as either turns back; past the last Space, only the pull's clicks. Nothing clicks at the lift or in the momentum after (owner, #1075: a detent at halfway was never felt, as most swipes are let go short of it). The detent is AppKit's level change haptic, since its alignment haptic was too faint to feel (#1075). Swipes don't page while a rename or a row drag is up, or while the Create a Space form is up in a Space not shown.
  - On the Create a Space form, fingers going back page to the Space it was made from, as to the Space before, whichever Space that is. The form goes as a page does, with the page of the Space beside as above, never held back until the form is gone (the owner, from use). Let go on a flick back or past halfway, it's Cancel: the form's page goes out as that Space settles, and the new Space is deleted once it's out, so the window's colour crosses from its own. Let go short, the form settles back. Going on, the form rubber-bands and readies no new Space. A wheel's tick back is Cancel, with its slide. Arc's swipe off its form isn't recorded.
- The window's colour changes across the whole window at once, with no sweep (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×). During a swipe it's as much of each Space's colour as its page shows (Ardia). A switch to the Space next door changes it exponentially, τ ≈ 30 ms, well ahead of the slide; a longer jump changes it with the slide, as Arc's fits. Motion › Space colour.
  - Ours (*interim*): the gradient is as much of each Space's colour as its page shows, during a swipe and while a switch's pages slide.
- Measure in Arc: the Space title flow, the icon picker's Emoji tab, hover and motion, the ••• and Done's look, the menus the form's Profile and Choose a Theme rows open, and how a Space shows its profile.

**Creating** (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×, one Space named and made)

- New Space, from the menu or a pull past the last Space, shows the Create a Space form in the sidebar. Arc's has an illustration, a title and a subtitle, the name field, Profile and Choose a Theme rows, and at the bottom Create Space and Cancel. Ours has the title "New Space", the field and the two rows, as the illustration and the copy are Arc's.
- The Space goes right after the Space shown, in the switcher, the Spaces menu, ⌃1–⌃9 and the Library, as Arc's does (#688), not after the others. Cancel takes it out again, and ⌘Z on a deleted Space puts it back at its place in the order. The order is the core's, saved with the Spaces, and a dot dragged along the switcher changes it (Footer and switcher).
- Create Space is Arc's blue, #2129FB, 36 tall with a radius of 8 and a white label, semibold 13. Until the field has a name, all of the button is at 0.4. Cancel is white 0.6, semibold 12. Create Space doesn't change under the pointer or while pressed. Ours, as Arc's aren't recorded: Cancel brightens to white under the pointer, and the Profile and Choose a Theme rows take the rows' hover fill, white 0.12.
- Create, or ↩ with a name, sends the form away from the bottom up (Space form leave): the buttons fade, then the rows, the field and the title fade as they sink. Arc then plays its illustration for about 2 s and shows the Space's rows within a frame. Ours shows them once the form is gone, 0.28 s after Create.

**Renaming and deleting** (Arc's menu; our wording)

- A right-click on the Space's name, on its dot in the switcher or on its Library card, or a press on the name row's •••, shows the Space's menu in Arc 1.171's order: Change Space Icon…, Rename Space…, Edit Theme Color…, Set Profile ›, a line, New Folder…, with the key the user gave it if any, a line, Manage Spaces…, which opens the Library on its Spaces, a line, and Delete Space, off for the last Space. Before Manage Spaces…, Export “name” into Folder › lists the user's Spaces (Arc 1.171), each with its swatch. Ours: Remove Icon after Change Space Icon… and Remove Theme Color after Edit Theme Color…, each once there's one to remove, and Export as Markdown… after Export into Folder. Arc's Live Folders ›, after New Folder, isn't carried over, as keeping a GitHub live folder fresh means polling an idle browser (the owner, 2026-10-29, #679). Share Space…, before Export into Folder, comes on ilo-server after sync, with Unshare (the owner, 2026-10-29, #680).
- **The name row** (Arc 1.171, #1400). Under the pointer the row takes a tab row's plate, 8 in from both of the sidebar's edges and 36 tall, and the ••• shows on a 24 pt square whose end is 6 in from the plate's, its middle about 26 from the sidebar's edge; the plate, the ••• and the chevron in the icon's slot (Pinned tabs) come and go in one frame, and the plate stays while the row's menu is up. The ••• opens the Space's menu at the pointer and hides while the name is a field. Ours (*interim*, Arc's look unrecorded): the plate is a tab row's hover fill, white 0.12 at radius 12; the ••• is Lucide's ellipsis in the secondary text, lit as a row's ✕ is; the name ends before the ••• while it shows; the ••• and chevron stay for VoiceOver.
- **A lone unnamed Space** (Arc 1.171, #1400). With the browser's only Space unnamed, which Arc counts as no name and no icon and ours as no icon and the name it gave a Space the user didn't name, "Space 1" (the first launch's and the form's), the name row hides, and what's under it rises as Arc's does: 39 under the URL field, 36 under the favorites. Its menu, from the sidebar's empty space or its dot, has Show Space Header after Edit Theme Color… (Remove Theme Color when there's one), which keeps the row in every window and over a quit, and then says Hide Space Header, as Arc's item names what it will do; a named Space's menu has neither. The row shows while the Space is renamed or given an icon there, as the field and the icon picker hang from it, and stays once it has a name or an icon of the user's. Ours: an agent's Space is a second Space, so the row shows while one is open, and the row comes and goes in one frame, the rows with it (Arc's motion unrecorded). With the row hidden its chevron goes with it, and View's and the command bar's Collapse and Expand Pinned Tabs still fold the pinned rows, as Arc offers the chevron only in a named Space.
- Ours (*interim* until Arc's is recorded, #433): a right-click on the sidebar's empty space, anywhere in a Space's page that isn't a row, a button or a field (under the last tab, the gap between pinned and Today, an empty Space), shows New Tab (⌘T), which opens the command bar as ⌘T does, New Folder…, a line, then the same Space menu without its own New Folder…, for the Space whose page is under the pointer. Mid-swipe that's the page under the pointer, and New Tab and New Folder… make theirs in its Space; while a Space slides in after a switch, it's the Space switched to, as a press on a row is (Showing a tab). A private window's menu has New Tab alone, as it has no folders.
- Rename Space… turns the name into a field with all of it selected, on the row's plate, with Done in the •••'s place, 32 × 26 (Arc 1.171; ours: radius 6, 11 pt medium in the secondary text on white 0.12). ↩ or Done keeps what's typed unless it's empty, Esc cancels, and losing the keyboard ends it (Other row actions). A double-click doesn't rename, as the row's press folds the pinned tabs.
- Export “name” into Folder › turns the Space into a folder in the Space picked, as Arc's does. The Space's own entry is dimmed, as Arc's is, and so is each Space of another profile (ours): there its tabs would go as new ones, which ⌘Z couldn't put back. The item is left out while no Space can take it. The folder goes at the bottom of that Space's pinned tabs, open, with the Space's name and its emoji if it has one, and holds the Space's pinned tabs, folders and split views in their order. The Space's Today tabs go to the top of that Space's Today, in their order. The window shows that Space, and the Space exported goes. Ours, as Arc's handling isn't recorded: the Today tabs move rather than go to the archive, and nothing asks first, since nothing closes. A toast "Space Exported into Folder" offers its Undo. One ⌘Z brings the Space back where it was, with every row in its place, and deletes the folder; ⇧⌘Z exports it again. With Settings › Tabs & Sidebar › Share favorites at In Each Space, the Space's own favorites go with it and come back with the ⌘Z, as Delete Space's do. It's the user's sidebar, so agents get no tool for it; an agent keeps to its own Space.
- Export as Markdown…, and the command bar's Export Space as Markdown… for the Space shown, saves the Space as a Markdown file of links where the save panel says, named for the Space: its name as a heading, then Pinned, with each folder an item and its rows nested under it, then Today, a split view's tabs a link each. A pinned tab links to the URL it goes back to, any other to its page; titles and folder names have Markdown's marks escaped. Ours: Arc's Settings › Advanced › Export Data saves every Space in one file (#674). A private window has none. It's the user's file, so agents get no tool for it.
- Delete Space asks first, if the Space has tabs, and its tabs close with it: its Today tabs go to the archive, and its pinned tabs with the Space. An empty Space goes without asking. Ours: the dialog says "Its 3 tabs go with it. ⌘Z brings them back." under Arc's question, as Clear Archive's names its ⌘Z, since "close" made the delete sound final (#1388). Either way the toast says "Space deleted! Use ⌘Z to undo." (Toast).
- ⌘Z brings the Space back where it was in the switcher, with its name, colour, icon, folders and pinned tabs, and the Today tabs that went to the archive, unloaded, and pages the sidebar to it with the usual slide. Arc's toast stays until its time is up; ours goes with the ⌘Z, since our ⌘Z undoes every edit, and a second one the toast still on screen prompts would undo the one before (Toast, #1375). Its Today tabs also reopen with ⇧⌘T in the Space shown; ⌘Z then leaves out those already back. Ours: ⌘Z undoes whichever of Clear and Delete Space came last, once, after the toast as well as during it, as Clear's does.
- **The dialog** (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×; ours asks before Clear Archive too). The window dims to #151515 at 0.62 under a card 450 wide, centred on it: #161B2F at radius 16, with a 1 pt line of white 0.1 inside its edge, 0.5 pt of black 0.9 outside it, and a shadow of black 0.56, 16 down and blurred 16.
  - Text is inset 38 from the card's top and left: the question in 18 pt medium white on a 22 pt line, then 15.5 below it the detail in 14 pt at white 0.78 on 18. The buttons are 38 under it, or 46 under the question with no detail, at the right, 28 in from the card's right and bottom and 8 apart. A third answer, as ⌘Q's has, is a button at the left, at least 16 before Cancel.
  - The buttons are 36 tall at radius 8, the label in macOS's control font, 13 pt regular, 12 in from the left, and the key's chip, 20 tall at radius 5, 8 after it and 12 in from the right. Cancel is #2F305F with a 1 pt line of #6462A9 and text at white 0.9, lightening to #45436F under the pointer, its ESC chip 34.5 wide, white 0.14 over the button (#4D4B77 on it) with 11 pt semibold #BBB9E3. The action is #E1492C on a 2 pt ledge of its colour at 0.2, and #CC4126 under the pointer, with a ↩ chip 30 wide, white 0.1 with a line of white 0.27.
  - Esc and Cancel cancel, ↩ and the action confirm, and the action happens once the dialog has gone (Motion › Dialog). Ours: a click on the dim cancels, and the card has no icon over the question, where Arc's has its own.

**Icon** (Arc 1.170.0 cap of its icon picker on the Icon tab, at 2×).

- Change Space Icon… in the Space's menu, or a click in the Library card's icon slot, opens the icon picker: a popover hanging under the name row. Ours: so does a click on the icon before the Space's name while the Space has no pinned rows to fold, where the row's press would do nothing, and a click on it while the picker shows closes it; the icon takes clicks on Arc's 26 pt frame round it, which lights under the pointer with the header's plate, white 0.077 at radius 6, in one frame, as a header button does (*interim*: Arc's isn't recorded).
- The card is 273 × 273 at radius 20 (continuous in Arc), its top 25 below the middle of the name row. Its arrow's tip is 13 over the card, 13 in from its left and under the icon's middle, so it overlaps the row's plate. Its sides go down at 45°, its tip is rounded by 5, and a 3.5 fillet joins its right side to the card's top. Its left side runs into the card's left edge, which has no corner there.
- It's translucent: grey #484848 at 0.565 over the blur, lighter than the command bar's #191919 at 0.86. The edge is the command bar's: a 0.5 pt near-black outline, a 1 pt grey one inside it, and its shadow.
- At the top, 14.5 down, Emoji | Icon: a 114 × 24 track of white 0.18 at radius 6, the chosen half filled with the accent (Arc's measured #3D81F9, the system's; ours is the Space's accent, its label in the accent's label colour), its label 13 pt white and the other's #EDEDEF. A trash button follows 7 after it (its glyph 12 × 13), the two centred.
- **Only an icon can be removed** (Arc 1.170 cap, 2026-10-27, on a Space with none). The trash shows only when the Space has an icon; with none, Emoji | Icon is centred alone. Remove Icon in the Space's menu, and in a tab's and a favorite's, follows the same test, and so does the name's place (Sections, top to bottom). An icon is one the sidebar draws: a glyph this build doesn't know draws the dot, so it counts as none.
- **The placeholder** (Arc 1.170 cap at 1×, 2026-10-27). While the picker shows for a Space with no icon, a dashed rounded square holds the icon's place in its frame, centred on the favicons' centre (x 26), with the name after it at x 41, and the arrow points at its middle. It is 24 across, rounded by about 6, a 1 pt line of 4 pt dashes and 2 pt gaps in black at 0.30 (seen over a red Space; *interim* over the others, where ours is the same). A picked icon takes its place. Closed with no icon, it goes, and the name goes back to x 18, with no motion. A Space with an icon shows none, and so does a tab's row (Arc's isn't recorded there).
- **The Icon tab.** 56 filled glyphs, 8 × 7 on a 31 pt pitch. The first column's middle is 27.75 in and the first row's 63.25 under the card's top. Their ink is 12–13 pt, and the Space's sits on a 30 pt plate of white 0.25 at radius 4. A click on a glyph gives the Space that icon at once and closes the popover.
- The trash, and Remove Icon, take the icon away. Esc, a click outside, another window and another app close it with no change.
- **Motion** (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×). It grows in as a system popover does, from its arrow, and goes in one frame. Switching between Emoji and Icon slides the content a card's width the way the chosen segment is, so Icon's comes in from the right. Motion › Icon picker, Icon picker tabs.
- Ours, where Arc's isn't recorded or isn't ours to copy:
  - The glyphs are Lucide's outlines (NOTICE) at the icons' one stroke, the nearest subject to each of Arc's in Arc's order. The baseball and the planet are ours, drawn on Lucide's grid, as Lucide has neither; the cloud outline is Lucide's cloudy, as every glyph is an outline now, and the trays are its gallery-vertical-end. The circle, square and triangle are outlines, so the circle reads close to the no-icon dot, which is 8 pt to the glyph's 16.
  - They draw in a 16 pt box, 13 pt of ink. The pointer's glyph and the trash get the ladder's plate, white 0.12, and 0.065 pressed (ours). The segment not chosen darkens instead, #16140F at the same alphas, away from its white label. The trash is Lucide's at 16 pt.
  - It grows from 0.05 its size about the arrow's tip, its blur with it, and its shadow fades in alongside. It's the command bar's kind of child window (Overlays): only one of the two shows at a time.
  - It opens on the Emoji tab for a Space with an emoji, else on Icon. It waits until the name row has stopped moving, such as during a Space's slide or the sidebar docking.
  - From the Library, Change Space Icon… closes the Library and hangs the popover under the sidebar's name row (Arc's from the card: measure).
- **The Emoji tab** (ours: Arc's emoji grid isn't recorded, so it takes the Icon tab's grid, plates and keys, and lives in the card rather than in the system's palette). A search field under the control, 44 down, 24 tall and as wide as the grid (8 × 31 = 248, 12.25 in) on the control's fill at radius 6, with "Search" in white 0.53 until something is typed. Under it, 6 below, a list that scrolls on the Icon tab's pitch and columns: eight to a row, 31 pt each, the first 12.25 in, in 20 pt emoji on 30 pt plates, down to 6 over the card's bottom. The recently used come first, the last 16 picked, newest first, under a "Recently Used" header, and then Unicode's groups in order, Smileys & Emotion to Flags, each under a header in 11 pt white 0.53 that takes a row, 4 in. Only fully-qualified emoji are in it, one of each, and only the rows in view are laid out. **Skin tones** (Arc's help centre lists them; ours, as Slack's and the Mac's palette's: one tone for the whole grid). A 20 pt plate at the search's right end, 2 in, holds ✋ in the tone the grid shows, at 14 pt. A click on it puts the six hands along the field's right end, Unicode's default yellow then light to dark, with the shown one on the chosen plate, and a click on one shows every emoji that has tones in it, a search's finds too, and folds them back to the one button. The tone is kept with the recents, as a `skin tone` line an older build reads as no emoji, and an emoji whose toned form the system can't draw stays in the default. The keyboard stays in the search through both clicks. It lists only the emoji the running system can draw: the crate knows newer Unicode than an older macOS's Apple Color Emoji, which draws a new emoji as a "?" box and a new ZWJ sequence as the emoji it is made of. The first time the tab shows in a launch, the host is asked about each emoji (~14 ms for the ~1,900), and the answer is kept. On the Mac that is CoreText laying out the emoji in Apple Color Emoji, drawn if it comes out as one glyph of that font, so no table of macOS versions has to be kept right; another platform answers through its own call, or offers everything. An emoji a newer Mac saved as a recent or as a Space's icon stays in the file and on the Space, and shows again there; it just isn't offered here. These numbers sit together at the top of `app/src/icon_picker/emoji.rs`, so a capture of Arc's tab changes only them.
  - The Space's emoji is on the chosen plate, as the Icon tab's glyph is, and in view when it opens. The pointer's emoji gets the Icon tab's hover plate. A click on an emoji gives the Space that icon at once and closes the popover, and puts it first among the recents, kept in the profile directory's `Recent Emoji` and so across a relaunch.
  - The search has the keyboard. Typing narrows the list to the emoji whose name or shortcode holds every word typed, in Unicode's order with no headers, and puts the plate on the first, so a word and ↩ is a pick; Japanese input works in it as in any field. Emptied, the whole list comes back at the Space's emoji. ↑ and ↓ move the plate a row, keeping its column, and ← and → move it along the emoji while the search is empty, else the caret; ↩ gives the Space the emoji on the plate, and Esc closes with no change. Names and shortcodes are the `emojis` crate's (NOTICE), which has no keywords beyond them.
- **Where it shows.** An emoji shows before the Space's name in a 20 pt slot, 13 pt, a tab's size, centred on the favicons' centre (x 26) as Arc's 26 pt icon frame at x 13 is (ax), and on the name's cap height as a tab's is on its title. A glyph takes the same place at the emoji's size, in the name's colour; Arc's envelope there is 14 × 11 pt of ink. Both show in the sidebar, on the Library's Spaces cards and in place of the dot in the switcher (Footer and switcher). The name moves over to make room for it (Sections, top to bottom), with no motion.
- The Spaces menu lists the Space after its emoji. A glyph isn't shown there, as GPUI's menu items take no image.
- The core keeps an emoji, or `glyph:` and the glyph's name. The names are ours, so a glyph can be redrawn without changing what Spaces keep, and a name the build doesn't know draws the dot. The icon is kept with the Space in the core, saved in the Spaces file, and back after a relaunch and after ⌘Z on a deleted Space. Agents read it with the Space's name; they have no tool to set it yet.

**Colour.** The Space colour drives the window gradient (Shared rules) and the accent. Dia's hue families are neutral, red, orange, yellow, green, blue, pink and purple. Only pink is measured: tint #B25B6B (P3), New Tab band #B5556B. A Space's colours come from Arc's theme picker (Themes), from the Create a Space form's Choose a Theme row and the Space menu's Edit Theme Color….

- The core keeps the theme with the Space, and its main colour as the Space's colour, saved in the Spaces file and back after a relaunch and after ⌘Z on a deleted Space. A new Space has no colour, and the gradient uses the system accent's. Agents have no tool to set a theme yet.
- Edit Theme Color…, from the Space's menu or the command bar's Edit Theme…, opens the picker as Arc 1.171's does: with no arrow, against the sidebar's edge at the window's top, over the page rather than the sidebar it colours. Arc's card there is 355 × 503, 14 inside a 382 × 534 window; ours keeps the form's 357 × 509 card and puts it 14 in from the sidebar's edge and the window's top (#1400). It grows in from its top left (ours: Arc's growth there isn't recorded). The form's Choose a Theme row and a Library card's brush still open it beside themselves, its arrow pointing at them. A Space coloured before themes opens the picker at its colour's hue and lightness, keeping the colour itself, as a swatch does. Remove Theme Color, after Edit Theme Color… once the Space has a colour, as Remove Icon follows Change Space Icon…, takes the theme and the colour away, so the gradient uses the system accent's again; nothing in Arc's recording takes a Space's colour away.

**Agent Space** (ours: decision 8). Each agent's tabs open in a Space of its own, which the core keeps hidden: a task's, made with the task, and an outside client's, one for each client the user connected, which its connections share with their tabs and permissions. No window lists one: not the switcher, the Spaces menu, ⌃1–⌃9, Move to, Search Tabs or the Library, and the user sees an agent's work on its card (Agent task). Neither Arc nor Dia has a reference, so the layout values are ours to set.

- A Space is the user's or one agent's, by the id and name the agent's client gives, from when it's made. New Space always makes the user's, and no tab of the user's moves into an agent's: an agent never takes the user's tabs, and the user keeps its work with its card's Open.
- Agent actions never change the user's selected Space or tab, the keyboard focus or the cursor. Agent tabs never come to the front.
- **Confirmations.** The card offers Allow, Deny or Always allow on <site>. Settings › Agents lists each agent's answers for good, Always Allow and Never, each with Revoke, and Revoke All; a ⌘T task's are its CLI's, under What Claude may do from ⌘T, as each task is new (#1913). A confirmation times out after 5 minutes (prototype).
- **Handoff.** At a sign-in, 2FA prompt or CAPTCHA, the tab pauses and the card reads "<agent> needs you", with Show tab and Hand back. It times out after 15 minutes (prototype).
- **Take over / Hand back.** A press in the agent's page takes it over, and the press goes on to the page; a scroll never does (#1080). While the user drives, the agent can't act there; Hand back, on the strip under the page, gives it back. ⌘↩ is Take Over or Hand Back, and ⌘. is Stop while the agent works, with focus in the page too (#365). A press meant only to select is undone by Hand back or ⌘↩, and the agent goes on where it was. Escape, Stop Loading's other key, never stops an agent, as a stray one would end its work.
- **Status and activity.** The status reads "Waiting for you" or "Waiting for your OK". Settings › Agents lists each agent's actions newest first.
- **The agent's pointer** (ours, #365). Where an agent clicks or types in its page the window shows, watched from its card, its pointer, Lucide's mouse-pointer-2 in Signal green with the agent's name in a pill beside it, glides to the element's centre in 0.26 s ease-out, from where it was or 36 pt up and left, and a 2 pt Signal ring, 3 pt outside the element's box, comes in over 0.12 s with the ref the agent named the element by in a chip on its top left. It stays 1.6 s after the last action and fades in 0.2 s. Reduce motion puts it there at once. It draws in the window over the page, which the mouse goes through, so it's never in the user's way, and a page the window doesn't show has none. *Interim*: the look and the timings.
- **Launch and quit.** Quit deletes the outside clients' Spaces with their tabs, as it does the tasks', and a finished session's tabs never reopen. At launch, a task's or client's Space a run before left, as a crash does, or as a run from before #1080 did, when the user saw a client's as a Space of theirs, gives its tabs to the top of the user's Space in its profile, then goes (ours, #1080). A crash shows no receipt, so the user chose neither Keep nor Dismiss, and of the two only Keep can be undone: tabs close to the archive with ⌘Z, where pages dismissed at launch would be gone for good (UX principles › Forgive rather than forbid). Arc brings back every tab a crash left, too.

**Agent task** (ours: decision 8). A task the user asks from ilo, which the user's own `claude` or `codex` CLI runs. Neither Arc nor Dia has one, so the layout values are ours; *interim* while the owner lives with it.

- **Setup.** Settings › Agents, under its one switch, has a row for each of `claude` and `codex`, found on the PATH: Signed in, Not found with how to install it, or Not signed in with the command that signs in (`claude auth login`, `codex login`) and Copy. ilo never asks for the login itself. Under them, Run tasks on picks the default agent, and each agent's row has a Model pop-up, its CLI's own default first, since ilo runs the CLI without the user's settings files. ilo learns whether each is signed in when Settings › Agents or ⌘T's ask first shows and when a task fails for it, never on a timer, and keeps the last answer till then (UX principles › Pay when it's looked at). With no agent ready, ⇥ in ⌘T offers Set up Claude or Codex, which opens Settings › Agents, and nothing else about tasks shows. Agents are named, with no logo or colour of their makers'.
- **Asking.** While Settings › Agents' Tasks from the command bar is on, typing in ⌘T shows "Ask Claude", the agent ↩ would run on ("Ask an agent" until ilo has learned which are signed in), with ⇥ at the field's end in a key's chip with a hairline rim, so "Tab" off the Mac doesn't read as a tab, as the bar's site-search hint does (#1080); a site's keyword still takes ⇥ first, and Return without ⇥ still searches. ⇥ makes the text a task: the page the user is on goes with it as a chip before the text, which ⌫ at the start takes off, the hint reads Run ↩, and the rows become the agents that are signed in, the default from Settings › Agents selected, so asking it is still ⇥ ↩, and ↓ picks another for this task only. Each agent's row has its CLI's terminal glyph in the icon box and the model it runs on at its end, dimmed as a row's detail is; one at its plan's limit says when it resets there instead, dimmed, and isn't selected. A long ask scrolls to keep the caret in view, ending 11 before Run ↩, and an edge with more text past it fades over 24 into the bar, its outer third solid so a glyph the edge cuts never shows a sliver. While the ask shows, the sidebar's foot opens an empty slot for the card, a plate's 40 pt over the others, and the slot takes one of the three places (UX principles › Everything has a place). Return folds the bar into the slot: over 380 ms on (.2, .8, .2, 1) its plate goes to the slot's size, corners and fill and its shadow goes, its rows and text fading in the first 110 ms, then the card's content fades in over 150 ms, and the task starts in the current Space. Linux and Windows hide the bar and fade the card in (*interim*). Esc or ⌫ on an empty field leaves, and the slot closes. A task an agent starts while the ask shows takes a plate of its own. The page goes as its address and title: the agent opens it in its own Space if it needs it, and never gets the user's tab. ilo never picks the agent by the task. A task stays with the CLI that started it, as its conversation is that CLI's session.
- **The foot.** Tasks are the window's, not a Space's: their cards sit at the sidebar's foot over the media players, so pinned tabs, Today and New Tab never move for them, and a switch or a swipe between Spaces leaves them where they are. Pages a task moves still go to the Space it was asked from. A private window offers no tasks and shows none. Each card is a plate of a player's shape, a tile's plate with the hover's fill: 40 tall, radius 12, 8 in from each side, 6 apart and 6 over the players or the footer, the oldest lowest, with no heading; the list ends over them. It holds what's live or unread: a finished task the user has seen goes to the Archive (Done). Up to three show: those waiting for the user first, then working, then done or stopped, newest first in each. With more, a fourth plate on top says "2 more tasks" and opens the foot to all of them, scrolling past half the sidebar's height, its plate then saying Show less. A window too short for the list keeps the foot and scrolls the list above it. The pages a task opens never become rows, however many there are, and a card never takes the selection or the keyboard.
- **The card.** Its state is an 18 pt glyph in the favicon column, centred on its two lines: a Signal ring, r 5.25 and 1.5 thick, at 0.28 with a 1.9 dot circling it once every 1.4 s while the agent works; a question stops the dot where it is and slides it to the centre as the ring comes to full (240 ms), the ringed dot; done, the ring shrinks to 0.6 and fades, the dot goes, and a Signal tick draws in over 300 ms after 90 ms, as Copy URL's check does; stopped, the dot fades and the ring turns white 0.45. Working again, the dot circles again. The rings keep these values, never raised as text is to read on the window, as the status beside them says the same in words. On the selected card's fill, which Signal stands out from less, the working ring is 0.5, which stands out from it as 0.28 does from a card's own fill on a dark window, over a dark backdrop and a light one, and the ring's other values brighten with it. The title the agent gives it, two to four words in 13 pt, is over its status in 11 pt at the ink's 0.75, where secondary's 0.62 read under 4.5:1 on the card's plate (#1080), both on the tab titles' column at x 35. The status says how far the agent has got, from its own steps and never a clock: "Step 5 · Clicking “Tickets”", the step running, as its trace and the activity log name it, after how many of the turn's steps it is, its thoughts left out, and "Thinking · no steps yet" from the moment the user sends until the first. Its lead, the count, or "Needs you" while it waits on the user, is in the title's ink at medium, so the one card that needs the user reads heaviest and the count survives the ellipsis. Each new status rolls in, the old going up 8 as it fades and the new coming from 8 below (200 ms), and a highlight sweeps along it every 1.8 s while the agent works, from the status's colour to the full ink. The status is text, so it never dims below what reads on the window, and the highlight starts from wherever that puts it: a dark window raises the status to 0.88, and the highlight still goes to 1. A new title crossfades over 200 ms. ✕ shows on hover, a player's button. Under Reduce Motion every change is a crossfade and the glyph holds its dot still.
- **It asks on the card.** A question is the card's status, the question alone, up to two lines, the plate growing to it over 280 ms; what the user needs to know to answer it shows over it in the conversation. An agent that puts it all in the question has its last sentence that asks on the card. Its answers are as chips under it on the URL field's plate, the first chip's text on the title column and its plate hanging 8 out to the left, each rising 4 as it fades in over 180 ms, 50 ms after the one before; an answer folds the plate back. Answers that won't fit two rows of chips don't show, and a click opens the conversation to answer there. A confirmation is a card's Allow, Deny and Always allow on <site>, and a handoff its "<agent> needs you" with Show tab (Agent Space); while one waits the status reads "Needs your OK · <what it would do>" or "Needs you · <why>". The card under the URL field has one primary button, on the right in the Space's accent as the dialog keeps its action: Allow Once, Show Tab, or Hand Back when the agent asks for its tab back. The answer that leaves things as they are is on the URL field's plate, and Always Allow, which reaches further than it looks, is its words alone until the pointer is on it, on a row of its own (#1080). The agent never asks for a password or a code in the conversation: a sign-in is a handoff.
- **Opening it.** Clicking the card gives the page area to its conversation at once, and the card has the selected tab's fill while it shows, the Space's tab giving up its own, as only what's in the page area looks selected. The URL field shows what's in the page area too: the conversation is no page, so the field reads as an empty card's, the bar's placeholder, from the frame the conversation shows. A click on it or ⌘L opens the bar empty over the conversation, as ⌘T does, Copy URL and the site controls have no page to act on, and back, forward and reload dim (Watching a page has a page's). Its header has the title and, under it, the agent, its model and where the task is ("Claude · Haiku · Working", "Waiting for you", "Done 13:28", "Stopped"), while it works followed by how far it has got, "step 5 · 4 pages", which changes in place as steps come; Talk and Pages N are one switch, a track with the view shown lifted on the selected tab's plate, and Stop ⌘. stands apart at its end on a chip's plate while the agent works. A page with no picture yet says so in its tile, "No picture yet" under the site's globe, and once the core had none to give, that it's "Taken when Claude next acts here". The reply field has a hairline inside its edge and the key that sends it, ↩ (Enter off the Mac), in a key's chip at its end. Under it, the task's pages as pictures in a row that scrolls sideways and never wraps, the one the agent is on ringed Signal; a page closed leaves the row, and they're pictured only while the conversation shows. Then the user's ask on its plate with its page as a chip, and the agent's messages, with its work between them as a trace: one per stretch between messages, a line that says Working and how many steps while it runs and Worked for 41s after, open while the agent works and folded once its message comes, unless the user opened or folded it, which holds. A turn the user sends, by Run ↩ or a reply, has that line under their message in the same frame, saying Thinking with the running step's shimmer, before the CLI says anything, and the agent's first step or message takes its place. A CLI that ends, or says nothing for 3 minutes, which it then stops, turns it into Couldn't start with why in red, and the card says why; a reply starts it again. Each step is a line that says what it did once done and what it's doing while it runs, the one running shimmering while it shows (still under Reduce Motion); one that failed says Couldn't … in red, its glyph too, with the reason when its error gives one (not found, timed out, 504, still loading, not a web page) and nothing more when it doesn't. A value typed shows only for a field that isn't a password and only up to 40 characters. A thought opens to the agent's reasoning, a look at a page to what it saw, an open, titled by its site alone, to its whole address, a failure to its error, and how long a step took comes from ilo's clock, not the agent's. The activity log has the same lines. The agent's messages show the everyday part of Markdown: paragraphs with their line breaks, lists, bold, italic, code on a faint plate in the text's own face, and links, which open as the user's tab and leave the conversation; a heading is a bold line. Finish's result is the turn's answer: the same words written just before it show once, and what the CLI writes after it doesn't show. A question's answers are chips under it; the reply field sits at the bottom with the keyboard, and the lines meet a clean edge 16 above it: the window's material under them is the system's, so no fade's colour can match it. The newest line stays in view over it as lines come, when the conversation opens again, and when the window gets shorter while it's at its end. It reads and takes input as it shows.
- **It moves as it changes.** Only what changes while the conversation shows moves, each on (.2, .8, .2, 1); opening it, switching to it, scrolling back and relaunching show it as it is, at once. A reply leaves the field at once, which keeps the keyboard, and goes from the field's rect to its plate in 260 ms, from the field's corners and fill to the plate's, as the lines make room on the same curve. The status rolls 8 over 200 ms ("Working", "Done 13:31"), and Stop fades in and out with the work over 150 ms. Thinking rises 4 over 150 ms and shimmers; the turn's first step crossfades it, 90 ms out and 110 ms in, to "Working · 1 step" with its chevron, and its count rolls as each step comes. A step rises 4 over 150 ms and shimmers while it runs, then crossfades to its past tense as its note fades in over 200 ms. A page the task opens grows into the row over 220 ms as it fades in, and the Signal ring slides to it 140 ms after. What Claude writes comes in as it writes it, under the open trace, each new run fading in over 160 ms, at most once a frame; a call takes it into the trace where it showed. As the turn ends, its trace folds first, its steps fading over 110 ms as its height closes over 280 ms and its chevron turns back over 200 ms, to "Worked for 4s · 3 steps"; an answer that comes whole (Finish's result, Codex's messages) then comes a block at a time, 50 ms apart. The lines follow what comes at their end only while the user reads there: once they've scrolled up, nothing moves what they read. A question's chips rise 4 over 180 ms from 120 ms, 50 ms apart; the one picked goes on from where it was clicked as the reply, the others fading over 120 ms as their row closes over 260 ms. Done fades Stop and the ring out as Open rises in. Text reads and takes input as it comes, and nothing waits on motion. Under Reduce Motion it all crossfades in place. Frames are drawn only while something moves or a step shimmers, and only while the conversation shows.
- **Watching a page.** Clicking a picture zooms that page out of it to fill the page area, and going back shrinks it into its picture. The Signal ring and the agent's pointer show only while it drives. A strip under the page, not over it (GPUI can't draw over the page view), has back to the conversation, the card's glyph as the foot draws it, then "Claude is working · Step 5 · <step>", "Claude needs you · <why>" in semibold and the full ink, "Claude finished · <site>" or Stopped, and its chips: Stop ⌘. while the agent works, and "You're driving · <agent> can't act here" with Hand back ⌘↩ while the user drives, and Open <site> once it's done, if the page shown is one the task left the user, which moves that page alone. They're quiet, on a chip's plate, until the agent needs the user: only Hand back, while the card waits for the page the user holds, is primary, in the Space's accent (#1080). The keys work with the keyboard in the page too (#365), and Esc steps back to the conversation. An agent's tabs start muted: while one plays, its tile in Pages and its strip show a crossed speaker before its site and Stop, which a click unmutes, as a row's does; Open, which makes a page the user's, unmutes it (#1080). While a task's page shows, the URL field shows its address, and Copy URL, the site controls and Edit URL act on it; ⌥↩ in that edit goes as ↩, since the page stays in the task's Space.
- **Needs you.** Show tab opens the page with the user driving; the agent never sees what they type, and Hand back ⌘↩ gives it back. A task waiting for the user turns its card to "Needs you" at the foot, which every Space shows, and brings a hidden sidebar out once, as a question does; it recedes as after any reveal once the pointer leaves, so it's never held over the page while the user works elsewhere (#1080).
- **Outside ilo.** It stays quiet until it needs the user. The Mac's Dock icon has a badge with the number of tasks waiting for the user, ⌘T's and outside agents' alike: a sign-in, a code, a CAPTCHA, a confirmation or the agent's own question. It goes once none waits, and it stays while ilo is active, as Mail's does, so it never flickers. On Windows the taskbar button's overlay carries the count over the Space's dot, up to 9, then 9+. On Linux, where no badge is standard, the windows have the urgency hint while one waits, on X11; Wayland lets ilo mark no window. A need that comes while ilo isn't the active app posts one system notification, never repeated: "<agent> needs you" over the step, such as "Sign in to github.com", with no sound. Its click brings ilo forward and opens the task's card, as the card's click does, and never puts it away if it shows already. The Mac asks the system's leave the first time one is posted, not at launch, and once refused asks no more, the badge still counting. Nothing else interrupts: no sound of ilo's, no bounce, no flash. ilo keeps nothing of them past the run, as the activity log has the need. There's no switch in Settings yet (#1636); the system's own notification settings for ilo turn them off.
- **Done.** The tick draws itself in and the status holds the agent's summary of what it did, or without one its result's first words, as the tick says it's done. An outside agent's session has no summary, so its status is its receipt (#1080), which names only what had consequences: "Done · 1 order placed · 2 messages sent", each one the user allowed and the page did. Its pages are Open's button's to count. A session with none of them says when it ended ("Done 13:28"), as its conversation's header does. When the work ends on pages only the user may finish, as agents never pay, the agent leaves up to ten, and the card has Open <site> for one or Open 3 pages for several, which moves them into the Space as tabs, in the agent's order, the first shown: each leaves the chip as a ghost row and grows into its place at the top of Today in 420 ms, 70 ms after the one before, as Today makes room for it in 220 ms, and the strip's Open does the same for its page. Under Reduce Motion the rows fade in. The summary rolls in as a status does and its chips come as a question's. A page the strip opened leaves the count, and with none left the chip goes. ✕ on hover, or ⌘W with its conversation shown, dismisses the task to the Archive, its plate folding away (its height to nothing as it fades, 280 ms), an edit on the window's ⌘Z history. Archived Tabs lists it there among the tabs until ilo quits, and a click on its row brings it back with its conversation open (Library › Archived Tabs); ⇧⌘T brings it back too, as it does a closed tab. A finished task, done, stopped or failed, goes there by itself once the user has seen it, as the foot keeps what's live or unread: 120 ms after they leave its conversation, by another tab, ⌘W or a Space switch, having opened it or seen it finish there, and 80 ms after Open's last page lands in Today, with pages left, it stays. Its plate goes at once and the plates above close its room in 280 ms after 40 ms, as a copy flies into the Archive button at the footer's left in 380 ms on (.4, 0, .2, 1), to the button's box and corners, its content fading in the first 100 ms and its plate over the last 40 %; as it lands, the button's glyph grows to 1.18 and brightens to white 0.95 35 % of the way through 300 ms, eased out, and goes back. A task never opened stays until the user opens it or ✕ takes it, one at its plan's limit waits for Retry, and one asking or working never goes. It's listed in Archived Tabs, and ⇧⌘T and ⌘Z bring it back, as for a dismissed one. Under Reduce Motion it fades where it is and the stack closes, and a private window, with no Archive button, folds it away as ✕ does. Brought back, a task offers the pages still in its Space; one already opened stays the user's tab. Clear leaves the tasks, which are the window's.
- **Stopped or out of usage.** ⌘. or Stop ends the task at once and the card says how far it got, "Stopped after 6 steps", or Stopped with none; a reply goes on from there. When the user's plan hits its limit, the card says when it resets, or "Claude hit its usage limit" if the CLI doesn't say, with Retry, which goes on from where it stopped. ilo never retries by itself. A failure is said in plain words, never a code: the CLI's own sentence, or "Claude ran into an error" when what it gave was a code, a status or JSON, which the log keeps.
- **Cost.** Nothing about tasks runs while ⌘T opens or takes keys, or while a tab switches: ask's rows come from what ilo last learned, and a card redraws only when its task changes. The foot asks for frames only while something on it moves: the circling dot and the highlight run on the loading dots' clock, which stops in a window that's hidden or covered, and nothing runs once every task has stopped. The CLI's output is read on a thread that waits, as the MCP server's are, never polled.
- **Same rules.** Confirmations, handoffs, always-allowed sites with Revoke, and the activity log are any agent's, and Settings › Agents lists each agent's always-allowed sites. The task's own tools (its title, a question, finishing) each log an entry too.
- **Outside agents** (owner, 2026-11-03, #1080). An MCP client the user connected, such as Claude Code or Codex, gets the same card from the same component, with its states, chips, taking over by a press and Hand back, Done, Open and the Archive. What differs is that its conversation is in the agent's terminal:
  - One card for each client, not for each connection: its connections share its Space, its tabs and its permissions, and their calls are steps of the same card. Its title and name are the client's: Claude Code or Codex for those Settings › Agents connects, and for any other client the title its `clientInfo` gives, else its name, else MCP client.
  - It starts at the client's first call that opens or acts on a tab; list_tabs alone shows nothing. It's done when the client's last connection closes, or once 10 minutes pass with no call under way and nothing waiting on the user (*interim*), and its status then says when ("Done 13:31"), with Open for the pages it worked in that are still there. Its next call after that starts a card of its own.
  - Opening it shows its steps one by one, with no ask and no traces, under a header with no agent's name before its status, since the title is the name. In the reply field's place a line in secondary text reads "Talk to Claude Code where you started it.", and the window keeps the keyboard, so Esc, ⌘W and ⌘. work as for a task. Its question chips are only confirmations and handoffs, as it can't ask the user anything else here.
  - Stop, ⌘. or the strip's Stop stops the client on every connection: each call under way is answered that the user stopped it, then each connection closes, calls coming meanwhile refused, and the card says Stopped. The activity log says the user stopped it, which closed its connection. The client then shows ilo's server as disconnected; reconnecting it from the client is the user's step, and starts a new card. ilo's bridge exits when its connection closes and never reconnects by itself.
  - Open moves its pages to the top of Today in the Space the window shows, or in the first of the user's Spaces in the client's profile, as it was asked from none. Archived and dropped, it closes only its own pages, and none another session of the client's works in.

### Command bar

Arc's panel and rows (Arc 1.170.0 cap, ax); Dia's materials and colours where Arc's aren't measured (decision 6). Parsing, inline completion and ranking follow Chrome's omnibox, because Dia runs Chrome's AutocompleteController (O), except where Arc's own signals order the rows: open tabs rise, rows alike on a web app list once and the page shown goes last (Rows).

**Opening**

- ⌘T and ⌘L open the same panel, centred on the window (Arc 1.170 rec; Arc ax). A click on the sidebar's URL field opens a narrower one over the field (Arc 1.170 rec; Over the URL field, below). ⌘T makes no tab until a pick (Arc; Ardia's opt-in, P§7); Dia's New Tab page isn't ours (New tab). ⌘L and the click open it on the tab's URL, selected, with the page's favicon as the field's icon until the text is edited (Arc 1.170 rec).
- The recording's panel over the field is taken to be a click's, with medium-high confidence: the field brightens under the pointer 242 ms before it opens, and ⌘L's, recorded right after, is centred.
- With the sidebar hidden there's no field, and the click in the reveal's panel opens the centred one as the panel makes way (*interim*: Arc's isn't recorded).
- In a split view's new pane the panel moves right to 70 in from the card's right edge, which puts its field at x 719 where ⌘T's is at 426 (Arc cap).
- ⇧⌘A opens Search Tabs, as Dia's and Chrome's do (Arc 1.170.0 has none). Ours is the command bar, centred, listing only the open tabs but the one shown, the Space shown's first; typing keeps those it names, as the bar's Switch to Tab row does, and ↩ switches to one, in any Space or window.
- Open and close are one frame, for ⌘T, ⌘L and the click alike (Arc rec 120 fps, 3 runs, ⌘T; Arc 1.170 rec). Over the field the panel shows already grown, as Arc's does; Dia's grow out of the URL field isn't ours.
- ⌘T or ⌘L while the bar shows closes it as Esc does, whichever of them opened it (Arc's help centre). The browser's other shortcuts go on to the browser.

The text is ≈ 14 pt at white 0.35, the key 30 × 24 at radius 6 on white 0.06. It's the field's end, not the row's. It shows while the selected row hints a site search; no Go pill |

"Switch to Split View" for a split's row. - Ours, where Arc's isn't measured: all the bar's text is medium, as its title's strokes are, though Arc's cap height reads ≈ 13.5 pt against our 14; the placeholder colour is Dia's (Colour tokens), and the caret and selection are the Space's accent (#319); the bar is opaque, and its accent is the Space's (`app/src/palette.rs`: the theme's hue at an OKLab lightness that holds 3:1, ilo green for none); "Add to Split View" in place of "Switch to Tab" over a split's new pane; Tab's key on an unselected row isn't shown; a sum's row has no icon; each command has Lucide's glyph for it where Lucide plainly has one, and the rest share Lucide's command; page rows ask the profile's history for the favicon, with a globe until it comes; the field's icon stays the search glyph until something is typed, as Arc's empty bar has it.
- Not built: the info button, and the recents and commands Arc lists under the open tabs for empty text.

Ours: the shadow is the popover level's, as the other panels hanging from the field have, since the centred panel's modal one falls further than this panel is tall and read as a blank grey bar under it over a light page (#1306) |

**Rows**

- The Enter target is the inline match, else a typed URL, else a search for what was typed, except where an open tab takes row 0 (below). Page titles are never the default (Chrome). Typed with http:// or https://, an address is a typed URL whatever its host, so one that can't resolve shows the error page (ours: Chrome searches for a .invalid host or one it finds malformed). Dia's "Use website first" setting puts a strongly matching page first instead, and "Prefer search engine" turns that off.
- The search row comes right after the top hit (Dia 1.49).
- Row 0 is computed from local data only; remote suggestions append below it, so row 0 never flickers (Chrome).
- Open tabs appear as "Switch to Tab" rows across windows, never the current tab (Dia). Ours (*interim*, as Chrome's): a row for a tab another window shows live brings that window forward with the keyboard, rather than taking its page; any other comes to this window. Whether Arc raises or moves: measure.
- **Closed tabs and visited pages** (#2337; Arc's bar told neither from a page in history, so all of it is ours). A page whose tab is in the archive is that tab's row: ↩ brings it back with its history, name and icon, as the Library's Reopen does, and a page that left the archive since loads. Not while it's open here, where the open tab's row is the better one, nor for an address typed, which loads where ↩ goes, nor over a split's new pane or a private, Little ilo or Blank Window, which show a Space of their own. A page the profile's history has says the day it was last visited. Either day is today, yesterday, the weekday for the five days before, so it's never last week's, then the date, "3 Nov".
- **Open tabs rise** (Arc's signals, #1396: an outside reverse-engineering of Arc 1.17x's command bar decoded them, but its weights are in a model that wasn't recovered, so the order is ours, *interim*). Arc's bar is first of all a tab switcher, so when the typing starts a word of a tab's title, or of its site's name (the host's labels without the top-level domain, and without www.), at two characters or more, case aside, that tab rises with "Switch to Tab". Up to three rise (ours): the best is row 0, and ↩ switches to it; the others come right after the search row, before the profile's pages, each in place of the core's own row for it. A renamed tab whose own name starts with what's typed goes first, as Arc's `customTitlePrefix` boost; then the tabs switched to more, as Arc ranks by its last 10 switches to each tab (`recentlySwitchedToTabs`); then the one shown most recently. Ours, *interim*: each of a tab's last 10 switches counts one, halving each week. Arc also ranks by how much of the title or URL matches where, which waits. The list before anything is typed stays most recently shown first. Chrome's row 0 stays when it completes inline to a page that isn't an open tab's; a completion to an open tab's own page makes that row the tab's (ours: Arc's for an inline completion isn't measured). An address typed (a dot, a colon or a slash) whose page an open tab shows keeps its row first, where ↩ goes, and that tab's row comes second, as Arc's bar was seen doing; no tab rises by name while the typing reads as an address. The tab's row takes the site search the first row hints, which follows what's typed rather than the row (Site search), so Tab still enters it. It looks only at the tabs the bar was opened with (the Space's and the favorites, never the tab shown or what shows with it), so the rows don't wait for the core. A command that matches strongly still sits above the search row, under the tab.
- **Tabs on your other computers** (#1234; Arc's bar had none, so all of it is ours, *interim*). The tabs open on the account's other computers, which sync brings as Chrome's "tabs from other devices", are found by the open-tabs rule: two letters or more that start a word of the title or the site's name, none while the typing reads as an address. At most two, the computer seen last first, come right after the open tabs that rise, each in place of the core's row from history for its page, as a page row: its favicon, title and " — host", ending with the computer's name as Settings › Sync lists it ("ilo on Linux (ThinkPad X1)") where an agent's row has its model, white on the plate. ↩, ⌘↩ and ⌥↩ open the page here as any page's row does; it has no remove button, as it isn't history. Left out: the first row, where ↩ goes; a page a tab here or the window shows, whose row is better; a page in a Space here, as Spaces' tabs sync as the Spaces' own (#2098), which leaves the tabs outside the other computer's Spaces, such as Little ilo's and a Blank Window's; a page another computer listed first. Only over a window of the profile that syncs, the first; never in a private window, never with nothing typed, nor in Search Tabs, which are this computer's tabs. A computer's tabs stay listed until Chromium drops its session, 28 days after it last changed them. Nowhere else lists them yet: the Library and a Space's menu wait for a reason. Agents get none.
- **One row per title on web apps that give many pages one** (Arc's `dedupByTitleAndHostList`, #1396). A page from history, or a site, whose title an earlier row on the same site has, or an open tab's row there, is left out, so Figma's "Untitled" or GitHub's "Pull requests" lists once. Row 0 and open tabs' rows always stay, and other sites keep every row. Ours, *interim*: figma.com, notion.so, notion.com, github.com and zoom.us and their subdomains; Arc's list has more that weren't recovered.
- **The page shown goes last** (Arc's `derankCurrentItemURL`, #1396). Rows for the pages the window shows as the bar opens, each pane of a split, go to the end of the list, since picking one loads the page again; another tab's row at the same address keeps its place, as it switches. Row 0 stays, as ↩'s target, so a URL typed or completed to the page shown still loads it.
- **What the bar learns** (#1395, #1396, the owner's rules of 2026-11-03). Only the times of the user's picks of commands and switches to tabs, per profile, on this computer: never synced, an ilo account's included, never sent and never in a crash report. Picks are kept in `Command bar picks` in the profile's own folder, so they go when it's deleted and stay through a rename, at most 256 commands, the least counted going first; switches only while their tab is open and the app runs. A private window learns nothing, nor does an agent's Space, and agents never drive the bar. Deleting history forgets what was learned of it (ours): all of it, everything; a span of time, as Clear Browsing Data's Last hour, the picks and switches in it; a page, from the History page or the bar, the switches to tabs at it, since a pick names no page. Opening Clear Browsing Data forgets nothing, and history expiring isn't the user deleting it.
- Before anything is typed, the rows are the Space's open tabs, each with "Switch to Tab" (Arc cap). Ours (*interim*): its favorites, pinned tabs and Today tabs, the most recently shown first, without the tab shown or its split, at most ten; none over a split's new pane. ↩ switches to the selected one, and emptying the field lists them again.
- A page from the profile's history, or a search typed before, shows a remove button under the pointer that takes it out of the history, and ⌥⌘⌫ (Arc's help centre) or ⇧⌦ (Chrome) takes out the selected one; the row under it takes its place. Default suggestions have none: the search for what's typed, the well-known sites, the Space's tabs listed before anything is typed, sums and commands (Arc's help centre; Dia has the ✕ too). Ours: the button is Lucide's trash, not Arc's ✕, as ✕ closes and trash removes (ux-principles; the owner, 2026-11-02, #1394), in the Switch to Tab arrow's tile, at the row's end, on the selected row too. Off the Mac only ⇧Del does it, as Chrome's, since Ctrl+Alt+Backspace can end an X11 session. ⌘Z puts back what was taken out as it was, a page with each of its visits, so it ranks as before, or a search with its term, and ⇧⌘Z takes it out again: it's a step on the window's history, Remove from History (Undo, everywhere), so it can be undone once the bar has closed too. The bar's field has no undo, so its ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z undo and redo that step while it's the window's next, and nothing else, which would be out of sight under the bar (ours: Arc and Chrome have no undo for it, and a slip of the pointer shouldn't cost a page's history). No toast: the row going under the pointer says what happened. The core keeps the window's last ten removals to put back, as it keeps Clears.
- Bookmarks, a calculator (↩ copies the result) and commands are also rows (Dia).
- **Calculator** (Dia, as Ardia has it). A sum typed, such as "12*7" or "(1+2)^3", shows its result as the row after the first: "= 84". ↩ on it copies "84", closes the bar and says "Copied 84" in the toast.
  - Ours (*interim*): + − × ÷ ^ (or * / **) and parentheses, with . as the decimal point; 12 significant digits, so 0.1+0.2 is 0.3. A date such as 2026-10-26 isn't a sum. Dia's units, functions and its row's look: measure.
- **.new addresses** (owner, 2026-10-29, #698). An address that makes a new document in a service, typed, such as "docs.new" or "meet.new", lists a row naming what it makes, "New Google Doc" with docs.new after it, first, or after the row of an inline completion. ↩ on it opens the site in a new tab at the top of Today, even from ⌘L's bar, which otherwise loads in the tab shown. The row comes once what's typed reaches the address's dot, so "meet" and "linear" stay searches, and only when typed: the rows aren't commands, so the command list and Settings › Shortcuts don't have them. Ours (*interim*), as Arc's bar isn't recorded with them: the addresses in `app/src/notes.rs`, Google's docs, sheets, slides, forms and meet, linear, figma, figjam, replit, and the four New Note picks from (Profiles).
- **Commands** (Arc 1.170.0 cap, ax). Commands share the one list with the suggestions, up to 15 rows with 5 shown and no section headings, from one letter typed: "q" lists Quit Arc. Each has a glyph and, if it has one, its key as text at the row's end. A command whose name or a word of it starts with what's typed comes first, above the search row, unless a site completes inline ("clear": Clear Today ⇧⌘K, the search row, Clear Cache and Refresh, Clear Cookies and Refresh); other strong matches may follow it, and weaker ones come after the suggestions ("split": Add Split View first, Add Top, Left, Right and Bottom Split last). Commands match hidden words too: "duplicate" finds New Window, "hide" Collapse Pinned Tabs, "toggle" Enter Full Screen. Page commands show only with a page. Arc's bar names where its menus differ: Pin Tab, Duplicate Current Tab, Rename Current Tab…, Favorite Tab, Toggle Sidebar, Open General Preferences (⌘,), Edit Keyboard Shortcuts, Mute All Tabs, Switch to Dark Mode and Switch to Light Mode, Exit (⎋). The field's icon becomes the command's.
  - Built: one letter finds a command, by its name or a hidden word, and the commands fill the list to its 15 rows. From three letters, the best goes above the search row when its name or a word of it starts with what's typed, the others whose name or hidden word starts with it come before the core's other rows, and the rest after them; a site completing what's typed puts them all after. The unit tests type Arc's "copy", "clear", "split", "space", "tab", "duplicate", "hide" and "library" and check the rows.
  - Ours (*interim*):
    - The order: a name that starts with what's typed, then a hidden word that does, then a later word of either, then what's typed with its letters in order from a word's start; equals by how often and lately the user picked each in the bar (Arc's `typeTravelUsage`, #1395), then in the table's order (`app/src/shell/commands.rs`), which follows Arc's dumps where they agree. Ours, *interim*: each of a command's last 10 picks counts one, halving each week; with nothing typed the list keeps the table's order, learned by place. They don't always: "archive" lists View Archive… above Archive Tab, while "duplicate" puts Duplicate Current Tab's name above New Window's hidden word. Ours puts the name first. Close Window is the exception: its name counts only once what's typed goes past "close", so "close" and "clos" put Archive Tab first, as Arc's "close" does, and leave Close Window after the suggestions, as ↩ on it would take every tab (#1374).
    - Under three letters every command comes after the suggestions, as Arc's one-letter dumps show none above the search row ("c" lists fifteen sites).
    - "space" puts New Space… first by the rule; Arc's dump, caught while its rows changed, has the Space commands after the suggestions.
    - Hidden words: Arc's seen ones for commands we have ("duplicate" and "copy" find New Window, "copy" Duplicate Current Tab, "close" Archive Tab, "hide" Toggle Sidebar), and ours for the rest, such as "reload" for Refresh the Page, "screenshot" for Capture…, "private" for New Incognito Window and "unpinned tabs" for Clear Today.
    - Names: Arc's bar's for the commands we have, Pin Tab, Favorite Tab, Duplicate Current Tab, Rename Current Tab…, Toggle Sidebar and Hide Sidebar, Open Library…, View Archive…, View Downloads…, Add Split View, Find…, Capture…, New Folder…, New Space…, Manage Extensions… and Quit ilo; ours where Arc's isn't seen: Unpin Tab on a pinned tab, Mute/Unmute Tab, and Turn on and Turn off Developer Mode for this site (Developer Mode). The menu bar keeps its titles, and the Library button's tooltip keeps View Library.
    - Only commands that would do something now are listed: Go Back with history behind, Close this Split Pane in a split, Hide Sidebar and Rename Current Tab… while the sidebar shows, Favorite Tab and Rename Current Tab… on a tab that isn't a favorite, and so on.
    - The chip is the shortcut in 13 pt, at 24 tall, radius 6, white 0.08 and the dim text colour; on the selected row white with accent keys (Panel).
    - Spaces and folders by name (Arc's help centre and bar). Typing a Space's name lists Focus [Space], which shows it, with its ⌃ key if it has one, then Pin to [Space] and Move to [Space], which take the tab shown there as Move to does: pinned at the bottom of its pinned tabs, or at the top of its Today, with the one-time warning for another profile's Space. Pin Tab alone stays the Space shown's. Focus lists every other Space, and Pin to and Move to them, while a tab that isn't a favorite is shown. With rows chosen, a folder among them, Move to [Space] moves them instead, as their menu does, to the other Spaces of their profile, and Pin to isn't listed (ours, #1353). Open [Folder] opens a closed folder of the Space shown, with the folders it's in, and scrolls the sidebar to it. Move to [Folder] (Arc's `moveToFolder`, #1396) pins the tab shown, or the rows chosen, at the bottom of that folder of the Space shown, as their menu's Add to Folder › does, in one undo step; it's listed for each folder but the one the tab shown is in, named by its path as that menu names it ("Move to Reading / Recipes"), never in an agent's Space. The name finds them as a hidden word does, and so does the row's own name ("move"). Ours: none with nothing typed, so the commands-only list starts with the table's commands; Pin to is one undo with its move; the glyphs are Lucide's layers, pin, arrow and open folder, Move to [Folder] taking Move to [Space]'s arrow. Agents switch Spaces and move tabs and folders through the same core calls (bc_window_show_space, bc_tab_move_to_space, bc_folder_move_to_space, bc_folder_set_open, and bc_tab_move and bc_folder_move for a folder), so the rows add no agent path.
    - Go to Sidebar Item [N] (Arc's type-travel items 1 to 9, #1396). A digit 1 to 9 typed with "tab", "item" or the row's name, as "3. tab", "3. item", "item 3" or "go to sidebar item 3", lists one row that shows that sidebar item, as ⌘3 does, the favorites counted or not as Settings › Shortcuts numbers them. Ours: none until the digit is typed and none past the last item; its chip is ⌘1 to ⌘8's, and the ninth has none, since ⌘9 shows the last item where the row's 9 is the ninth.
    - Extensions by name (Arc's `webContentExtension` rows and help centre, #1396). Typing an extension's name lists a row for it, which runs its action on the tab shown as the Extensions menu does, its popup hanging where that one's does: from its button at the URL field's end if it's pinned, else under the field. Listed are the extensions of the Space shown's profile that run in the window, those allowed in Incognito alone in a private one, and only while a tab is shown. Ours (*interim*, Arc's row unrecorded): the row is the extension's name alone, with Lucide's puzzle, Manage Extensions…'s glyph, and no hidden words, so "extension" lists Manage Extensions… and Add Extension… rather than every extension; no chip, even for an extension with a key.
    - Edit Theme… (Arc's bar; decided by the owner, 2026-10-29) opens the theme picker for the Space shown, as its menu's Edit Theme Color… does, the Library going first. "theme" finds it first, as in Arc; ours, "color" and "colour" find it too, and Lucide's palette is its glyph. Settings › Shortcuts doesn't list it, so it has no key.
    - ↩ or a click runs the command. The bar closes first, as for a pick.
    - Mute All Tabs mutes every tab of the window, those silent now too, and Unmute All Tabs, listed while any tab is muted, unmutes them (ours, which tabs: Arc's isn't recorded). Enter Full Screen does what View's item does, and reads Exit Full Screen in full screen, as that item does; "toggle" finds both. Exit (⎋) is Arc's View › Exit, which leaves a page's full screen or the window's: listed while the window is full screen, for itself or for a page, it takes it out, as Exit Full Screen does, and the page with it (ours: ilo's View has no Exit of its own). New Profile is the Spaces menu's Change Profile › New Profile: listed while the Space shown is empty, as only an empty Space changes profile, it asks for the name in the dialog. No agent path for any of them: muting, full screen and profiles are the user's, and agents switch none of them.
    - Not yet: Arc's commands we don't have, waiting on their features.
- **Commands only** (Arc's help centre). Tab in an empty field, or "/ " typed, lists only the commands, which what's typed next filters as it does the list's commands. Arc took away the hint for it in March 2024 and kept the key. Ours (*interim*, Arc's look unrecorded):
  - With nothing typed it lists every command that does something now, in the table's order, scrolling past five; typed, the commands the bar would list for it, best first, with none of the core's rows. ↩ runs the one selected, and with none does nothing.
  - "Commands" is a chip before the input, as a site search's engine is, the placeholder "Search Commands…" and the field's icon the command glyph until one is selected.
  - ⌫ at the start, ⇧Tab or Esc leaves it, putting "/ " back before what's typed if that entered it, as a site search puts its keyword back, and the bar stays open.
  - Not over a split's new pane or for Search Tabs, which list no commands; there Tab moves the selection.
