- Well-known sites that what's typed could start, such as YouTube for "you", fill the rows the profile's own leave, under them and never as row 0, so a new profile's list isn't bare. The list is built in and ranked by popularity alone (ours).
- **Another Space** (Arc's help centre). Another Space shown while the bar is open, as its keys (⌃1 to ⌃9, ⌥⌘→ ⌥⌘←) show one, rebuilds the rows for it and its profile: its open tabs, and the suggestions for what's typed asked again of that profile. The rows from before go at once, and a completion from them, so a pick never goes to the Space before or navigates in its profile (decision 5). Ours, where Arc's isn't recorded: what's typed stays; the URL ⌘L opened the bar on goes, and ↩ then opens a new tab, as the tab it edited is the other Space's; a site search leaves, putting back the word typed before Tab, as the profile's engines may differ; Search Tabs lists the open tabs from the Space shown first; the bar over a split's new pane closes, as the split view stays behind. Only the window in front has the bar, so a Space another window shows, as an agent's, doesn't move it.
- The row under the pointer has the hover plate and keeps it as the keys move the selection; ↩ takes the selection, and a click the row clicked (Arc 1.170 rec; Panel).

**Inline completion** (Chrome; Dia rec, O)

- It completes only one term, with the caret at the end.
- It never completes during IME composition, and ↩ then commits the composition instead of navigating. It completes again after the composition is committed.
- It never completes after a deletion (⌫, ⌦, ⌥⌫, ⌘⌫ or cut) until the next typed character. After a deletion, the search row goes first.
- It never completes right after a paste, or when the input ends in whitespace.
- Hosts complete first; paths only when the typed text contains "/". The typed prefix keeps its case.
- Only hosts the user has typed are eligible: typed once for a host, twice for a deeper URL. Bookmark-only and open-tab-only hosts are listed but don't inline (Chrome).
- → or ⌘→ accepts the completion, and so does a click at its end. A ⌫ after accepting deletes one character and doesn't complete again.

**Site search** (Chrome's keyword mode, O; Arc's sites, trigger and colour, #1397)

- A new profile has Arc's 19 site searches, put once into Chromium's keywords table as active, so one the user deletes or turns off stays that way: Google Drive, Gmail, Google Maps, Google Calendar, Google Docs, Dropbox, Amazon, GitHub, Figma, X (x.com, and twitter.com too), YouTube, Wikipedia, Reddit, SoundCloud, Asana, LinkedIn, Apple Music and Spotify, each one's keyword its site's host (core/site_searches.cc). An engine the profile already has with that keyword keeps its page, and one Chromium made by itself is turned on. The engines Chromium adds as the user visits sites stay inactive and never hint, as in Arc. A private window uses its profile's. The list is the owner's to trim.
- A word typed that starts an active engine's keyword, two characters or more, case aside, hints it on the first row, whatever that row completes to, as Arc's hint follows the host: "twit" hints X while it completes twitch.tv, and "gm" hints nothing, as Gmail's keyword is mail.google.com. Of several, the shortest keyword, as Chrome ranks them; one letter hints none, which would hint one for nearly anything typed (ours). Typing with a space keeps Chrome's hint, on the row whose first word is the keyword or whose page is on the engine's site.
- Tab on the selected row, if it hints, searches with that engine alone, keeping what was typed after the first word. Only Tab, as Arc's (its space trigger is off, and was migrated away): a space after the keyword is only a space, so "github issues" stays a plain search.
- ⌫ with the caret at the start, or ⇧Tab, leaves it and puts back the word typed before Tab, not the keyword it completed to, as Arc's `queryBeforeEnteringScopedSearch`: "yout" comes back "yout", and anything typed since follows after a space, with the caret between them. Left untouched, the field reads as it did before Tab.
- Esc leaves it for the bar as it opened, as Arc's `postScopedSearchRestoration`: nothing typed and the Space's tabs for ⌘T, the URL ⌘L edits, selected. A second Esc closes the bar.
- In the search, the engine's name is a chip before the input, whose placeholder is "Search…", as Arc's, since the chip names the site; VoiceOver hears the field as "Search <engine>". The chip and the selected row are in the site's own colour (tokens.toml › command-bar.site-colors), as Arc's pill and rows are, moved along its lightness until it reads on the bar at 3:1 with Ink or Paper on it at 4.6:1. GitHub's, X's and Wikipedia's marks are black and white, so they, and the engines the user adds, keep the chip's plate and the Space's accent. Ours: the chip keeps its size and radius, and has no glow, as glows are for thresholds (UX principles), where Arc's pill is 73 × 23, round, with a glow in its colour.
- It lists only the search for what's typed, as Arc's does: not the site's searches from history the core also gives, nor commands, sums or well-known sites. Nothing is listed until something is typed, and ↩ does nothing until then.
- The selected row that hints shows "Search <engine>" dimmed and a Tab key at the input's end, as Arc's hint (Panel).
- **Settings** (ours, #1636). From three letters typed, the bar lists the settings they name as "Peek — Settings", each with its section's glyph, at most three, after the commands that come early and before the core's other rows. It finds them as Settings' search does, by the starts of the words of their names, the names they're also known by and keys, from the same index: what Settings' search reads of its rows, which the bar reads once each time it opens, from its first suggestions. Shortcuts' rows stay out, as the bar lists each command itself with its keys, and a row's line isn't read. ↩ on one opens Settings on its section, scrolled to the row, which flashes once, as a click on a found section's name does. It never changes the setting itself, so a switch's row opens Settings at the switch. The rows come from the user's keyboard alone: agents get no settings tools, and no MCP tool lists or opens them.
- **Site Search Settings** (Arc's help centre). The bar's "Site Search Settings" command, found by "site search", "search engines" or "keywords", opens Chromium's search engines page (chrome://settings/searchEngines) in a new tab, where the profile's site searches are added and edited, each a name, a keyword and a URL with %s for the query. It's Arc's own place for them, arc://settings/searchEngines, and ilo opens Chromium's history and extensions pages the same way, so a Settings section of ours would be more to build for the same form. A private window doesn't offer it, as it keeps no engines of its own, and agents have no path to it, as they get no pages outside http(s). Not built: Arc's toast that suggests a site's search and turns it on in every profile.

**Keys**

| Key | Action | Source |
|---|---|---|
| ↩ | open where the bar shows pages: a new tab for ⌘T, the tab for ⌘L. On the URL ⌘L or the click opened it on, as it opened, it loads the page again rather than going to it (Arc's help centre) | Chrome, Arc |
| ⌘↩ | a tab behind the one shown, at the top of Today, which loads once first shown; the bar stays open. A command runs as for ↩ | Arc 1.170.0 cap |
| ⌥↩ | a new pane on the right of the page, in a split view | Arc 1.170.0 cap |
| ⇧↩ | where ↩ goes, in a new window made as ⌘N makes one, a private one from a private window. A command runs as for ↩, and over a new pane it's ↩ (*interim*; whether Arc opens a window, Little Arc or Peek: measure) | Chrome |
| ⇧⌘↩ | search for what was typed, even if it names a page | Dia |
| ⌃↩ / ⌃⇧↩ | www.<word>.com for a word typed, else as ↩ / the same in a new window | Chrome |
| ↑ ↓, ⌃N ⌃P | move, wrapping at the ends | Chrome |
| ⇞ ⇟ | first / last row | Chrome |
| Tab / ⇧Tab | enter site search when hinted, or in an empty field the commands-only list, else ask an agent when one is set up (Agent task), else the next row / leave either, else the previous row | Chrome, Arc |
| Esc | leave site search for the bar as it opened; otherwise close the panel, drop the typed text and give the page its focus back | Ardia, Arc |
| ⇧⌦, ⌥⌘⌫ | take the selected row's page or search out of the history; the row under it takes its place | Chrome, Arc |

- What's typed or pasted right after ⌘T, ⌘L or ⌘F, ⌘V, ⌘C, ⌘X or ⌘A too, waits up to 400 ms for the new field and lands there in order, never in the old page (Ardia, from a user report). Ours: a composition goes whole to the one field. A page that keeps the shortcut, as Google Docs keeps ⌘F, gets the keys when the 400 ms are up.
- A click anywhere outside the panel, or another window or app taking focus, closes it like Esc; the click still does its own thing.
- **URLs** (Chrome's `AutocompleteInput::Parse`). A dotted host without a scheme goes to https, falling back to http. Single labels, localhost, IP addresses and `.local` hosts go to http.
- A right-click in a text field shows Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All. In the command bar's field it adds Paste and Go and Paste and Search, which go where ↩ and ⇧⌘↩ would for the copied text, a line break in it read as a space. Nothing reads the clipboard until one is chosen, so they're there whatever it holds, and macOS never sees an app read it unasked.
- Every field of ours (the command bar's, rename, find, Settings) takes a Mac text field's keys and clicks: ⌥← and ⌥→ go a word, with ⇧ selecting; ⇧⌘←, ⇧⌘→, ⇧Home and ⇧End select to an end, and ⌃A and ⌃E go there; ⌥⌫ and ⌥⌦ delete a word. A double-click selects the word, the spaces or the mark under it, and a drag from it goes by words; a triple-click selects the whole field. A word is AppKit's: a run holding a letter, number, mark or symbol, so example.com and can't are one (NSAttributedString, measured, #1385). Ours: Japanese and Chinese go a character at a time, as there's no dictionary; a password field's moves go to its ends and a double-click selects all of it, as a Mac's does. Off the Mac Ctrl does what ⌥ does and Alt+← stays Back, as in Chrome; on Windows Ctrl+→ and Ctrl+Delete go to the next word's start, and a double-click takes the spaces after a word, as Windows' fields do.
- The field holds the page's whole address, its scheme too, so a copy of the URL keeps its https://, as Arc's adds it (Arc's help centre).
- **Copy links without tracking** (Dia; the lists are ours, in `app/src/shell/clean_link.rs`, #2351). Every copy of a link leaves out what only tells a site where a click came from: ⇧⌘C and as Markdown, the tab, folder and chosen rows' menus, the command bar, the Library and the share sheet. Everywhere that's the utm_ parameters and the click IDs ad and mail services add, such as fbclid and gclid. A site's own share IDs go on that site alone, by a table of sites and names, each row naming its source: si on YouTube, igsh on Instagram, s and t on X and Twitter. A name goes only where it's known to be a tracker, so a name nobody listed always stays: t is a video's time on YouTube and stays there, as do a #t= fragment, v, list, index, a search's q and a signed link's token. The table grows by PR, each row with its source and a test. Settings › Privacy's Copy links without tracking, on by default, turns it off: every copy path then gives the address as it is. The URL field's own text, selected and copied by hand, always copies whole. Links aren't cleaned as they open, and no modifier key copies one whole.

### New tab

⌘T opens the command bar over the page shown and makes no tab until a pick (Arc; Command bar › Opening). With no page shown, the card holds ilo's mark over a line that says so, the URL field the bar's placeholder, and a click on either does the same (Window › Card, no page). Dia's New Tab page isn't ours (decision 6): its box is the assistant's chat field, and its empty card holds the answers. Its measurements and intro are in D›New Tab page and D›1.50 New Tab intro.

### Split view

Arc 1.170.0's values where measured (cap, ax, rec), Dia's (P§6, D›Dragging tabs and split targets) for the rest.

- **Panes.** Up to 3 (Dia), the core's cap. Arc's maximum is unmeasured: an outside reading infers 4 from its strings, which isn't enough to change it. A page asked to join a full split, by ⌃+, an ⌥-click on its row or New Note Split, doesn't, and Arc's toast "Cannot Add New Pane" says why for 2 s over the focused pane (*interim*: Arc's time isn't measured). Side by side, or stacked top to bottom after Convert to Vertical Split View, 10 pt apart, sharing the card. Each has its own page, history and docked DevTools. The focused one has a 2 pt ring inside its edge and a ≈ 8 pt glow outside it (Arc), in the Space's colour, else the accent. Arc's read P3 (49, 57, 251) in the one capture, which doesn't show whether it follows the Space's colour. Unfocused panes aren't dimmed (*interim*, measure). A page in full screen takes the window alone.
- **Focus.** A click in a pane focuses it, and its page has the keyboard at once, as a new pane's page does (Arc). The URL field is the focused pane's, as Arc's address bar is its panel's (Arc help): it shows that page's URL, and ⌘L, a click on it and Copy URL act on that page. Arc's toolbar, with each panel's URL, isn't ours, as the card has no toolbar (Sidebar). Close this Split Pane closes the focused pane, and the one beside it takes focus, and so does ⌘W in a Today split. In a pinned split ⌘W unloads every page and the row stays a split, as its − does (Arc 1.171), and the window shows the next tab as after a pinned tab's ⌘W. A Today tab's pane closes its tab, into the archive. A pinned tab's pane only leaves the split, unloaded, its row under the split, as ⌘W keeps a pinned tab's row (Pinned tabs): its place is the user's (ux-principles › Everything has a place). ⌘Z and ⇧⌘T put either back in its pane (Closed tabs). Separate Page from Split View puts the focused pane in its own row under the split, shown alone. The find bar, the zoom HUD, toasts and a tab's dialogs, such as print preview, go over the focused pane.
- **Split controls.** Each pane has a handle of three faint dots centred over it, in the window's margin over the card or, stacked, in the gap under the pane above, on a plate under the pointer. A click shows Convert to Vertical Split View (Horizontal on a stacked split), Separate Page from Split View and Close this Split Pane, acting on that pane whether it's focused or not, as Arc's ⋯ does (Arc help). Their look, place and that they show at rest are ours, as Arc's aren't measured (*interim*). A page in full screen has none.
- **Sizes.** The gap between two panes drags them wider and narrower, or taller and shorter stacked, under a resize cursor. Each keeps a tenth of the card, no less than 50 pt and no more than 200, and the gap snaps to even within 15 pt (Chrome's, *interim*: Arc's limits and the divider's motion aren't measured). A double-click on the gap evens them, as one on the sidebar's edge puts its width back. Sizes go with places, not tabs: Flip and Move Pane keep them where they were, a change of orientation turns them onto the new axis, and a tab joining or leaving evens them.
- **In the sidebar.** A split is one row of segments with a 1 pt line between them: each segment's favicon (18) 7 into it, its title 10 after that, ending 5 before the next line (Arc). Arc's segments are equal, so at a usual sidebar width every pane's title is cut to a few letters. Ours fold: the pane with the keyboard has the row's width past the others and its whole title, and the others fold to their favicon, with their speaker and loading dot 4 after it, so a folded pane still says it plays or loads (#2340).
  - Two panes fold too.
  - While the split isn't shown, the row keeps the pane that had the keyboard last, the one ⌘1–⌘9 and a click on the row bring back.
  - A click on a folded segment shows its pane focused and unfolds it at once.
  - A change made elsewhere, from the page or a key, waits while the pointer is on the row, so the segment the user aims at doesn't move from under it.
  - The segments trade widths over 120 ms on CSS's ease, not a spring, which would overshoot and show the next favicon past its line. The title keeps its whole width while it moves, so the segment's edge cuts it and it fades, rather than its ellipsis running along. Reduce Motion folds at once.
  - A tab dropped on the row takes its width at once, since that's what the row would show.
  - A split with a name or an icon is one plate, below, with nothing to fold, and a favorite's tile keeps its segments equal, as a tile has no titles.
  - What it costs, against Arc's equal row: the ✕ under the pointer takes its room from the wide segment, so the folded ones move left by its width; in a narrow sidebar with three panes the wide title is short; and the other panes' titles aren't readable at a glance, only their favicons. Three panes going from no readable title to one is worth all three.
  A split given a name or an icon is one plate instead, as Arc's: the icon, an emoji, else Lucide's columns-2 (rows-2 while stacked) in the secondary ink, where a tab's favicon is, then the name, else its pages' titles joined by commas; a click anywhere on it shows the pane focused last. The row counts as one for ⌘1–⌘9, ⌃Tab and next or previous tab, which show the pane focused last. Its ✕ closes all its tabs. A pinned split's − unloads them, and the row stays a split. With ⌥ held over either, it turns to Lucide's ungroup, and a click takes every tab out of the split, closing none, as Arc's ⌥-click on it does; the glyph follows ⌥ as the pointer moves, since GPUI hears no key while the page has the keyboard (ours: Arc's glyph isn't recorded). Moving or pinning the row moves the split, and so do Move to and Move to Space: the split goes to the other Space whole, joined again there in its order and orientation, and one ⌘Z brings it back (Arc 1.171). Into another profile's Space its pages arrive as new tabs, each in its own row, with nothing to undo, as a tab alone has. A split made with a pinned tab is pinned, as Arc keeps a split made in the pinned section pinned: a tab joining a pinned tab's split is pinned at its page (the core's bc_tab_split_with), and a pinned tab joining a Today tab's split takes that split into its own place in the pinned tabs, pinned at its page, so no split takes a pin away (ours). A tab joining a favorite shown, by the page's targets, ⌥-click or the hover card, joins a copy of the favorite at the top of Today, which shows, as Arc 1.170's did, since no Space holds a favorite: a pinned tab or another favorite joins as a copy too, both originals staying in their places (Arc), and a Today tab moves into it, as it does to join any other (ours: Arc's isn't recorded). ⌘Z closes the copies and puts a Today tab back. A pinned tab dropped on that split then follows the rule above where Arc's added another copy (ours: the split is Today's, and its pin is the user's to keep). Its menu is Arc 1.171's, from either segment: Separate All Tabs; Add Tab…, dimmed while the split is full or closed; Convert to Vertical Split View, or to Horizontal when stacked; then a Tab Options header (11 pt semibold in the menu's secondary ink, not a row) over a submenu per page, titled with it: Separate from Split View, Share ›, Mute, Copy Link with its ⌥ Markdown alternate, and Archive Item, or on a pinned page Back to Pinned URL and Pinned URL › in its place. Then Change Icon… and Rename…, which give the split its own icon and name, as do a double-click on its row and the command bar's Rename Tab from either page: the field starts with the split's name, else empty, an emptied ↩ takes the name off, and the picker's trash the icon. The name and icon go with the split through a quit, ⇧⌘T, Undo Clear, Move to and Duplicate, and go when it separates, which ⌘Z gives back; Duplicate, which copies the whole split in its order and orientation; Move to ›; Archive Item, which archives every page, or takes a pinned split out of the pinned tabs into the archive, as a pinned row's remove button does, which ⌘Z pins again; and in Today, Archive Tabs Below. Arc's Share Split View… needs Arc's servers, and its Pin item is ⌘D and a drag here.
- **As a favorite.** Arc's favorite can be a split view, which keeps its focused pane across Space switches, and its icon can be changed (Arc release notes). Ours is one favorite, one place among the tiles and one of the 12, counted once by ⌘1–⌘9. Its tile, as wide as the others, has each pane's favicon in a segment of its own, all as wide, each after the first behind the split row's 1 pt line, 16 tall; a segment narrower than a favicon and 6, as three panes in a 47 pt tile are, takes a 12 pt favicon. While one of its pages shows, it has the default ring, as no one icon themes it. The split keeps the pane focused last, whichever Space it was focused in: a click on the tile, its ⌘ key and a Space that shows it show it focused there, and a click in a pane focuses that one. Its hover card has the split's pages side by side in its picture's box, 4 apart, the pane focused last ringed 1 pt in the Space's colour, that pane's title and every pane's host in their order, and ★, which takes the whole split out (ours, from the mock the owner took: Arc's tile and card aren't recorded, #689). Made by dragging the split's row onto the tiles, or Add to Favorites on a pane; Remove from Favorites, or the tile dragged into the list, puts it there whole, a split as it looked, pinned among the pinned tabs, and ⌘Z makes it a favorite again. Separate All Tabs in the tile's menu leaves its first pane the favorite and puts the others at the top of Today, with no ⌘Z yet (*interim*: the tabs stay, so nothing is lost). The tile's other menu items, Back to Favorite URL, Replace Favorite URL with Current and Duplicate Tab, act on its first pane (*interim*: the split row's per-pane Tab Options isn't on the tile yet). ⌘W, or Close Tab in its menu, unloads every pane and keeps the tile, as a pinned split's − does. It comes back after a quit, focused where it was. Under Each Space each Space has its own copy, and under Every Space each profile its own tabs of the same sites, focused where that copy was; the scopes match a split only with a split of the same pages in the same order. Its icon can be changed, the emoji taking the segments' place.
- **Creating a split.** Add Split View: in one frame the page narrows, an empty pane opens on the right and the command bar opens over it, its panel ending 70 in from the card's right edge (Arc). A URL opens in the pane; a tab picked joins the split. ⌥↩ in the command bar opens its URL in a right pane (Arc). Dragging a tab from the sidebar onto the page shows the targets, below. Dragged onto another tab's row, it joins that tab on the side of the row the pointer is on, the halves split at the plate's middle (Arc's sidebar drop regions, Arc 1.171): ours, until Arc's is measured, once the pointer has rested 0.4 s on the middle half of the row, so passing rows still reorders. The row then shows the split it would make, with its hover fill and no drop line: its tab's segment and a slot for the dragged one, 3 in from the segment's edges at radius 9 in the hover fill, holding that tab's favicon and title at the secondary opacity, and the ghost goes into it (ours: Arc's preview isn't recorded). The pointer crossing the middle moves the slot to the other side, a detent at each change. Within the 24 pt at the sidebar's edge where a row pages to another Space it rests on no row, and a rest begun before it got there ends, so the wait to page isn't cut short by a split (Dragging to another Space). ⌥-click on another tab's row joins it to the page shown, on its right, and on the page shown alone splits it with a copy of itself, and on a split's tab or segment takes that tab out of the split (Arc). On a row it splits as the press is let go, if the pointer hasn't moved, so the press can start a drag instead (Reorder: ⌥ held as it's let go drops a copy). ⌥-click on "+ New Tab" adds a pane as Add Split View does; Dia's opens its New Tab page in the pane, which isn't ours (New tab). ⌥-click on a link opens it in a pane on the right, focused, as Arc's does, and so does the page menu's Open Link in Split View (Chrome); ⌥⌘-click opens it in Little ilo (Keyboard › Mouse). In a split with three panes the link opens as a tab of its own.
- **Commands.** Arc's View menu: Add Split View ⌃+, Close this Split Pane ⌃⇧-, Separate Page from Split View, and Expand Current Split, which leaves the other pane 220 pt wide at any card width and gives the focused one the rest, in one frame; pressed again it does nothing, and it's kept after a quit (Arc 1.170.0 ax, rec). Ours, where nothing's recorded: it's in the command bar and the View menu after Developer, with no key; it works on the pane focused when it's run, so a split expanded and then focused on the other pane stays as it was until run again; with three panes each of the others keeps 220 pt, and on a card too short for that the others take an even share at most, so the focused pane is never the smaller (an even split at the narrowest); the way out is the divider, dragged or double-clicked to even. The core keeps it as a flag on the pane that took the card and works the widths out at every layout, so the others stay 220 pt as the window or the sidebar changes the card's width; it ends when the divider is dragged or double-clicked, or a pane joins or leaves, and it's saved with the session, not as shares. Agents split their own Space's tabs through the same core calls a user's split makes, with `split_tabs`, `add_to_split`, `separate_tab` and `list_split`, each with its permission rule and activity-log entry (#1597); Expand Current Split, the pane sizes and the orientation have no agent path, as how the panes sit in the card is the user's. Arc's ⌃⇧] / ⌃⇧[ switch split views, not panes (Keyboard). Dia's Flip, Change Orientation, Move Pane Left/Right and Separate All are in the command bar, kept out of Arc's menu, as Flip Split View, Move Pane Left and Move Pane Right, and under Arc's names where its split menu has them (Arc 1.171): Convert to Vertical Split View stacks the panes and Convert to Horizontal Split View puts them back side by side, the bar listing the one that would turn the split, and Separate All Tabs. Dia's names find them. The focused page stays focused. In a stacked split, left is up (*interim*: Dia's names for them there aren't recorded). ⌃⇧] and ⌃⇧[ focus the next and previous pane (Dia, *interim*).
- **Kept.** A split comes back after a quit, stacked if it was, at its sizes or still expanded, and so does one brought back by ⇧⌘T or Undo Clear, at even sizes.

**Drag targets** ("Add left split" and "Add right split" at the card's sides, "Add top split" and "Add bottom split" at its top and bottom)

| Stage | Size (fraction of the card) | From the card's side | Look |
|---|---|---|---|
| At rest | 198.5 × 333.5 (0.132 × 0.383), centred vertically | 37.5 | radius ≈ 20; fill a step lighter than the page with a lighter 0.5 pt edge; a dashed rounded rect 9.5 inside it (6 pt dashes, 6 pt gaps, ≈ 1.75 pt wide); a ≈ 19 pt half-filled-rectangle glyph above a 13 pt semibold title at white ≈ 0.6 |
| Grown, right after sliding in | 322 × 532 (0.215 × 0.611) | 30 | the same |
| Under the dragged card | 362 × 600 (0.241 × 0.69); its centre leans 43 % of the way toward the pointer's x | — | fill the accent at ≈ 0.15; dashes, glyph and title in the accent; 7 pt dashes and gaps, ≈ 2 pt wide |

- **One at a time (Arc 1.170, live).** Arc opens one region, at the card's edge nearest the pointer, where Dia shows every target. Ours shows one of Dia's targets there: nearest as a fraction of the card's width or height, so the card's diagonals split it, and on a tie the side before the top. As the pointer comes nearer another edge, that one slides in in its place, unless the tab is over the one shown, which stays until the pointer leaves it. Arc also scales the window's content to ≈ 0.92 while its region is up. That isn't built: the page's view is the core's to scale (#1398).
- **Timing.** The targets slide in from outside the card's edges in ≈ 110 ms, then grow over ≈ 120 ms with no overshoot. In the top strip they appeared when the pointer was 26 pt below the strip. For the sidebar, measure the trigger in Arc.
- **What the drag looks like.** For a frame or two the tab itself follows the pointer. Then it becomes a 209 × 109.5 picture of a window, centred on the pointer, with a 0.5 pt light rim, a shadow and traffic lights. Over another window's tab list it turns back into a tab in ≈ 50 ms. Ardia then opens a tab-wide gap at the pointer in 120 ms; Dia's: measure.
- **The content shrinks back.** While a tab or a link is over the page, Arc 1.170 draws the window's pages at ≈ 0.92 of their size about the card's centre, the card and the targets with them, and at their size again once it isn't (live, not measured frame by frame).
- **Drop.** On a target, the page splits and the new pane slides in on that side (timing: measure). Away from a target, the tab opens in its own window, which grows out of the card at the pointer in ≈ 200 ms and takes focus. A pane leaves its split the same way. Dragging the only tab on a page splits it with the tab used before it.
- **Ours, until Arc's are measured:**
  - The content's scale takes 230 ms each way, the targets' slide and grow, on their ease-out cubic. The pages are drawn smaller, never laid out again (`bc_window_set_page_scale`), and the window shows round them. The side panel keeps its size. Under Reduce Motion the content keeps its size. On Windows the pages keep their size for now, as each is a window of its own there (*interim*).
  - The targets come once the pointer is 26 pt past the card's left edge, Dia's distance from its top strip. They go when it's back over the sidebar, and the tab is a row again.
  - A target is under the tab while the pointer is in it. It stays so until the pointer leaves the bigger target it grows to, and grows and shrinks over 120 ms on an ease-out cubic.
  - The look: a fill white 0.06 over the window's, a 0.5 pt edge of white 0.14, and dashes of white 0.25.
  - The picture of the window: radius 10 with a white 0.25 rim, traffic lights 6 pt, and the tab's favicon and title on a page under them. Dia's picture has the page's image.
  - The tab becomes the picture at once, not after a frame or two.
  - On a drop, the split shows at once, focused on the tab dropped. Away from a target, the tab opens at once in a window as big as this one, centred on the pointer, with no grow, and ⌘Z sends it back, as a tab let go off the window does. A private or Little ilo window's tab goes back to its row.
  - While the targets are up, the page under them is the one the tab would join. Where the press showed the tab in that page's place, the page shows again as they come, and the tab once they go without it let go on one: back over the sidebar or let go off them (#1467).
  - A split has one orientation, so a page alone can show any of the four targets, a split side by side the left or right one, and a stacked split the top or bottom one. Let go on the top or bottom one, a page alone stacks with the tab (Arc).
  - The top and bottom targets are the side ones turned, each as long as 0.28 of the card's width at rest, 0.45 grown and 0.51 under the tab, so they fit between the side targets, and their centre leans toward the pointer's y.
  - Targets are at the card's sides only, not at each pane's. Favorites, folders and split rows get none, and neither does a tab of the split shown or a split that is full.
  - **A link dragged over the page** (Arc help): the same targets come at once, from the page or from another app, with the system's picture of the link under the pointer, not the window's. On a target the drag is the browser's and the page doesn't see it; off them the page has it, so a field or a drop zone in the page still takes the link. Let go on one, the link opens in a new tab joining the page shown on that side, focused. Only an http or https link, and only while the page shown has room for another pane. The core takes the drag through a hook in Chromium's drag destinations (`engine/patches/ours/chromium-core-page-link-drag.patch`).

### Settings

ilo's own window, opened by ⌘, (Ctrl+, off the Mac) and ilo › Settings…: its sections run down a column on the window's material, as the sidebar's rows do, and the section shown sits on a card, as a page does (#1636). It stays a window of its own, as it's the browser's rather than a Space's or a window's, and the user changes a setting and then tries it in the browser window beside it. The column and card are the sidebar's and the page's, so the hands know them, and the headings are Outfit, the Library's, so it reads as ilo's and not as System Settings. Arc's toolbar of icon tabs and its colours go (owner, 2026-11-03).

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Window | 820 × 660 at first, resizable from 720 × 480; close only (minimize and zoom off); centred over the browser window the first time. It follows View › Appearance, light or dark. ⌘W and the close button hide it, and it opens again on the section shown last. Its title is the section's name |
| Column | 208 wide on the window's material (Linux and Windows: the sidebar's stand-in), the window's buttons in its first 48, then the search field, then 12 under it the sections in three groups 12 apart: General, Tabs & Sidebar, Features, Agents, Links, Shortcuts; Profiles, Passwords, Privacy; Sync, Import & Export, About. Each a sidebar row's plate with its Lucide glyph, lit under the pointer and on the selected plate while shown. At its foot, 8 up, Chromium settings with the external-link glyph opens chrome://settings in a tab and hides the window; where the window is too short for all twelve, as at its 720 × 480 least, they scroll above it. A press on the column's ground moves the window |
| Card | 10 from the window's top, right and bottom, at the card's radius; dark, the overlay fill with the card's rim; light, the dialog fill with its ring and shadow. It scrolls, its foot fading into its fill 28 tall while rows go on below, and once scrolled the section's name holds at its top, 44 tall, in body semibold over a hairline, followed by › and the last heading scrolled under it, as Import & Export › Import, in the heading's colour |
| Search | The Library's field, 36 tall at radius 12 on a 0.09 plate, 8 in from the column's sides: the search glyph, "Search", and at its end ⌘F (Ctrl+F off the Mac) in grey until typing starts, then ✕ on an 18 circle, which empties it. ⌘F puts the keyboard in it from anywhere in Settings. It matches as typed, by the starts of words in any order, a row's name, the names it's also known by (Peek is also "link preview", Mini Player "picture in picture" and "pip", Little ilo "little arc" and "small window", Block ads and trackers "ad blocker" and "content blocker", Relay calls "webrtc" and "ip leak", Reopen your windows and tabs "restore session") and a command's keys, named as words or glyphs, so "cmd t" finds ⌘T and ⇧⌘T. Only when no name, other name or keys has the words are the rows' lines read, so "archive" finds the five settings named by it and not every line that mentions the archive. It reads the sections of settings: General, Tabs & Sidebar, Features, Agents, Shortcuts, Privacy and Import & Export; the user's own things, their profiles, logins, link rules and account, are found where they're listed. The rows found replace the card's page, live, so a switch flips right there: each run of them from one section under its name, a feature's own settings under "Features › Little ilo", the headings at the page's spacing, and the buttons under a section's groups, as Shortcuts' Restore Defaults, under its last. While it finds rows the column lights no plate and dims the sections with nothing found to 0.35. A click on a group's section name empties the field and shows the section scrolled to its first row, under the held title if it was out of sight, and that row's plate flashes once, to 0.12 and back over 600 ms. A click on a section in the column empties it too. "Nothing found" in one grey line when nothing matches. Esc empties the field and shows the section again; a view inside that takes Esc, as the import's, keeps it. The command bar finds settings from the same index (Command bar › Settings). Agents have no path to it |
| Keyboard | Every control takes the keyboard (#1526). ⇥ and ⇧⇥ go through them in reading order: the sections down the column, then the search field, then each row's controls on the card top to bottom (switches, pop-ups, buttons such as Rename, a shortcut's chip and ↺, and the views' own, such as the import's), then Chromium settings; past the last, ⇥ goes round to the first. In the column ↑ and ↓ move between sections, staying at the list's ends, as the sidebar's keys do (Focus Sidebar), and Space or ↩ shows the section. Space or ↩ acts on the control that has the keyboard, as a click does: a switch flips, a button runs, a pop-up opens its menu, whose ↑, ↓, ↩ and ⎋ are the menu's own; ⎋ closes the menu and nothing else, the pop-up keeping the keyboard. The control with the keyboard carries Focus Sidebar's ring, `shell.keyed-ring` wide inside its plate in the ink at `shell.keyed`, while a key put it there; a click shows none. Settings opens holding the keyboard itself, so typing a character starts the search with it, as a type-to-search list does, while ⌘W, ⌘Z and Esc are Settings' and its views' as they were; a press on a control clicks it and leaves the keyboard with the view around it, the import, Links or Settings, as before controls took the keyboard, which only ⇥ gives them; one that had it and goes gives it back to Settings, or to the import while it shows; opened at a row, from a found section's name or the command bar's settings rows, the row's control has it. A text field's ⇥ moves on, as the browser's fields' does |
| Page | 36 in from the card's sides and 26 from its top, at most 632 wide. The section's name in Outfit 20, then one sentence in body grey saying what it's for, then the groups 20 under it, 10 apart. A heading in body medium goes 20 under the group before and 7 over its own; a group of one row needs none. Text keeps one column 14 inside the groups' plates |
| Rows | At least 48 tall, a control's 24 and the padding, so even with and without one; the name 12 from the top and bottom, a grey line 4 under it, on 16, in the grey a task card's status takes, as is the page's sentence (#1636), controls 20 after the text and 8 apart; a hairline between rows from the text to 14 short of the plate's end. A feature's row leads with its glyph on a quiet 28 plate, 12 before its name, and its own settings sit under it in its group, starting where its name does. A row whose control fills it, as a shortcut's chip, is 38 tall. No coloured tiles: the accent shows only where something is on |
| Controls | Ours, all of them brightening under the pointer and a step more pressed. Switch 30 × 18 with a 14 knob 2 in, the paper's in dark and white in light, white on the accent, sliding over 150 ms. Pop-up buttons and buttons 24 tall at the control radius, a button's title 10 in, a pop-up 155 wide (Share favorites' 195) with its chevrons 5 from its end, opening its menu over itself with the chosen item on its text. Buttons under a group go 11 below it |

A setting each profile keeps sits in its topic's section. With one profile it's one plain row; with more, it shows once per profile, the profile's name in grey before its control. A new profile starts at 12 hours, and its own row changes it. Agents get no path to any of them: the core refuses an agent's `profile_settings`, with why.

- **General.** "How ilo opens, quits and looks." While ilo isn't the default browser, the sentence says so and Make Default sits beside the heading, asking macOS, which asks the user; once it is, both go. The way back is macOS's own, System Settings › Desktop & Dock. The row reads macOS's setting again when Settings shows and when the app becomes active, and agents get no way to change it. Opening and quitting: Reopen your windows and tabs, on (Several windows says what off does), its line saying what off brings; Ask before quitting, on (Window › Warn before quitting). Look: Appearance, a pop-up of Automatic, Light and Dark, then Page zoom, a pop-up of Chrome's choices from 50% to 200%, 100% by default, every profile's pages' default zoom, a site zoomed with ⌘+ keeping its own (#1636); the same state as View › Appearance and the command bar's, which every window takes; on the Mac, Dock icon takes the Space's color, on (Dock dot), and on Windows Taskbar button takes the Space's color, with none on Linux, which draws no dot: off, the dot goes back to green at once and no Space switch swaps it, sparing the swap's 10 to 45 ms, and on again it takes the key window's Space's color. Search and downloads: each profile's Search engine, a pop-up of the engines Chrome offers as a default, which the command bar searches with in its Spaces (none while a policy or an extension picks it); and its Save downloads to, the folder with ~ for the home folder and Change…, which asks in the system's panel and sets Chrome's download location for that profile; then Ask where to save each file, off, Chrome's own, which every profile takes (#1636).
- **Tabs & Sidebar.** Today tabs: each profile's Archive Today tabs after (Archive › Auto-archive): 12 hours, 24 hours, 7 days, 30 days, or Never (ours, #1636; Arc had none), with which auto-archive takes none of the profile's tabs however long they wait, while Clear and Close Tab still archive them, Unload tabs you haven't looked at, Battery Saver, on (Sidebar › Battery Saver), and Ask before moving a tab to another profile, on: the question a move to another profile's Space asks, whose Move turns it off, and this row turns it on again. Then Share favorites (Favorites › Scope): three drawings, captioned in sentence case on every OS as the pane's other lines are, In every Space, In each profile's Spaces and In each Space, kept with the Spaces. The hidden sidebar: Show the hidden sidebar at the window's edge, on: off, the pointer at the edge brings nothing out, as a display to the left or a scrollbar against that edge can set it off by accident, and ⌘S and ⌥ still show it; on the Mac and Linux, Show the hidden sidebar when ⌥ (Alt) is pressed alone. On the Mac, Haptic feedback, on, for a swipe between Spaces and a tab dropping into place (Reorder). Once the core has backed the sidebar up, Restore an earlier sidebar with Restore…, which hides Settings and lists the backups where the window in front's command bar was, newest first, each asking first, as Help › Restore Earlier Sidebar does; it's the way in Linux and Windows can find, with no menu bar. The app keeps the edge's and the Dock icon's switches in its profile directory, a file each while off.
- **Features.** A row each: Little ilo, with Show in the menu bar on the Mac and Archive windows left unused after under it; Peek at links from pinned tabs, favorites, ⇧-click and a link's menu, on, its line naming every way in it turns off (Peek › Which clicks open it); Mini Player, on, for every site; New Note, on, with each profile's notes service while it's on (Profiles › New Note); and on Linux, where the core offers it, Google's Widevine module, where it is in grey, with Download, Try Again or Remove, and Restart ilo after a download, as pages play with it only once the browser restarts (#1393).
- **Agents.** Let agents use the browser, and under it while agents are on, Tasks from the command bar, on (#1636): asking the user's own Claude or Codex from ⌘T and letting an app connect are two trust decisions. Off, ⇥ in ⌘T and ⌘L makes no task and shows no Ask hint, no slot opens at the sidebar's foot, the bar lists no agents, and no task starts; a task's card offers no Retry and its conversation no reply, saying "Tasks are off in Settings › Agents." in the field's place, while an agent's question can still be answered, so tasks running finish and their cards stay until seen. Apps that connect go on as they were. Its line says what off does. The choice is kept with the agents' settings, in the app's `Agents` file. Then Tasks (Run tasks on, the user's Claude or Codex), shown while tasks are on, Apps that connect, each app's permissions and Activity (Agents).
- **Links.** "Where a link from another app opens." The rules, then Everything else opens in (Link rules). A link clicked in another app, `open https://…` or an .html file from Finder opens in a new Little ilo window for the Space the regular window in front shows, which comes to the front, as Arc's opens in Little Arc. The same link again, while that window is open, brings it to the front instead. Everything else opens in The Space in Front makes each a new tab at the top of Today there. Not in a private window, as Chrome's don't, and not in an agent's Space: then the user's Space that window showed last. One that launches the browser opens over the Spaces it brings back: a tab before the window's first frame, a Little ilo window once the window is open. Only the prod build declares links and documents, so a dev build never shows in macOS's list of browsers.
- **Shortcuts.** Every command by the menus' sections, its keys in a chip as the command bar shows it, at least 56 wide (Keyboard says which can change). A click on a chip records the next keys pressed, which need ⌘ or ⌃ unless they're a function key: escape stops, ⌫ leaves the command without keys, and keys another command keeps stay recording, with "Used by" and its name before the chip. ↺ before the chip gives a command its defaults back once it has others, and Restore Defaults under the groups every command's. After Tabs 1 to 8, Include Favorites in ⌘1–⌘8 (Favorites) is a switch, kept with the user's keys, which Restore Defaults turns on again, and after Last Tab, Last Tab Goes to the Ninth Tab is another, which it turns off. A right-click on a chip offers Prefer Websites and Prefer ilo, checked as chosen, and a command with Prefer ilo says so in grey before its chip; ↺ and Restore Defaults give the page its keys first again. A reserved command's menu has Prefer ilo checked and neither enabled (*interim*). Settings' search finds a command by the starts of the words of its name, in any order, or by its keys, named as words or glyphs ("cmd d", "shift cmd a", ⇧⌘A), which finds every command held with at least those keys and the same key; the section has no field of its own (#1636). A click in the search field stops a recording. Keys that can't change show grey. The app keeps the user's keys in its profile directory, in `Shortcuts`.
- **Profiles.** The profiles alone, a row each: the name and how many Spaces are in it, Rename and, for a profile no Space is in, Delete, which asks in an alert first (off the Mac, in the dialog) and says how many of its favorites go with it (#1524). Where Delete is held back, the grey line says why (#1391): "The first profile can't be deleted." while there are others, "Delete its Spaces to delete it." or "An agent's task is working in it.". New Profile under them makes one, named as Chrome numbers them, with its name in a field to type over. Rename, or a double-click on a name, puts it in a field: return keeps it, escape leaves it, and losing the keyboard ends it (Other row actions).
- **Passwords.** From the command bar's Passwords and Site Controls' All Passwords… too: a profile's logins. With more than one profile, a Profile pop-up, which opens on the profile of the Space the command ran in, or of the tab whose Site Controls opened it, else the one the browser started with, and keeps it until the browser quits; then that profile's switches, on by default, in Chrome's words: Offer to save passwords, Save and fill addresses and Save and fill payment methods. Then Saved, the logins by site, each its site over its username ("No username" for one without) with Remove, which asks first; with none, "Passwords you save are listed here.". Then Never saved, while there's a site set to Never, each with Remove, which takes it off at once. Then, while the profile has one, Addresses and Payment methods, a row each with Remove, which asks first: an address's name (its email for one without) over its first line and city, most recently changed first; a card's nickname, else the name on it, over its network and expiry. Passwords never show: they stay in the core, and the app gets each login's site and username. No digit of a card's number leaves the core. A profile no Space has loaded loads when the section shows it. Agents get no path to the switches or the lists.
- **Privacy.** Copy links without tracking, on, "Copied and shared links leave out what only tells a site where a click came from. A video's time stays. The URL field's own text copies whole." (Command bar › Copy links without tracking), kept on this computer and never synced; Let sites ask to send notifications, on, Chrome's default, every profile's default for notifications: off, no site can ask, while a site the user allowed or blocked keeps its own choice (#1636). Then Relay calls, off, "Calls always go through a relay. Some apps won't connect, and calls may lag.": on, every profile's WebRTC goes only through TURN relays, as a profile with a proxy's always does (decision 10); agents get no way to change it. Then Browsing data, a row per profile one of the user's Spaces is in, with Clear…, which opens Chrome's Clear browsing data for that profile in a new tab of the front regular window, showing one of its Spaces first, and hides Settings. In a build that names a crash collector (`app/branding/crash-reports`, which only the prod build passes on), Send crash reports, off, its line naming where they go ("Sentry (EU region)", "Sentry", else the collector's host) and that turning it off deletes those not yet sent. While it's off, the launch after a crash asks once in a toast, "ilo quit unexpectedly.", with Send Report, which sends that crash's report alone, and Don't Send, which deletes it; the toast stays until one is pressed or another takes its place. In a build that names a server for the daily usage count (`app/branding/daily-count`, which only the prod build passes on), Send a daily usage count, on, its line saying exactly what a count holds (decision 11); turned off, nothing more is sent. Agents get no way to read, change or answer either. Last, in every build, Privacy Policy: "ilo never sees the sites you visit or what you type, and never sells your data. The policy names every service it talks to.", with Open, which opens the build's site's /privacy (ilobrowser.com/privacy, listing every service and URL ilo talks to) in a tab and hides Settings, as About's notices do (owner, 2026-10-29, #684: the statement in Settings › Privacy and on the site).
- **Ad Blocking** (Lucide's shield-ban, #2107). Block ads and trackers, on (Picked by the owner › Ad blocking); then while there are any, "Ads are shown on" and the sites it's off on, each with Turn On (Ad blocking › Turning it off); then My filters and the filter lists (Ad blocking › Settings for it).
- **Sync.** Sync for the browser, not a profile's, of the Spaces and the first profile's bookmarks, passwords and open tabs, through ilo's server or another one. Signed out, a group says what syncs, with Sign In, and under it "Server: ilo · Change…" takes an address; a new server signs out first. A build that names no server (`app/branding/sync-server`) has the address field open there instead, empty, saying "Enter your sync server's address to sign in."; Sign In signs in to the address typed. Sign In opens the server's page in a small window of the core's, which closes it when the server answers; Cancel stops waiting. The account's first device shows its 24 recovery words once, with Copy and Done. Any other device asks the account's devices to add it and shows the code they'll show; Use Recovery Words Instead takes the words, and Ask Again asks once more after a device says no or none answers. Under the words, "Lost your devices and words? Start over…" asks once, saying it erases everything synced on the server for every device, that what's on this device stays, and that the old words stop working, with Erase and Start Over; then it makes this the account's first device, with new words. A device asked shows a dialog over the window that has the keyboard, "Add MacBook Air (macOS)? Check it shows 312 568.", with Add and Not me, and ↩ is Not me. Signed in, the email, the server and Sign Out, which asks first on the account's only device; then Also sync's Settings and Extensions switches (Sync); then the devices, this one marked and each other with Remove; then Show Recovery Words…, which asks first. The same view is onboarding's account step, on that step's ground (Onboarding). Agents get none of it.
- **Import & Export.** Import from other browsers… opens it. Under Import, the import (Import from other browsers): a row per browser found with something to bring, the most recently used first, its app's icon as the system draws it at a glyph plate's 28 before its name, or a quiet globe on the plate where the system has none, its profiles' names grey under its name and Import… at its end (#1673: ilo ships no browser's icon; the Mac's comes from its bundle, Linux's from its desktop entry and the icon theme, and Windows' is the shell's for its exe, beside its profiles where it's installed for the user, else as the registry's App Paths names it, each at the window's scale); a browser installed twice has a row for each, the one from Flatpak, Snap or the Microsoft Store named by how it came ("Chrome (Flatpak)"). Import… reads the browser's files, away from the UI, and shows what comes from it under the heading Import › its name, each kind a switch beside its glyph, on by default, with how much of it comes on the line under its name (Arc's Spaces by name, each on a chip with its colour), "Counting…" until the read is in; then Passwords, a row of its own saying where that browser exports them, with Choose File…, and once a file is chosen its name with a switch, which Chrome's importer reads as the rest comes in; then a line naming the profiles it goes into, those the import makes as the browser has them, and that logins don't come. Back and Import are under them, Import pressed while it counts goes on once it has, and Export waits for the import's end; each state's one button, Import here, is white on the host's accent, Settings' green or the first launch's Signal, and Esc goes back to the list, keeping what was switched off for that browser. Import brings them in, and says how much came, with Done. While it runs, each Space it makes or fills is a card in its own colours, its glyph at its top left, its name and count at its foot, 72 wide in Settings and 80 on the first launch's ground, under the name of the profile it goes into ("Work · new profile", "Default · existing"), the profiles 24 apart (#1673). A card comes as the core makes its Space, fading in and rising 4 pt over 180 ms, 50 ms after the one before; its count rolls up as its pinned tabs go in, at most once a roll of 200 ms, the old number leaving 8 pt up as the new comes from under it; and its badge, a dot of the ink breathing once in 1.6 s while it waits, becomes the accent with a check that draws itself in 250 ms once its tabs are in. Under the cards, Favorites, History and Addresses, each that comes, say Not yet until the Spaces are in, then History "Adding 12,408 pages…", the number read, shimmering while the core adds them, then the number it added, ticked, as the core reports only its end. Under Reduce Motion the cards and counts fade in place, the dot holds and the check fades in whole. Until the Spaces and tabs are in, Cancel stands where Done will and stops it: "Stopped. Nothing came in. The two Spaces it made are empty, and importing again fills them.", with Done and Import Again, which reads the browser again with what was switched on, and Esc goes back to the list; the cards of the Spaces it made stay over the line as outlines, with no fill or badge, each "empty" (#1673). A profile it made that hasn't opened 20 s on ends the wait, named, "⚠ Work hasn't opened in 20 seconds, so its three Spaces wait.", with Cancel and Try Again, which asks for it again and waits as long again (#1392, #1673). Its cards are drawn at half their opacity, with ⚠ for their badge and "waiting" for their count, holding still however long they wait; the line is under the cards and rows, which say what they read "once Work opens", and in the first launch the host's title says it instead. Once a browser has come in, its row says when after its profiles, "imported today at 10:42; again brings only what's new", and its button is Import Again…, until ilo quits: the record of each browser's last import is kept in memory for the run alone, as the Archive's tasks are, as nothing that records use is stored without the owner's yes. After a relaunch the row is Import… again, and importing again still adds nothing twice, as the import skips what's here. Then the files a browser exports, Passwords and Bookmarks, each with Choose File…. The same view is onboarding's import step, with Skip, on that step's ground. Its own layout is #1673's. Under Export, Spaces as Markdown with Export…, which opens the system's save panel on Spaces.md and writes, under "# Spaces", a "## <name>" for each of the user's Spaces top to bottom, then its favorites under "### Favorites" and its pinned tabs under "### Pinned", each a "- [title](URL)" item at the URL it goes back to, and each folder an item of its name with what it holds nested under it; a heading with nothing under it is left out. Today tabs come and go and an agent's Spaces are the agent's, so neither is written. It's the reverse of the import, as the user's data leaves as easily as it came. There's no agent path.
- **About.** Last, opened by ilo › About ilo and the command bar's About ilo, headed by the app's name over "a good tool.", the first launch's line. A group with its version and build, and the engine's Chromium version, in grey, with where its update stands and, where the build carries a nightly feed, Get nightly builds (Updates); then, on the Mac and Windows, where ilo updates itself, Check for updates automatically, on (#1793): off, the updater asks its feed nothing on its own, neither at launch nor on its 8-hour schedule, on the Mac Sparkle neither checking nor downloading and on Windows the updater's timer not firing, until Check for Updates… in the app menu, the command bar or this row asks once; its line says so. Turned on again, the schedule runs from the last check at once, with no relaunch. The app keeps it in its profile directory, `no-automatic-update-checks` while off, for the whole browser. Linux has none, as the Flatpak's updates are Flathub's, nor does a copy from the Microsoft Store. Agents have no path to it. Then Show what's new after an update, on: off, an update's first launch opens no notes, and What's New in the Help menu and the command bar still opens them, kept as a file in the profile directory while off; then Licence (ilo is free software under the GNU GPL, version 3), Notices (NOTICE: what ilo adapts), Libraries (THIRD-PARTY.txt: the Rust crates in the app) and Chromium (chrome://credits), each with Open, which opens it in a tab and hides the window. A file the build didn't carry, as with `cargo run`, has no Open. The engine's version is the Mac's framework's version folder, Windows's manifest named after it beside chrome.exe, and the VERSION the Linux engine's package writes beside chrome; an engine packaged before that shows none.

### Archive

Arc's structure. Dia replaced it with Clean Up Tabs, recorded below for reference.

- **Undo, everywhere** (ours; Arc undoes only Clear and Delete Space, and the owner asked for the rest). ⌘Z undoes the last thing the user did in the window that closed something or moved it, and ⇧⌘Z does it again; the Edit menu's Undo and Redo say which ("Undo Move to Space"), and are plain Undo and Redo, for the page, when there's nothing of ours to do. They are one history of the window's last 100 edits, each undone by doing its opposite, so redo is the same code run from the other side. What it covers: close tab, Close Tabs Below, Clear, pin and unpin, a pinned tab removed by its row's button, Add to and Remove from Favorites, a tab, folder or favorite moved by a drag or Move to Space, a tab's copy an ⌥-drag left, Delete Folder, Remove from Split and Separate, a split view made ("Undo Split View": by a tab rested on a row or dropped at the page's edge, ⌥-click, the split bar, the hover card's Open in Split View, or a link opened or dropped into one; ⌘Z takes the tab back to its row and place, or closes it if it opened for the split, and the page shown stays shown), each pane added and each change of orientation, renames, icons and colours of tabs, folders and Spaces, a Space's theme (one step for each click or drag in the theme picker, and one for Remove Theme Color), Delete Space, Delete from Archive, Clear Archive, and a page or search the command bar took out of history (Remove from History). Each comes back in its exact place, with its name, icon, mute, history and split, and a tab that was loaded comes back unloaded. A private window records nothing. Not undoable, because the user didn't do them or there is nothing to bring back: closing a window, auto-archive, what an agent does (it has its own activity log) and a move to another profile. A step whose tab or Space has since gone is dropped and the one before it is tried. ⌘Z in a page's text field is the page's, and the Edit menu's Undo clicked with a page focused goes to the page; ilo gets ⌘Z only when the page doesn't keep it.
- **Clear** (Arc 1.170.0 rec, 3 runs). ⇧⌘K or the Clear button closes every Today tab of the Space but the one shown, including grouped, split and playing ones. Pinned tabs and favorites stay. The rows go in one frame and the one left is in its place at once, with no toast. ⌘Z brings them back in place, fading in on Row motion, unloaded, splits still split, and the tab shown stays. With nothing else to close, the tab shown alone or its split being all of Today, Clear closes those too, as its arrow says (Clear divider), and the window shows the tab the close rule picks (Arc in use, the owner). Ours: ⌘Z then shows them again, as the page that was shown, with its split. ⌘Z in a text field, in a page or ours, is the field's own, and Edit › Undo clicked in the menu goes to the focused page or field first (Undo, everywhere).
- **Arc's clear actions.** Arc's binary has three: clear all, clear unselected Today tabs, and tidy-and-clear. Tidy is AI and out of scope.
- **Dia's Clean Up Tabs** (⌥⌘K) moves untouched tabs to "Recently Cleaned", with a daily automatic run. It is not ours.
- **Closed tabs.** Closed tabs reopen with ⇧⌘T, newest first, with position, pin, group, custom name and icon, mute state and back/forward list (Dia, P§2). Ours: a split view's pane goes back to its place in the split, if a tab of the split is open and it has room, and so does a pinned tab whose pane closed, pinned, until another tab closes. A pinned tab removed by its row's button comes back pinned at its place the same way. Panes closed together by the row's ✕ come back together, one ⇧⌘T at a time.
- **The archive.** A Today tab that closes, by ⌘W, its ✕, Clear or its menu, goes in the archive, as does a pinned tab removed by its row's button, newest first, with its URL, title and when it closed. Arc's menu calls ⌘W Archive Tab. A tab ⇧⌘T or ⌘Z brings back leaves it. It's saved with the Spaces. Ours: it keeps a tab 30 days, up to 500 tabs, and archived tabs keep no Space.
- **Auto-archive.** Arc archives Today tabs not shown for 12 hours by default. Arc offers 12 hours, 24 hours, 7 days and 30 days (Arc 1.170.0 bin), and keeps the choice per profile in Settings › Profiles; ours keeps it per profile in Settings › Tabs & Sidebar, each Space's tabs going by its profile's. A profile starts at 12 hours, and its own row changes it (#1636: the old New profiles archive tabs after went, as a rule nobody could explain). Ours: a Today tab's 12 hours start when no window shows it any more, or when it opens unshown, and count on the wall clock, through sleep and while the browser isn't running, so a launch archives the tabs whose time ran out. The tab a window shows and one playing sound stay. It goes in the archive as a closed tab does, so ⇧⌘T can reopen it. Little ilo windows have their own time (Little Arc › Archive). Downloads that have ended leave the Library's list after the profile's time too (Downloads › Old downloads leave the list).
- **The first auto-archive's note** (Arc shows new members a one-time banner on it; ours, as it isn't recorded). The first time auto-archive takes tabs, at launch too, the toast (Toast) says "Tabs unseen for 12 hours went to the archive.", the interval the user's, with a View Archive chip, which opens the Library on Archived Tabs, and a ×. It has no timer: it stays until one is pressed or another toast takes its place, and comes back with the next launch's auto-archive until one is. Pressed, it never shows again, as the core keeps that with the Spaces. No agent path: it tells the user what the browser did.
- **The Library** (Arc 1.170.0 cap and ax, ⇧⌘L, Window › View Library, or the footer's button at its left). It slides in from the left, 417 wide (Spaces: as wide as its cards), as it pushes the card's left edge to its own without resizing it, so the page runs off the window's right edge (Library push). It starts its own width to the left and moves with the card's edge, so they meet as they stop. Opened or closed by a click, the sidebar under it fades out where it is in 2 frames as it comes, and back in over 130 ms from 80 ms after it starts to go, the Library fading in and out over it by as much, uncut (Library fade, Library recede); moved by fingers, it recedes (Spaces › Switching). While it moves short of where it opens, the sidebar draws the window's buttons, which recede with it, and the Library's panel draws a copy on its column, which slides in with it (ours, *interim*: Arc's slide-in isn't recorded). The window's own take over once the panel is at rest, within half a pixel, on the column as in Arc, and stay while fingers pull it past; the way back and a swipe let go short of halfway swap back the same way. A swipe or a wheel's tick past the first Space opens it too (Spaces › Switching). A column 112 wide, white 0.06 with a 1 pt line of white 0.04 at its edge, holds the sections, 97 × 80 at x 8 with 100 between them, centred 5 above the middle; the selected one has a white 0.09 plate at radius 12, its glyph in the Space's colour 31 below its top and its label, 12 pt semibold at white 0.85, 56 below. Back (←) is 32 × 32 at (8, h − 40), with the ladder's white 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed. Ours: a section not shown takes a white 0.05 plate under the pointer. Arc's sections are Media, Downloads, Easels & Notes, Spaces and Archived Tabs; ours are Media, Downloads, Spaces and Archived Tabs, in Arc's order and places (centred, as four), until Easels & Notes' feature lands, so no button does nothing. The footer's button and ⇧⌘L open the Library on the section shown last.
- **Archived Tabs.** A search field on the window buttons' row, x 120, 36 tall at y 10, white 0.09 at radius 10, its glyph 15 pt at x 134.5 and its text 14 pt at x 164, "Search Archive…" in white 0.56. The field runs to 409, but while the archive holds a Little ilo tab or a task, Arc's Filter button, 73 × 36 at x 336, takes its end and the field stops 8 before it (ours). The button is the field's plate at its radius with "Filter" in the field's text, centred, or what it filters to, "Little ilo" or "Tasks", white 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed as a row; a click shows a menu of All, Little ilo and Tasks, the one shown checked: Little ilo lists only the tabs Little ilo windows closed, and Tasks only the tasks, search narrowing those. Arc's menu isn't recorded, so its items are ours, and the Library forgets the choice when it closes. The list starts at y 56: day headings at x 124, 12 pt semibold at white 0.6, and a row per tab, 56 apart, with its favicon 16 pt at x 134 and its title (14 pt, white 0.78) over its host (12 pt, white 0.6) at x 164. Ours: headings Today, Yesterday, Earlier This Week, Last Week and Older (Arc's capture showed Today and Earlier This Week); a row takes the ladder's plate (x 120–413, 50 tall), white 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed; search matches titles and URLs as typed; a click opens the tab in Peek, beside the Library, which stays open, as Arc's archive opens a preview (the owner, 2026-10-29, #696): the preview grows from the row over the part of the page the Library leaves in the window, in the profile of the Space shown; ⌘W, Esc, its Close or a click on the dim close it and leave the tab in the archive, with nothing for ⌘Z to reopen, as it's still there; ⌘↩ or Open as Tab keeps it, as it is, at the top of the Today tabs of the Space it closed in, which the window shows with it, while that Space is in the preview's profile, else of the Space shown, takes it out of the archive and closes the Library. Ours: ⌘-click (Ctrl-click off the Mac) restores it at once, and so does a click where no preview can show it, with previews off in Settings › Peek, on a page that isn't http(s) or with no tab shown: at the top of the Today tabs of the Space it closed in, as Arc's goes back to its Space, which the window shows with it, or of the Space shown once that Space is gone or for a window's own, with its history if it closed since the browser started, closing the Library. A tab kept from the preview comes back at its page without that history. A right-click shows the row's menu, Restore Tab, Copy URL and Delete from Archive, and Delete takes the tab out without asking, as Remove from List does a download. The tasks dismissed from the sidebar's foot list among the tabs, by when each left the foot, under the same headings and on the same rows (Agent task › Done): the card's mark in the favicon's place, Done's tick or the grey ring of one stopped or failed, and its title over its CLI, how it ended and when, and the pages it still holds, "Claude · Done 13:31 · 1 page". Search matches a task's title and what was asked. A click closes the Library and brings the task back as ⇧⌘T does, its card to the foot with its conversation open; a right-click shows Open Conversation, which does the same, and Delete from Archive, which takes it out without asking and out of ⇧⌘T's reach, a step ⌘Z undoes as it does a tab's. A task lives in memory only, for the run: the Archive keeps the last ten until ilo quits, and nothing of them is written to disk, as nothing that records use is stored without the user's yes (owner, 2026-11-02). No agent path: it's the user's own history of tasks. Esc closes the Library (ours, the owner's call in #1074; Arc's doesn't). **Empty** (ours, no recording): the list holds one line in place of the rows, 48 under its top, 12 pt in white 0.6, centred and wrapping in the list's width 12 in from either side (x 124–404, #828), never under the column or the card: "Closed Today tabs stay here for 30 days." says what fills it, and "No archived tabs match." replaces it while a search finds nothing; under Tasks, "Finished tasks stay here until ilo quits." and "No tasks match.". No art, and no action, since closing a tab or a task is what fills it.
- **Spaces** (Arc 1.170.0 cap, one Space; Arc 1.170 rec, three Spaces). The Library is 216 + 264 a Space wide here, 480 with one and 1008 with three, with the card pushed there, and there's no search field. A card per Space, in the switcher's order, 240 wide from x 140 (28 after the column) and 24 apart, from y 48.5 to 48 above the window's bottom, at radius ≈ 14 (measured off an enhanced capture), in the Space's gradient over the Library with a soft shadow round it. Inside it is the sidebar's list at the sidebar's metrics (41 pitch, favicons at x 17, folder tabs 16 in, a Clear divider, whose Clear has its arrow by the sidebar's rule): the Space's name in a field whose middle is 25 below the card's top, then, from 44 down, the pinned tabs and folders, and the Today tabs under their line with Clear, the first row's middle 64.5 down (Arc 1.170 rec, with the field 7 to 43, and Arc 1.171's 25 and 64; the one-Space cap had both 8 lower). The field (Arc 1.170 stills; *interim*, by proportion to the card's 240): a plate 8 in from the card's sides and 36 tall, white 0.1 at the controls' radius 6 where Arc's is 8; the Space's icon (Icon), or its swatch without one, in a dashed slot 24 square, 6 in, its dashes the icon picker's placeholder's in white 0.3; the name 6 after the slot, semibold 13 in white 0.5; and the brush (Arc's glyph) on a plate 28 square, 4 in from the field's end. Ours (the owner, 2026-10-30, #811): at rest the field is the icon or swatch and the name alone, and its plate, the slot's dashes and the brush show only with the pointer on the field, the slot and the brush included, not the rest of the card, in one frame as the other hover plates; they stay while the name is typed over or a picker shows for the Space, and keep their places at rest, so nothing moves as they come. At the card's foot a ••• shows the Space's menu, as a right-click on the card does, its middle 22 in from the card's right and 21.5 above its foot (Arc 1.170 rec; Arc 1.171's 21). Ours: it's the sidebar's ••• button, and it shows only with the pointer on the card, where Arc's is there at rest, as the field's controls show only under the pointer (#811). Arc's move handle, at the card's foot with its middle 22 in from the card's left and 21.5 above its foot (Arc 1.170 rec), drags the card along the others to move the Space (#1580). Ours, *interim* (Arc's drag isn't recorded): the handle is a grip on the sidebar's ••• button, shown with the pointer on the card as the field's controls are (#811), and its press doesn't pick the Space; the card comes away whole from it with a popover's shadow, its place dimmed to half, and the rows' drop line stood up, its ring 7 below the cards' tops and its end 2 above their foot, stands in the gap it would go into, never where it was. Let go over the section, the Space goes there as a dot dragged along the switcher does (Footer and switcher), the cards gliding to their places on Row motion, the one let go from where its copy was, with the Library left open; off the section, or after Esc, nothing moves. ⌘Z puts it back. Arc's blue + is 30 across, filled #1F2AFF with a white glyph, centred 48 after the card and halfway down the window. Ours: under the pointer a card lightens by the ladder's white 0.12, the brush has the ladder's 0.12 and 0.065 pressed, and the + darkens to 0.9 of its fill (#1C26E6), as the dialog's action does; once its cards don't fit, the section leaves the page 56 however narrow the window, live as it resizes, and its cards and the + after the last scroll across, clipped at its edge (Arc 1.171 ax, 8 Spaces: 1044 wide at 1100, 444 at 500). Two fingers across the cards, their lists or the gaps between them scroll them one to one, elastic at both ends, about 17 for a 340 pull, springing back in about 0.25 s once let go, and a scroll along doesn't move them; a swipe that starts in them never leaves the Library, and one that starts with them at rest at the end it pushes past is the Library's swipe; Shift and a wheel scroll them 10 a line, and at their end go on to leave the Library for the first Space (Arc 1.171 rec). Ours: no scroller is drawn, as ilo's other lists draw none (Arc shows AppKit's overlay scroller as it scrolls); over the column a swipe stays the Library's (Arc's did nothing there); the momentum after the lift carries the cards up to an end without bouncing; the wheel leaves only once it has rested 250 ms since it last moved them; the band gives at most 80, its stiffness fitted to Arc's 17 for 340, and springs back on the push's spring; the cards start at their first each time the section shows; it goes to its width or back to 417 on the push's spring when the section changes or a Space comes or goes (Arc's isn't recorded). A click on the name makes it a field there, all selected, with the Space left as it was and the Library open, as Arc's is: ↩ or a click outside keeps what's typed, the same rename as Rename Space, so ⌘Z and agents have it, escape puts the old name back, and so does an empty name; closing the Library keeps it too. The icon slot, whose tooltip says Edit Space icon, hangs the icon picker from itself, and the brush opens the theme picker beside itself, each with its arrow at what opened it, as the sidebar's do. Ours (the owner, #1074, as Arc's open in its Library): the Library stays open under them with the Space shown where it was, so several Spaces can be changed in a row, and the card's gradient takes the theme as it's made. A click on another card's slot or brush moves the picker there, one on the slot or brush whose picker shows closes it, and one anywhere else closes it and does what it would anyway. Esc closes the picker, and then the Library. A click anywhere else on a card shows the Space and closes the Library, and a right-click anywhere on it shows the Space's menu, as the right-click on its dot does (Renaming and deleting), where Rename Space… shows the Space and closes the Library for the field in the sidebar; the + makes a Space as the footer's New Space… does, and closes the Library for its form. A tab's row dragged from one card and let go over another Space's, an empty one too, moves the tab there as Move to does, pinned if it was, and the Library stays open (Arc's help centre, #688); ours, *interim* (Arc's isn't recorded): the row follows the pointer on the selected plate in its Space's colour with a popover's shadow, the card under it is ringed 2 wide in its accent, the tab goes where Move to puts it, and let go over its own card or off the cards nothing moves. An agent's Space's rows don't drag and its card takes none, as in the sidebar. Favorites aren't drawn on a card (Arc's capture had none), nor the New Tab row. The section's glyph is ours, two cards, the front one solid. Agents already show and pick Spaces through the core's API, which this section only calls, so it has no agent path of its own. Measure in Arc: the shown Space's card, a card's hover, where Arc's icon picker opens from the slot, the + while another section is shown.
- **Media** (Window › View Media…, the command bar's View Media…). Arc's lists images, videos, screenshots and captures. Ours: the captures saved to the Library (Capture › Save to Library), in the profile's `Media` folder, and the finished downloads whose names say they're images or videos, together, newest first, under the archive's day headings, with "Search Media…" over them.
