Each is a square tile, three a row in the rows' width 6 apart at radius 12, white 0.09 under its picture, which fills it (the file's preview, as the recent downloads' rows ask for it), else the file's icon; a video has its glyph at the bottom left, and its tile plays it, as Arc's previews autoplay: without sound, from its start again at its end, filling the square, from when the tile is first drawn until it's scrolled away or the Library closes, its first frame showing before (ours: the system decodes it at the tile's size; the Mac's alone so far, and elsewhere a video's tile keeps its still). A click opens the file in a tab at the top of the Today tabs, where the page shows the picture or plays the video, and closes the Library (*interim*: Arc opens it in its own viewer, and its grid isn't measured). A right-click on a capture's tile shows Open and Delete, which moves it to the system's Trash, from where it can come back, as Arc deletes a capture from the Library by right-click; one on a download's shows that download's menu, as its row in Downloads does. Empty, as the archive's, it says "Saved captures and downloaded media show here."
- **Clear Archive** (Archive menu, and a right-click on the Library's Archived Tabs section, as Arc's on its Archive icon) asks first, then empties the archive, and ⌘Z brings it back (Undo, everywhere). Ours: the section's menu holds Clear Archive alone, dimmed while the archive is empty. The tasks Archived Tabs lists go too, as they're in the archive: the question counts both ("Its 3 tabs and 2 tasks leave the archive. ⌘Z brings them back."), the tasks stay in memory as Delete from Archive leaves one, and the one ⌘Z brings back the tabs and the tasks.
- **Agents** (ours, #1080, the owner's call; Window › View Agents…, and the command bar's): the past tasks and sessions of the window's profile's agents, by day, newest first. Each row has a task's mark, its title, and under it the agent, when it ended and what its receipt said ("Claude Code · 14:02 · 1 order placed · 3 pages"). A click on a row opens its page in the list's place: Back in the mark's place, then its title over the agent, how long it ran, when it ended and its receipt ("Claude Code · 12 min · 14:02 · 3 pages"); under Sites, each site it went to with its agent's answer there for good, Always Allow or Never with Revoke, or Asks first, as Settings › Agents lists and revokes them, so the way back from each permission is where the user sees what it was used for; and under Steps, its activity log newest first, each step in the words Settings › Agents' log has over when it came, and why it was refused if it was, a request opening to where it went and what it sent as there (Agents UI › A request's row) (ours, *interim*: the rows are the list's). A search goes back to the list. It keeps only what a receipt and the activity log show: the title, the agent, when, the pages' titles and addresses, the orders and messages the user allowed, and for each of its last 200 steps what it did, on which site, when and why it was refused, and for a request its address and what it sent, secrets hidden. Never what a page said, what on it the agent acted on, what was typed, a cookie, a password or the conversation. It's kept on this computer only, in a file in the profile's own folder, never synced, and never for a private window, which offers no tasks. An entry lives 30 days from when its task ended, then goes. ✕ on a row deletes it, Clear over the list deletes them all, and deleting history deletes those that ran in the time it deleted, all of them with all of it. Empty, the list says "Your agents' finished tasks stay here for 30 days."
- Measure in Arc: the archive's limits, the Filter menu, a row's hover and right-click, whether restoring closes the Library, Clear with a pinned tab shown, and ⌘Z after Clear with a page's text field focused.

### Profiles

A profile is cookies, history, logins, passwords, autofill and extensions (decision 5). The user switches Spaces, and the profile comes with the Space.

- The form's rows (Arc 1.170.0 cap and ax): 200 × 36 plates like the field's, 42 and 86 under its top. Each has a glyph of white 0.5 centred 30 in and a white semibold 13 label at 46.5. Profile's shows the Space's profile in a pill 22 tall, white 0.1, 10 after the label, its name bold 11, 8 in, then a chevron, 10 from the pill's end; Arc's (i) after it isn't ours. Arc's glyphs are its own; ours are Lucide's user and paintbrush. *Interim*: a press on Profile shows a menu of the profiles and New Profile, with the Space's checked and on the pill's name, as a pop-up button's menu is; a press on Choose a Theme opens the theme picker beside the form, its arrow at the row (Themes).
- Create, rename and delete a profile, and pick it for a Space. Arc's UI for both: measure. *Interim*, ours: Settings › Profiles (Settings), the New Space form's Profile row, and the Space menu's Set Profile submenu (Arc 1.171's name) with the profiles, the Space's checked; New Profile, named as Chrome numbers them; and Delete for each profile no Space is in, which asks first, as nothing brings it back. Its favorites go with it, and the question counts them, ours (#1524): under each profile's, a profile with no Space keeps favorites no Space shows, which the user couldn't take out first; under Every Space the other profiles keep the sites, so it counts none; under Each Space they closed with the Spaces. Only an empty Space changes profile, as its tabs' pages live in their own. A new Space is in the profile of the Space it's made from. The profile the browser started with comes first and can't be deleted; Chrome's own name for it names the browser, so it's "Default". The Library's Downloads list every profile's.
- **New Note** (Arc's help centre, "Customize Default Notes App"; Arc 1.170.0 Settings › Shortcuts). New Note opens a new document in the notes service the Space's profile picks, in a tab at the top of its Today tabs, as a pick in the command bar does, and New Note Split opens it in a split view beside the page shown, as ⌃+ and a pick do. New Note is in the command bar, the footer's + menu and on ⌃⇧N; New Note Split is in the command bar, with no key; Settings › Shortcuts rebinds both. Settings › Features picks the service per profile under New Note, as Arc's Profiles tab does: a row per profile with a pop-up of Arc's four, Notion, Google Docs, Word and Confluence, each opening its own .new address (notion.new, docs.new, word.new, confluence.new), which makes the document in the account the profile is signed in to. That pick is the one setting for new documents in other services (owner, 2026-10-29, #698); other services' are the command bar's .new rows. Its switch in Settings › Features, on until turned off, is ours, as its neighbours' are (the orchestrator's call for the owner, 2026-11-10, #1636): off, the + menu and the command bar have neither command, ⌃⇧N and any keys the user gave them do nothing, Settings › Shortcuts has no row for them and the services' pop-ups go, and the tabs New Note opened stay; its line says so. Ours: Google Docs until a profile picks one, as Arc's own notes, its default, are gone; Arc's menus have neither command, so the menu bar doesn't either; a private window runs neither, as it has none of the profile's sign-ins. Agents get no tool for the setting, and an agent opening a tab at one of these addresses goes through the same core call as New Note.
- **Incognito** (Chrome and Dia). ⇧⌘N opens a private window of the current Space's profile, dark by default. Private windows of one profile share one session; other profiles' don't. The session ends with the profile's last private window. Its downloads and history are never written to disk. *Interim*, ours, as neither Arc's nor Dia's private window is recorded: it opens with no page, as ⌘N's window does, and shows one Space of its own, named Incognito (and the profile, with more than one), with Today tabs only. No other window shows it, nothing saves it, and its tabs close with it. Black 0.35 takes the gradient's place, with no Space colour. It has no favorites, pinned tabs, folders, Space switcher or menu, or ⇧⌘T, and a page never moves between it and a regular window. Its Library button and ⇧⌘L open a Library of its own session (owner's call, 2026-11-04, #669): Media and Downloads alone, which list only the session's downloads, shared by the profile's private windows and gone with the session, as Chrome's Incognito downloads are; they save to the profile's folder without asking, as the regular windows' do, and the files stay where they were saved. Its Spaces and Archived Tabs aren't there, as the private window keeps neither, and a regular window's Library never lists a private download. Arc's incognito Library also has Easels and Notes, which ilo hasn't. Its command bar sends no search suggestions, as no window does (ungoogled-chromium turns them off). The extensions the user allowed in Incognito on Chrome's extensions page run in it, as in Chrome: their pinned actions, Site Controls tiles, keyboard commands and Extensions menu items show and work there, and the others' don't (owner, 2026-10-29). Pinning one from it pins it in the profile's regular windows too, as Chrome keeps one list. Their side panels don't open there, *interim*. Closing the last regular window quits, closing the private ones too. Agents never reach it.
- **Which profile** (#2339, ours: Arc shows the profile only in the Space's form). With more than one profile, the Space's name row shows the profile's name after the Space's, 6 after it, in the secondary ink at the normal weight; it truncates first, the Space's name keeping its width. The name shows once where the two are the same, and a private window shows none, as its session has none of the profile's logins or cookies. VoiceOver reads it after the name, "Home, Personal". Site Controls' chip says it too and opens the page in another profile (Extensions › Site Controls), and Little ilo's bar has its Profiles picker.
- **Dia's profile indicator**, kept for reference only (Dia 1.50.1 bin, D›Profile paging). It sits in the header beside the traffic lights: the name in 13 pt semibold with 9 pt padding, 34 tall, and a hover/press fill at radius 10. Its colour is the Space colour mixed 0.6 toward white (dark) or 0.4 toward black (light). It shows the whole name, a truncated name if at least 52 pt fit, or else a person glyph (14 pt) in a 32 pt button. Its tooltip is "Switch between profiles". It appears only with 2 or more profiles. Measure whether and where Arc shows a Space's profile.

### Extensions

Chrome's extension system, per profile.

- **Toolbar** (Dia 1.52 cap, D›Toolbar).
  - Only pinned extensions show: 16 pt icons on a 30.25 pitch, with no puzzle-piece button. Ours are on the URL field's buttons' 30, with the header's hover plate. An overflow button appears only when the pinned ones don't fit. Ours takes the last place, an ellipsis, and its menu lists the rest by title and runs them, their popups hanging from it. With no place at all, the Extensions menu runs them.
  - Each shows its badge for the tab shown, white on Chrome's grey 0x5f6368 unless the extension gives a colour. An action that doesn't act on the tab is dimmed to 0.4.
  - In our layout they sit at the end of the sidebar's URL field in the order pinned, always shown, with Copy URL left of them. As many show as leave 84 pt for the host (Ardia: two at 190 pt), counted with Copy URL shown, so they keep their places as it comes and goes. Measure where Arc puts them.
  - A click runs one, its popup hanging from it. Right-click gives Chrome's toolbar menu: Unpin; Options, if it has them, in a new tab (Chrome's extensions page shows those that don't ask for a tab); Remove, which asks first; and Manage Extension, which opens its details on Chrome's extensions page. Turn Off is left to Chrome's extensions page, which Manage Extension opens.
- **Menu bar.** The Extensions menu has one item per turned-on extension of the shown Space's profile, by name, which runs its action on the tab shown as a click on its toolbar button does in Chrome: the extension is granted the tab, and its popup opens or its click handler runs. Then Pin to URL Field, a submenu with a checked item per extension that pins or unpins it (Dia: Pin Extensions…), Add Extension…, which opens the Web Store in a new tab, and Manage Extensions…, which opens Chrome's extensions page in a new tab (Dia).
- **Popups** (Chrome). A popup sizes to its page between 25 × 25 and 800 × 600, shrinking as well as growing. `chrome.action.openPopup()` opens it over the active tab. It's Chrome's own popup, with the keyboard, closed by escape, a click outside or another tab. It hangs from its action's button when that's shown, or the overflow button it's behind, else under the URL field, or from the page's top left corner while the sidebar is hidden, *interim*.
- **Pop-up windows** (Arc). A window an extension makes with `chrome.windows.create`, as MetaMask's confirmations are, opens as a small window of its own in the extension's profile, at the size it asks for, as in Arc and Chrome, never as a tab (owner, 2026-10-29). It's Chrome's own window, as undocked DevTools are, *interim*: its look is Chrome's until Arc's is recorded. A page's popups still open as tabs, a Little ilo window's in a Little ilo window of their own (Little ilo › Links from other apps).
- **Action state.** Badge, title, icon and enabled state, for the tab shown, update from Chrome's change notifications and a tab switch. Ardia polls every 1.5 s; we don't (principle 1). The title is for the action's tooltip.
- **Side panel.** An extension's `side_panel` page shows in a card right of the page, 10 pt from it, under a 36 pt header: its 16 pt icon, its name, a ⋯ menu (Options if it has them, Manage Extension, Close Side Panel) and a close button (Dia). It opens as in Chrome: from `chrome.sidePanel.open()` in a user gesture, from a click on the action of an extension that set `openPanelOnActionClick`, and from Open Side Panel in its action's right-click menu, which reads Close Side Panel while it shows. A tab's own panel goes on a switch to a tab without one and comes back with its tab; a panel for every tab stays. Its page has the keyboard of its own once clicked. Its left edge drags it from 320 to 600 wide (the range is **Ardia**'s), opening at Chrome's 360 until then, and the width is kept in the profile, one for all extensions as in Chrome. It takes at most half the card, so a narrow window keeps the page as wide as it. It hides while a page is full screen and stays with the sidebar hidden. It opens and closes without motion, *interim*: measure Dia's.
- **Install** (Chrome's flow, Dia's dialog). Chrome's questions whether to add an extension, re-enable one that asks for more permissions, or grant more, are the Remove dialog's card, in Chrome's words: the extension's icon over Chrome's title, its heading ("It can:") and its warnings, each with its details under it, smaller and dimmer, then Chrome's Cancel and its action, in the Space's accent as it takes nothing back. Return accepts and escape cancels. A question waits for a dialog that shows, and a window asks one at a time; Chrome hears it dropped if the window or the tab that asked closes first. Chrome's "added" bubble is left out: it points at a toolbar we don't have. The Web Store's own "Add to Chrome" asks with it, and the store's extensions update from it (decision 2). A .crx installs from a page, with ungoogled-chromium's `--extension-mime-request-handling=always-prompt-for-install`. Removal asks "Remove “<name>”?" with Cancel and a destructive Remove; Chrome's extensions page and the store's "Remove from Chrome" still ask with Chrome's own dialog, *interim*. Measure Arc's prompt: size, layout and position.
- **Crashes** (Arc, owner 2026-10-29). An extension whose own process crashes twice is turned off, as Arc turned off one that crashed it. Chromium can't say which extension crashed the browser itself, as an extension's code never runs in the browser's process, so the crash of its own process, its background page or worker, popup or pages, is the sign; the browser or the system ending that process isn't. The count is kept in the profile across launches and starts again once it's turned on. The browser's dialog then says so over the window in front, “<name>” was turned off, "It crashed twice, so the browser turned it off. It stays off until you turn it on again.", with Turn On Again, in the Space's accent, as it takes nothing back, Keep Off, and Remove…, which asks first as Remove does. It asks once; if the dialog is busy or the browser went down first, it asks at the next launch. It's local and reversible: Chrome's extensions page turns it on too. The ad blocker the browser comes with is never turned off this way. Agents have no path to it. Arc's dialog and words: measure.
- **Desktop apps** (ours). An extension reaches its desktop app, as 1Password's and Bitwarden's do, through Chrome's native messaging. Those apps write their host's manifest only into the folders of the browsers they know, so a host that isn't in the browser's own folders comes from the first of Chrome's channels, Chromium, Edge's channels, Brave, Arc and Vivaldi that has it (`core/platform/native_hosts.h`). The browser's own folder always wins, and the manifest's `allowed_origins` still say which extensions may connect.
- **Keyboard commands.** Extension `commands` fire with the page focused and with the UI, before the page sees their keys, as in Chrome. An action's key runs it as a click on its button does. A key the user sets on Chrome's Keyboard Shortcuts page wins over ours, except the reserved ones. A key an extension suggests that's one of ours stays ours, as Chrome doesn't assign a suggested key that's taken; Chrome's page still lists it as the extension's, *interim*. Media keys don't run commands yet.
- **Site Controls**, the panel from the URL field's Site Controls button, and from the command bar's Site Controls (Arc has no key for it). Arc's look (Arc 1.170.0 cap, a dark Space): a popover 238 wide (four 48 pt tiles, 8 pt gaps, 11 in), radius 17, hanging from its button by a beak 12.5 tall with 45° sides, a 5 pt tip radius and 3 pt fillets at its foot. Its body's top is 9 under the URL field, and its rounded tip shows 1.5 over the field's bottom edge. It's centred on its button, kept 8 inside the window (ours), with the tip kept clear of the body's corners. With the sidebar hidden it hangs at the page's top left corner without a beak, *interim*. Its fill is 0x707387 at 0.30 over the window's see-through material, edged black 0.85 outside a white 0.10 rim, with a shadow of black 0.24, radius 16, 4 down. It's a child window with the keyboard (Over the page); escape, a click outside, another window or app, and its button close it. Agents have no path to it: what it changes is the user's, and the permissions are why (below).
  - Tiles, 11 in from the top: Arc's row and order, of the tiles whose features the browser has, now Share, New Boost (Edit Boost on a site with one, which opens the site's Boost card, Boosts), Capture a Portion of This Page and Capture Full Page, then the shield (ours, #2334); Copy Clean Link, Find and Picture in Picture are left to the command bar. The row stays 216 wide on the 8 pt gaps, its tiles sharing it equally on whole points: five of 37, 37, 36, 37 and 37 on the Mac, four of 48 where there's no share sheet, as on Linux. A tile is 32 tall, radius 8, a gradient from 0x282828 to 0x161616 with a white 0.20 line along its top, its glyph white 0.80, the ladder's white 0.12 over it under the pointer and 0.065 pressed (ours). Its tooltip names it. Share closes the panel and hangs macOS's share menu from the tile, with the page's URL and its title. The captures close the panel first, so the picture has none of it, then run the commands of the same names (Capture). ⌥ on Capture Full Page captures in Portrait Mode on the background kept instead, as Arc's ⌥-click on its Portrait Mode skips the panel. Their glyphs, Lucide's maximize and file, are *interim*: Arc's aren't recorded. The shield is ad blocking for the site (Picked by the owner › Ad blocking), in place of Arc's Block ads and trackers row, so it's always in reach without taking a line: Lucide's shield while it blocks, the accent over the plate at 0.16 and the glyph in the accent, 0.22 under the pointer and 0.28 pressed; Lucide's shield-off on the plain plate while ads are let through. Its tooltip and VoiceOver name say the state, "Ads and trackers blocked" or "Ads and trackers allowed", the first with how many requests it blocked on the page while there are any ("Ads and trackers blocked, 12 on this page"), and a click turns it the other way. A page Safari's engine shows has none, as the blocker never sees it.
  - Switches (ours, #2334), 8 under the tiles: only the site's own, Developer Mode while it's on (Developer Mode), Safari's engine for a site the profile keeps for it (Protected-media note), then the site's Boost (Boosts). A switch shows its state by its fill, as the shield does: tinted while on, the plain plate while off. One alone is a strip the panel's width and a tile's height, a 16 pt glyph 12 in and its state 10 after it in words, 13 pt medium: "Developer Mode on", "In Safari's engine" or "Safari's engine off", "Boost on" or "Boost off". Two or more are tiles of 104 × 52, two to a line on the 8 pt gaps, an odd one keeping its size, each a 16 pt glyph over its name in 11 pt medium, 5 under it. A click on Developer Mode's or the Boost's turns it the other way; Developer Mode's then goes, and "…" and the command bar turn it on again. Safari's engine's gives a menu of On and Off, the one it is checked: Off forgets the site, and every tab of the profile at the site goes back to Chromium at once. With none, nothing shows here.
  - Extensions: the heading in 14 pt semibold white 0.80, its cap 46 under the tiles' top, then 23 to a grid of 48 × 32 tiles on the 8 pt gaps, four to a row, on white 0.05 with radius 8. The grid lists every turned-on extension of the shown Space's profile, as it's the place to reach one that isn't pinned (ours, 2026-10-28): the pinned ones first, in the URL field's order, then the rest by name, wrapping to more rows with the + tile last. Turned-off ones stay off it. Each shows its extension's icon at 16 pt (ours), dimmed to 0.4 as in the toolbar when it doesn't act on the tab, and its title in a tooltip. A click runs it as its toolbar button does: the extension is granted the tab, and its popup hangs from the tile. The core reports the unpinned ones' action state only while the panel shows, so a tab switch with it closed pays nothing for them (principle 1). The + tile opens Chrome's extensions page, the browser's only way to add one, with Lucide's plus. The heading shows "Manage" at the panel's right, 12 pt in white 0.48, cap-centred with it, while the pointer is over it; a click opens Chrome's extensions page. A right-click on a tile gives its menu in the panel, dark as the panel is: Move to Top, dimmed on the first tile, then the pinned action's items, Unpin or Pin, Options if it has them, Remove if the user may, and Manage Extension, which opens its details on Chrome's extensions page. The side panel's item stays on the toolbar action's menu (ours). Move to Top makes the extension the first pinned, pinning it if it wasn't, as the grid's top is the pinned ones' (ours): Chrome has no call to reorder pinned actions, so the app pins it and pins the others again after it, in their order. Move to Top, Pin and Unpin keep the panel open while its grid redraws; Options, Manage Extension and Remove close it for what they open. Remove asks first in the browser window's dialog, as the toolbar action's Remove does. Agents get no path (#702).
  - This site (ours, #2334, in place of Arc's Settings rows): the heading, "This site", its cap 18 under the grid, then 17 to the lines, 28 tall and 30 apart. A line lists only what differs for the site, so the list is what's special about it: a permission whose value isn't the profile's default, or that the page asked about this session, as Chrome's page info lists it, and a site's logins. A line is Lucide's glyph for it at 16 pt, 8 in, in the accent while its value is on or Allow and the ink at 0.6 otherwise (ours, Arc's are SF Symbols); then Chrome's name in 13 pt, up to 132 wide, and Chrome's page info's word for the value, such as "Allowed" or "Not allowed", at the right in the secondary ink, which gives way first. A click gives the menu of the values Chrome offers for it, Allow, Block or Ask, the value it has checked. One a policy or an extension set shows at 0.50 and has no menu (ours). A site the profile keeps logins for has the first line, with Lucide's key-round and "Passwords" (ours, in place of Chrome's manage bubble), and as its value the username of its one login, "1 saved" for one without a username, "3 saved", or, drawn not allowed, "Never saved" for a site set to Never. The logins are those Chrome's password manager offers on the page: saved for any site of its registrable domain, or an affiliated one; Never is kept for the one site. Its menu has Remove “<username>”… (Remove Password Without Username… for one without) for each login, which closes the panel and asks in the browser window's dialog, "Remove the password for “<username>” on <site>?", with Cancel and a destructive Remove, as nothing brings it back; Offer to Save Passwords, which takes the site off Never at once, the offer coming again being its way back; and All Passwords…, the Settings section. A site with none has no line. Devices the site was given follow the permissions. Chrome's Reset permissions is the last line, with Lucide's rotate-ccw-clock and no value, showing only while a line it resets shows. A change applies at once, as Chrome's site settings do; Chrome's "reload to apply" bar isn't shown. "More" on the heading, as "Manage" on Extensions, opens Chrome's settings for the site's origin. With nothing to list, the heading goes (ours). Agents can't change them: a change could give a site the camera or where the user is.
  - A line in white 0.17, 9 under the last of This site's lines, or 15 under the grid with none, then 11 to the footer, 32 tall, with 11 under it. At its left the lock: a plate of white 0.05, radius 6, a 15 pt lock 10 in, then Chrome's short words for the connection, "Secure" or "Not secure", in 14 pt medium white 0.80, 6 after it, 8 to the plate's end; the browser's own pages show their site. Arc's lock is closed for Secure; the others' open lock is *interim*. A click shows macOS's certificate sheet for the page's certificate; without one, as for http, the plate is at 0.50 and takes no click. With more than one profile, the profile's chip sits just before "…" (#2339, ours: Arc's panel has none): a plate like the lock's with the Profiles glyph, the profile's name and a 12 pt chevron 4 after it, in the secondary ink. Beside it a secure page's lock is its glyph alone on a 32 pt square, with "Secure" in its tooltip and VoiceOver name, so the name has room. Any other lock keeps its words, as a page that isn't secure matters more than the profile, and the chip gives way instead: its name cuts at its end, and with less than 24 pt of room it goes, leaving the glyph and chevron. Beside "Not secure" that's always so. A cut name is whole in the chip's tooltip, and always in its VoiceOver name. Its menu has "Open in <profile>" for each other profile one of the user's Spaces is in, which opens the page in a new tab of the first of them in the sidebar's order, shown, as Little ilo's picker goes to a profile's first Space; it loads fresh there, as the profile's cookies are its own. A profile none of their Spaces is in offers "New Space in <profile>…", the New Space form in that profile. Set Profile isn't there, as only an empty Space changes profile. A private window, a Blank Window and a Little ilo window show no chip. At its right "…", a 32 pt circle of black 0.04 with Lucide's ellipsis, opens a native menu: Clear Cache and Clear Cookies, which also reload as the View menu's do; Turn On Developer Mode while it's off for a page that can have it, which closes the panel and turns it on as the command bar's command does; Manage Extensions… and Add Extension…, which both open Chrome's extensions page; and All Site Settings…, Chrome's settings for the site; then, on a page that can have a Boost, a line, New Boost… or Edit Boost…, and View Boosts… (Boosts).
  - The keyboard (#1382, ours: Arc's isn't recorded). Tab and ↓ take a ring down the panel in the order it's drawn: the tiles and the shield, the switches left to right and then down, the extensions' tiles and +, This site's lines a click changes, the lock while it shows a certificate, the profile's chip, and "…"; ⇧Tab and ↑ go back up. Tab and ⇧Tab go round from the last to the first, and the arrows stop at the ends. A line a policy sets, which takes no click, is passed over, and so are the headings' links, which show only under the pointer and are "…"'s items too. Space or return does what a click on the ringed one does: a line's menu hangs under it with its first item highlighted, as a menu from the keyboard does, the shield or a switch turns the other way, the profile's chip opens its menu, and a tile, the lock or "…"'s item runs and closes the panel. A tile or the shield, a glyph alone, shows its tooltip while it's ringed. The ring is Focus Sidebar's, 2 pt in the ink at 0.45, inside a tile's, a switch's, a line's, the lock's or "…"'s plate. A press takes it away, and escape still closes the panel.
  - It opens in 150 ms (ease-out) and closes in 100 ms (ease-in), growing from 0.9 of its size about its beak's tip and fading in, and shrinking back, *interim* until a recording.

### Passwords and autofill

Chrome's password manager and autofill fill pages. Our prompts replace Chrome's bubbles (Dia, P§16). No Dia values were measured: the layout uses the panel grid (**Ardia**) until Arc is measured.

- **Saving** (Chrome, Dia's prompt). After a login, Chrome's offer sits at the page's top right, 8 in from the card's corner and under the find bar while it shows, in Chrome's words: "Save password?" with Save, No Thanks and Never, or "Update password?" with Update Password and No Thanks for a changed password. Its username field can be edited or picked from the page's other fields that might hold it, and the password shows masked. It takes the keyboard only when clicked, as the find bar does: then Return saves and Escape is No Thanks, and the two buttons say so after their words, fainter, as the toast's Undo names ⌘Z: Save's ↩ as the dialog's glyph and No Thanks' esc (ours; the dialog's chips leave Never no room in the 320-wide card). Switching tabs closes a pending offer, and coming back doesn't bring it back; the key in the URL field does (Sidebar › Header and URL field). A form's address or card brings Chrome's offer to save it ("Save address?", "Save card?") in the same prompt, in Chrome's words, with Save and No thanks: the address's lines, or the card's name, last four digits and expiry, show in its two rows to read, and it takes the keyboard when clicked, as a login's does, since Chrome shows the bubble without taking it from the page. A card is saved to this device only, as ilo signs in to no Google account, and Chrome's offer to update a saved address shows nothing yet. An agent's tab gets none of these.
Settings › Passwords' Offer to save passwords turns the offer off for every site of a profile, not only one with Never; the profile still fills the logins it has. Its Save and fill addresses and Save and fill payment methods are Chrome's switches, which turn off both offering and filling them.
- **Filling** (Chrome). Chrome's dropdown sits under the focused field. ↓ ↩ fills every field. Edit › AutoFill › Contact…, Passwords… and Credit Card… open it at the field the page's focus is in, as Dia's do: Contact… and Credit Card… the dropdown a click in the field opens, with what the profile has saved for it, as Chrome no longer has a fallback listing every address or card, and Passwords… Chrome's Select Password, every login the profile has. With no field focused, or the keyboard outside the page, they open Settings › Passwords on the Space's profile. Linux and Windows show no menu bar: there a click in the field opens the dropdown, and the command bar's Passwords the settings page. The dropdown shows the user's addresses, cards and logins, so agents get no path, and an agent's tab has no dropdown.
- **Generation** (Chrome). Chrome's popup on new-password fields offers "Use Strong Password" and "Choose Your Own", with no Google sign-in. Its text says the password is saved on this device, and it is, in the profile, as a typed one is, with no offer to save it. An agent's tab gets none.
- **Reveal** (Dia; Blink's eye, Chrome's on Windows). An eye button appears in a page's password field once the user types into it empty, and a click on it or ⌥F8 shows the password. It goes, masking the password again, when the field loses focus or is emptied, and it never appears for a password filled in or set by the page, so a saved one is never shown. A tab becoming an agent's to act in, at the start of its session or as the user hands it back, first loses its page's focus, so no screenshot an agent takes shows a password the user did.
- **Seeing and removing** (ours: Arc's and Dia's aren't recorded). Chrome's other password bubbles, such as the confirmation after a save and the manage bubble, show nothing, and its key icon is the URL field's key. A site's logins are a row in its Site Controls (Extensions › Site Controls), and every login and every site set to Never is in Settings › Passwords, each removable, so Never isn't a one-way door. Under Settings › Passwords' boxes, Manage Passwords… opens Chrome's password manager page in a tab of a Space of the profile it lists, as Arc's Passwords setting opened Chromium's. A saved password is shown, copied, edited or exported only there, behind the page's own device check, which holds for five minutes, and agents, which open only http(s) pages, never reach it. Removing a saved login asks first, as no undo brings it back; taking a site off Never asks nothing.
- **Passkeys.** Chrome's WebAuthn dialogs appear centred over the page as child windows, over the ilo account's sign-in window as over a tab.
- **Agents.** Agents never read or type into password fields and never see autofill data (decision 8). There is no agent path to any of this: the core's login, address and card calls are the user's alone, agents hear none of their callbacks, and the core refuses an agent's `passwords` action, with why (Agents UI). Nor to a profile's settings in Settings' sections or › Passwords: the core refuses `profile_settings`, with why. The MCP server's tools are a fixed list, each asking the core only for an action on its allowlist, which the UI's tests hold to.
- Measure Arc's save prompt and password settings.

### Page dialogs

*Ours*: Arc's page dialogs aren't recorded, so these follow the principles and the dialog around them. What a page asks for itself, and what Chrome asks for a page, is the browser's dialog (Spaces › Renaming and deleting) over the tab's card, in place of Chrome's views, which have no window here.

- **Where.** The dim covers the tab's card alone, with its corners, and the card sits centred on it, so the dialog has the tab's place (Everything has a place) and the sidebar stays live: another tab, another Space and other windows can be chosen. It's tab-modal: it goes, with the dialog's motion, when another tab shows, and comes back when its tab does. Only the window in front shows it; another window's waits for it to come to the front. A click on the dim does nothing, as the page waits for an answer. It waits behind the browser's own dialog and Chrome's other questions, such as adding an extension.
- **Words.** Chrome's, with Chrome's title, "<origin> says", where Chrome has one. The buttons carry ESC and ↩ chips (Teach the keys where they're used). The action is in the Space's accent, as an answer to a page takes nothing back. While an input method composes in a field, ↩ is its.
- **Chrome's rules.** Chrome decides when a page may ask and what a dialog becomes as its tab hides or goes to another page: an alert from a tab that isn't shown lets the page go on at once and shows when the tab does, and a confirm or prompt from one is dropped; a tab that hides lets its alert's page go on, and the alert comes back with the tab, but cancels its confirm or prompt; a new dialog takes the place of the last, and going to another page takes it away. A page that asks in a loop is stopped as Chrome's is, by closing its tab, which the live sidebar allows; Chrome gave up the suppress checkbox for these.
- **alert, confirm and prompt.** The title, then the page's message as the detail. An alert has OK alone, confirm OK and Cancel, prompt a field under the message, holding the page's default, selected. ↩ is OK and Esc Cancel.
- **Leave site?** A page whose beforeunload handler asks, as the user goes from it, reloads it or closes its tab or window, asks in Chrome's words: "Leave site?" or "Reload site?" over "Changes you made may not be saved.", with Stay, which Chrome calls Cancel, and Leave or Reload in red, as leaving loses what was typed. ↩ leaves and Esc stays, and staying also stops a quit under way. Chrome asks these one at a time for the whole app, so a tab no window shows, such as one closed from its row, comes to the front window to ask. A page that asks again soon after gets Chrome's "Prevent this page from creating additional dialogs", and with it ticked it can't hold the user again.
- **Sign in.** A site's HTTP sign-in, basic or digest, asks in Chrome's words: "Sign in", the site's origin, and "Your connection to this site is not private" under it if it's plain HTTP, with a Username and a Password field, Cancel and Sign in. The username field has the keyboard, Tab goes to the password, which shows dots and won't copy, and ↩ signs in. It waits with its tab, as an alert does, and Chrome takes it away once another tab signs in to the same realm. Esc shows the page the site sent without signing in. A saved password isn't filled in yet (*interim*).
- **Open another app.** A link whose scheme another app handles, such as zoommtg: or maps:, asks in Chrome's words: "Open Maps?" over "<origin> wants to open this application.", with Cancel and Open Maps. Here ↩ is Cancel's and the action takes a click, as Chrome's dialog defaults to Cancel, so a key press the page timed can't open an app. From a secure origin it offers Chrome's "Always allow <origin> to open links of this type in the associated app", kept in the profile's prefs as Chrome keeps it, and a site going to another goes with its question. mailto: opens the mail app without asking, as in Chrome. A link that opened a tab only to start the app, as a meeting link can, closes that tab once the app has it, where the window keeps another, as Chrome's does (#685). A page that loads first and then starts the app, as Zoom's join page does, stays, as in Chrome.
- **Beside the question and the note.** A page's question and the protected-video note hang under the URL field, outside the dim, so they stay as they are while a dialog covers the card, and each is answered on its own. A tab showing its page in Safari's engine holds back the dialog its Chromium page asked until the tab is back in Chromium. A page in Safari's engine has no dialogs of its own yet: WebKit drops its alert, confirm and prompt (*interim*).
- **Agents.** Agents get nothing: no tool sees or answers a page's dialog, which goes to the user as anywhere. A sign-in in an agent's tab is the user's to give, so it's a handoff by its nature: the agent's page waits until the user signs in or cancels. A page in an agent's Space opens no other app, whoever clicked, and asks nothing, even where the user ticked "Always allow": agents act inside the browser alone. Nothing typed in a dialog is logged.

### Downloads

- **The Library's Downloads** (Arc 1.170.0 menus and cap). Downloads is a section of the Library, above Archived Tabs. ⇧⌘J and Window › View Downloads open the Library on it, and close it from there. The list is laid out as the archive's: search, then day headings, newest first.
  - Each row has the file's icon from the system, its name, and under it where it is: "48.2 MB of 121.5 MB" over a 2 pt bar while it runs, "Paused · …", "18 KB · data.example.org" once done, "Cancelled · …" or "Failed · …" with its site, and "May harm your computer" while Chrome holds it. Sizes count in thousands, as Finder's do. A row lights under the pointer as the archive's does.
  - A click opens a finished file, and on a held one shows its menu. A right-click shows what can be done with it: Keep and Delete while held; Pause or Resume, Cancel and Open When Done while it runs; Open, Show in Finder and Move to Trash once done. Then Remove from List and Clear List, which take finished downloads off the list and keep their files. Move to Trash puts the file in the system's Trash and takes its row off the list, or only the row if the file is gone already; a file that can't go keeps both.
  - A finished file's row moved 4 pt while pressed starts the system's drag of the file, with its icon under the pointer: Finder and other apps take a copy as macOS does, the page as Chromium does, and the tab list opens it as a tab, as files dropped on it. A press that doesn't move that far is a click. Ours (*interim* until Arc's is recorded): the Library closes as the drag starts, so the tab list under it shows, and comes back if nothing takes the file.
  - **Empty** (ours, no recording): as the archive's, one line in the list's place, "Files you download show here.", or "No downloads match." while a search finds nothing.
- **Footer button** (ours until measured; Arc's is recorded only with no download running). While a download runs, the footer's Library button keeps its glyph, its name, View Library ⇧⌘L, and its click, which opens the Library where it was, and shows a 16 × 2 bar of the running downloads' progress under the glyph. A download Chrome holds, until it's kept or deleted, or one whose size isn't known has no bar, as it would never move; Downloads lists both.
  - **Flight** (Arc 1.17x, measured by an outside reverse-engineering, *interim* until ours is recorded, #1406). A download that starts running while the sidebar is docked and the Library closed sends its file's icon from the pointer, or from the window's edge with the pointer outside, into a bin on the button. It crosses at a steady speed on a parabola whose top is 90 pt over where it left, landing 1.10 s later: 32 pt as it leaves, 48 at its top, then shrinking evenly to 10, and coming in from nothing by its top. It draws in the window over the page, so it crosses the page and the sidebar alike, and takes no clicks. Ours: where the system doesn't say where the pointer is (Linux, and a full-screen or maximized window), it leaves from the page's middle. Several starting together fly once, as the newest; downloads read at launch and ones that change state never fly.
  - **Bin** (Arc 1.17x, the same). The file's icon, 32 pt, its centre 9 right of and 12 above the glyph's, shows as the icon leaves, 6 pt high, drops to 12 under its place by 0.15 s, comes back to 2 over it and rests by 0.4 s. It leaves 30 s after the icon lands, fading over 0.15 s. Ours: it holds the file's icon, as the file is still coming where Arc's shows Quick Look's thumbnail; it takes no pointer, so the button under it keeps its name and click; the recent downloads opening send it away, as they list the file, and the Library opening, the sidebar hiding or a full-screen page take it at once; a newer download's flight takes its place. Under Reduce Motion the icon doesn't fly, and the bin shows still; nor does it on X11 with no compositor, where the window it crosses the page in would draw opaque over the page.
- **Recent downloads** (owner's capture of Arc, 2×). The Library button shows the newest finished downloads in rows over the foot of the sidebar, right above the footer, the newest at the bottom next to the button: one row per file, 56 apart, over the sidebar's own look with no card. A 40 pt column at 8 holds an image file's preview 40 wide, its aspect kept, at radius 4, or the system's icon for the file, 26 wide, centred. The name is at 61 in 15 pt white, truncated with "…", over its age in 12 pt grey: "just now", "5 minutes ago", "2 hours ago", "yesterday", "4 days ago", "1 week ago", "3 weeks ago", then the date, "Sep 7", with its year if it isn't this year's. They show 0.2 s into a rest on the button, ahead of its tooltip's 1.6 s, the bottom row first and each next one 25 ms later, each fading in over 0.18 s as it rises 5 pt over 0.12 s. They stay while the pointer is on the button, the rows or the 9 pt between them, and go 70 ms after it's on neither, the bottom row first and the top one 50 ms later, each fading out over 0.13 s where it is. Behind them the tab list is hidden from 3 pt over the top row, and comes back evenly over the 170 pt above that (Arc 1.17x, measured by an outside reverse-engineering, *interim*, #1406). Ours: a pointer back on the button as they go brings each row back from where it is, at once; the shade over the list fades with the rows, takes no pointer, and never reaches above the list's top, under the URL field; under Reduce Motion the rows fade without rising. Up to four, the newest finished ones with a file and those still coming, running, paused or held, from every profile, as the Library lists, and the captures saved to the Library, by when they were saved; with none, nothing shows. A right-click on the button picks what they list, as Arc's does: Images, Videos and Other Files, by the file's name, a line, then Captures (Arc's Screenshots, which are the system's; ours are the captures the Library keeps, as reading the system's folder would ask for the Desktop), each ticked while it's listed, all at first, and kept across a quit. One still coming says how much has come in its age's place, as its Downloads row does ("1.2 MB of 4.0 MB", "Paused · …"), takes its age once it's done, and gets its preview then. A held one, which Chrome keeps until it's kept or deleted, says "May harm your computer" there, as its Downloads row does, so the rows show it's waiting, and a click or right-click on it offers Keep and Delete. Ages are counted from when the download started, as it opens, and don't change while it shows. The rows draw over the tab list in the window's look, opaque, and take its pointer; a row lights under the pointer with a plate 4 in from the sidebar's sides at radius 10. A row does what a finished row of the Library's Downloads does: a click opens the file and the rows go, a press moved 4 pt drags the file out and the rows go (the Library stays closed if nothing takes it), and a right-click shows the row's menu. The Library opening, the sidebar hiding, the reveal going and a full-screen page take them away at once, and so does their last download leaving the list. Previews load off the main thread as the rows first open, once per file. In a private window they're its own session's downloads alone, and a regular window never shows a private one (#669).
- **Quarantine** (Chrome's, #685). On the Mac a finished download carries macOS's `com.apple.quarantine`, naming ilo as the app that downloaded it, so Gatekeeper checks the file the first time it opens.
- The list is per profile and persists across launches in Chrome's history, up to the newest 500; private windows' downloads are never written.
- **Old downloads leave the list** (Arc's release notes: downloads archive like tabs; the owner's call, #696). A download that has ended, done, cancelled or failed, leaves the Library's Downloads and the rows over the button after its profile's Archive Today tabs after, as Remove from List takes it: its file stays on the disk. Ours: the time counts from when it ended, on the wall clock, through sleep and while the browser isn't running, so a launch takes those whose time ran out, and with Never none leave. One running or paused stays. They don't go in Archived Tabs, which holds tabs.
- **Quit guard** (Chrome's). Quitting with downloads running asks first, on the Mac in Chrome's alert: "Download is in progress", with Continue Downloading or Quit. On Linux and Windows the app's dialog asks in the same words, with the app's name where Chrome's says Chromium, and ↩ on Continue Downloading, Chrome's default; Quit is red, as it can't be taken back. So does closing the last Incognito window with its downloads running, "Quit Incognito mode anyway?" as Chrome's Mac words have it. Continue Downloading keeps the browser running and shows the Library's Downloads, where Chrome would open its downloads page; Quit cancels them, and they come back cancelled. The alert names the product with Chromium's strings until branding lands. Arc's wording: measure.
- Measure Arc's Downloads section and its button while downloading, and record the flight, the bin and the recent downloads' motion to confirm #1406's values.

### Site notifications

- **A click shows the tab that sent it** (Arc's help centre, #685). A click on a notification from a site the user lets notify shows the tab that posted it: its Space and the tab in the window that holds it, which comes in front, before the page hears the click, as the page's own `window.focus()` would. A page whose handler focuses its tab too switches once. A notification whose page has gone gets Chrome's new tab at the site, and a service worker's click is the worker's to handle.

### Find in page

- **Keys** (Arc 1.170.0 menus, Dia P§8). ⌘F, ⌘G, ⇧⌘G and ⇧↩ in the bar for previous. ⌘J, Jump to Selection, scrolls the page to put its selection in the middle, such as the match a find left selected. Arc has no Use Selection for Find (⌘E), so neither do we. Its Find and Replace (⌥⌘F) acts only in AppKit's text views, which neither a page nor our fields are, so we leave it out.
- **State.** Each tab keeps its own query and shows "n of m".
- **Find bar.** It floats over the page, so it is a child window. With the page focused, ⌘F reaches us unless the page keeps it (as Google Docs does). Japanese IME works in the field. ⌘V right after ⌘F lands in the field.
- **Arc's bar** (Arc 1.170.0 rec ×3, cap, ax, on iana.org).
  - It's 300 × 32, 10 in from the card's top and right edges, radius ≈ 6, fill (19,29,50), with a soft shadow mostly below, ≈ 16 pt.
  - From its left edge, all 28 pt squares 2 pt from the top: the search glyph at 2; the field at 31, 152.5 wide and 18 tall; the count ("1/12") at 185.5; previous and next at 212 and 238; a divider; close at 270.
  - With no match, "NOT FOUND" in capitals and red (219,25,41) takes the place of the count and the arrows.
  - It opens and closes in one frame, with no animation.
- Ours: Arc's layout and fill, (19,29,50), a navy unlike the window's colour beside it in Arc's capture, the one Space captured; whether it follows the Space isn't recorded. AppKit's window shadow stands in for Arc's: one drawn in the bar's window would need room round the bar that takes the page's clicks. The glyphs are Lucide's search, chevrons and x at 14 pt, in the header's colours, on the ladder's white 0.12 under the pointer and 0.065 pressed; the count is 12 pt and NOT FOUND 11 pt semibold. Previous, next and close name themselves and their keys in the shared tooltip, hung under the button as the header's are: Previous ⇧⌘G, Next ⌘G, Close ⎋; a click or a key ends it.

### Zoom

- **Keys.** ⌘0, ⌘+ and ⌘- in the View menu, as Arc's Zoom to Actual Size, Zoom In and Zoom Out, and ⌘= as Chrome's hidden second ⌘+. Chrome's levels, for the page's whole site, which the profile keeps.
- **HUD** (Arc 1.170 rec at 2×, five zooms and a tab switch). A zoom shows its percent ("125%", "100%") on a pill in a child window over the card, in the toast's style: 41.5 tall, 8 inside the card's top right corner, the percent 14 pt medium and 16 in from the pill's right, the window's colour darkening down it, the toast's rim and shadow. The URL field shows nothing. Ours is 8 pt under the find bar while that's open, and the mouse goes through it.
- **Timing.** It stays after the last zoom until another tab shows, which takes it at once. Arc's stayed 4.9 s before a tab switch took it, so ours stays 5 s (*interim*: how long Arc's stays alone isn't recorded). Motion › Zoom HUD.

### Link target

The address of the link the pointer is on, as Chrome's status bubble shows it. Ours (*interim*): Arc's isn't recorded, so it's designed from Quiet controls, Wait for intent and Readable at once, and the toast's and tooltip's surfaces. Its 1.5 s to the whole address is Arc's, from its release notes and help centre.

- **Plate.** A child window over the card, 24 tall at radius 6, 8 inside the card's bottom left corner, in the window's fill with the popover's 0.5 pt rim. The address is 11 pt, one line, 10 in from each end, in the secondary colour (white 0.60) with the site, the registrable domain (eTLD+1), in the primary colour. The address is the core's: no `https://`, and a host in an IDN's script as the URL field shows it. Past half the card's width (160 to 480) it loses its middle, never the site and never the path's end; a site wider than the plate is cut at its end. Once it has waited for the whole address (Timing), it's as wide as the address up to the card's width inside its margins, and 1200 at most, about 200 characters, past which it still loses its middle. It snaps wider in its corner, as its other changes snap, stays whole for the next links while it shows, as Chrome's bubble does, and starts at half the card again once the pointer has left them. The mouse goes through it.
- **Timing.** It shows after the pointer has rested on a link for 150 ms, so a pointer crossing the page shows nothing, and in the frame it's drawn. Moving to another link within 400 ms of the last shows at once. After 1.5 s on the same link, Arc's (Chrome's waits 1.6 s), it shows the whole address; one that fits waits for nothing. It fades out over 120 ms when the pointer leaves the link, and the same when a tab switch, the Library or a page's full screen takes it.
- **Getting out of the way.** The pointer within 16 pt of the plate sends it to the card's bottom right corner, as Chrome's bubble goes, and back the same way. It snaps, with no slide. It stays on the side it moved to across links and starts on the left again once the pointer has left them.
- **Scope.** Only the tab the window shows, in a split any shown pane's, at the card's corner. Not while the Library is open, nor in a page's full screen.
- **Agents.** None: it's display, with no page data an agent doesn't already get.
- The core only reports the change (`tab_link_target`), through a hook in Chromium's `UpdateTargetURL`, since Chrome's status bubble is a view of Chromium's own the window doesn't have. Nothing polls: the window listens for the pointer's moves only while the plate shows.

### DevTools

Chrome's DevTools (Dia, P§23), docked in the card as Arc's.

- **Keys.** ⌥⌘I, or F12 as in Dia and Chrome, opens or closes them, ⌥⌘J opens or closes them at the console, ⌥⌘C opens them picking an element, and ⌥⌘U opens the page's source in a `view-source:` tab under it. They're Arc's View › Developer items: View Source, Developer Tools, Inspect Elements and JavaScript Console. From the page and from DevTools they reach the UI unless the page keeps them. The page menu's Inspect opens them at the element under the pointer. Dia and Chrome also have F12.
- **Arc's docking** (Arc 1.170.0 cap). On the right inside the card, full height, with their own 26 pt toolbar at the card's top. The page keeps the card's left part: 853 of 1274, so DevTools take a third. They open in two steps with no animation: an empty dark pane at once, then DevTools drawn in it ≈140 ms later with the page laid out narrower. ⌥⌘I again closes them.
- **Docking.** As Chrome's window does it: DevTools fill the card, and the page goes where they leave it, rounded at the card's corners it reaches. Each tab has its own; a tab without them shows its page across the card. Closing them gives the page the keyboard back. DevTools' own menu docks them on another side, where the page goes the same way.
- Ours: Chrome's width, 555 pt the first time, then wherever the user drags the divider, which DevTools keep per profile. Arc's third may be the capture's saved width.
- **Undocked.** DevTools' Dock side menu undocks them into Chrome's own window, which takes the keyboard, and docks them again. There, ⌥⌘I from the page brings them to the front rather than closing them, as Chrome's toggle does and Arc's, and ⌘C copies in them as in any page. Dia's is titled "Developer Tools - <url>", opens where it was last left, and is 640 × 640 the first time.
- **Task Manager.** Help › Troubleshooting › Open Task Manager, and Open Task Manager in the command bar, open Chrome's in a window of its own, with the page shown selected. As in Chrome, ⌘W closes it, as it closes any window of Chrome's that has the keyboard, and the tab the browser window shows stays.
- **Record a Trace.** Help › Troubleshooting › Record a Trace, and Record a Trace in the command bar, record Chromium's trace of every process, with Chrome's default categories, keeping the latest events once its buffer is full. Nothing records until then. While it records, a toast says "Recording a trace" with Stop. It stays until Stop, and comes back after any other toast. The command bar and the menu offer Stop Recording Trace in its place, in every window. Stop asks where to save the trace through the save panel, as Chromium's JSON, named "ilo Trace.json" in Downloads. Cancel drops it, and it's sent nowhere else. Arc's was one of its services' items. Agents get no tool for it (owner's call, 2026-10-29).
- **Agents.** DevTools are for the user. Agents get no DevTools and no remote-debugging port (decision 8).

### Developer Mode

A tab mode for working on a site, as Arc's (once Dev Tab). Nothing of it is recorded, so its look is ours, from the surfaces around it. It's the user's view: agents get no call for it.

- **Which sites.** A flag the profile keeps per site, the page's registrable domain or, without one, its host. localhost, its subdomains, 127.0.0.1 and [::1] are on by themselves (Chrome's `net::IsLocalhost`); other sites are off. The user turns a site on or off, and a site set back to its default isn't kept. Only http(s) pages have it. A private window's tabs follow the profile's sites and can't change them, as a private window keeps nothing. A change reaches every tab of the profile at the site at once.
- **Site Controls.** A switch with Lucide's square-code, under the action row while Developer Mode is on, which a click turns off (Extensions › Site Controls); off, "…" has Turn On Developer Mode. A page outside http(s), or in a private window, has none. Arc's row isn't recorded.
- **Command bar.** "Turn on Developer Mode for this site", Arc's View menu item's words in sentence case, or "Turn off Developer Mode for this site" while it's on: the bar lists the one that would change it, for an http(s) page outside a private window, with the row's glyph. "dev mode" and "localhost" find them too (ours).
- **Full URL.** The URL field says the page's whole URL, its scheme and all, as Chrome's Always Show Full URLs does, in place of the host alone (URL field text). Arc's isn't recorded.
- **DevTools button** (ours; Arc's developer buttons aren't recorded). In Developer Mode the URL field has one more button, DevTools, 28 before Copy URL (› Field buttons), with Lucide's code glyph and the field buttons' size, glyph colour, hover plate and fade. A click opens or closes the page's DevTools as ⌥⌘I does, and its tooltip names Developer Tools with ⌥⌘I. Nothing else joins it: no second button and no port switcher. Agents get no path to it, as they get no DevTools (Agents get sessions, never secrets).
- **URL field stays.** Arc keeps its URL bar in view for a tab in Developer Mode when its toolbar is hidden (⇧⌘D), so switching tabs doesn't move the page. Ours has no Hide Toolbar: the URL field hides only with the sidebar, in page full screen, for a form or the Library, never by tab, so it holds; a toolbar that hides by itself keeps the field for such a tab.
- **Look.** The tab shown has a dashed outline, 1 pt in the ink at 0.62, inside its row's corners over a fill of 0.26 in place of 0.20, or in place of its favorite tile's ring; in a split view the split's row has it while the focused pane is in Developer Mode. Every tab in Developer Mode keeps its title in the full ink, where another's is 0.78, with its host under it as Arc's (Tab rows › Host), and a favorite's tile rests at 0.16 in place of 0.10, so they stand apart in the sidebar. Ours: Arc's isn't recorded, and its yellow and black aren't ilo's; a dashed line reads as work in progress and, in the ink, on every theme.
- **View › Developer.** Its last item, after a separator, by the same two names, for the page shown; with no such page it's left out, as Arc's shows only with a page.
- **⌃D.** Arc's Toggle Developer Mode, as Settings › Shortcuts lists it, which can give it another key. It isn't reserved: a page gets it first, so a text field in the page, where ⌃D deletes forward on the Mac, keeps it, as a page that handles it does, and so do the UI's fields, the command bar's too. Anywhere else, from the page or the UI, it turns the shown page's site on or off. Off the Mac it would be Alt+D, which stays Chrome's (Keyboard), so it has no key there.
- Ours: no JSON viewer in 0.1.0, where entering Arc's installs the JSON Formatter extension (the owner, 2026-10-30); a built-in one would be its own item after it.
- **Little ilo.** A Little ilo window's page turns Developer Mode on and off by the command bar, View › Developer and ⌃D, as a regular window's does, its bar saying the whole URL while it's on. ⌘O takes the page to the main window in it, as Arc's Little Arc does.

### Capture

Arc's Capture (Arc 1.170 cap ×11, at 1 pt a pixel; no recording, so no motion is measured). Three commands: Capture a Portion of This Page (⇧⌘2, File › Capture… and "Capture" in the command bar), Capture Full Page, and Capture in Portrait Mode (both in File and the command bar). Site Controls' two capture tiles run the first two. None has a key, as in Arc, but each can be given one in Settings › Shortcuts.

- **Capture mode.** ⇧⌘2, from the page too, covers the focused pane's page with a still picture of it in a child window with the keyboard (Overlays). The page around what's lit is grey: Rec. 601 luma × 0.749, which gives Arc's grey to the level at the three points measured. What's lit shows in its colours inside a 4 pt ring at radius 6, outside the box: Arc's is (21,28,47), ours the Space's accent. The pointer is a camera.
- **Pill.** "Click or drag to capture a portion of this page", centred on a pill 356 × 67, its bottom 35.5 above the page's. It shows only while choosing. Arc's is 14 pt semibold (71,28,157) on white at radius 18, with a lilac glow round it. Ours is 13 pt semibold on ilo's menu's panel (`app/src/menu.rs`, `[menu]` in the tokens) at radius 16: its fill, rim, popover shadow and label in the window's look, Paper in a light window and Ink in a dark one (#829).
- **Choosing.** The pointer over the page lights the element under it: the core hit tests the page and answers with the element's border box, with no page JavaScript. A click takes that box and a drag the rectangle dragged. Esc, ⇧⌘2 again, a click in another window or app, or a resize leaves.
- **⇧⌘-drag.** On a page, with capture mode off, a ⇧⌘-drag (Ctrl+Shift off the Mac) starts capture mode already dragging and takes the rectangle dragged at once, as a drag in capture mode does. Arc's help centre gives Cutout this gesture; Arc 1.170 isn't recorded doing it, and the owner chose it on 2026-10-29 (#673). ⇧⌘2 stays. Ours: the core takes the press before the page hears it, and it's a drag past 4 pt, as in capture mode; a ⇧⌘-click let go before that is the page's, so a link still opens in a new tab.
- **Menu.** Once chosen, a menu 10 in from the ring's left and level with its top, inside the page, with a Send To… field at its top and a glyph before each row's title. Arc's is 273 wide at radius 7, fill (18,15,44); its field 10 in, 13 down, 29.5 tall, fill (35,32,59), its text (170,169,180) 10 in; then from 7.5 below it rows 40 tall, a 14 pt glyph at 18 in white 0.3 and 14 pt medium white text at 42; lines between groups take 9 pt, white 0.06, and 6 pt close the menu. Ours is ilo's menu, as the tab and Space menus are, in the window's look (#829): its panel at its narrowest, 200, with its padding, radius, rim and shadow, the field a 24 pt row tinted the line colour with the label's words, a line, then its 24 pt rows of 13 pt text, each with a 14 pt glyph in the secondary colour before the title, and its lines. Under the pointer a row is the Space's accent with the label solved on it, the accent pressed while the button is down. Arc's rows: Save to Library, Copy, Save…, Send via iMessage…, Edit, a line, Retake, New Easel. Ours leave out New Easel until Easel exists; each is an `Action` and a row in `capture.rs`. A private window's menu has no Save to Library, as a capture saved there would be written to disk (ours).
- **What the rows do.** Save to Library saves the PNG, without a question, into the Library's Media, a folder in the profile directory, named as the system names a screenshot ("Capture 2026-10-29 at 18.04.12.png", a number after it if a second capture comes in the same second), and the toast says "Saved to Library" (ours: Arc's words after it aren't recorded). Copy puts the PNG on the pasteboard and shows the toast "Screenshot Copied to Clipboard" with a Save chip and a share glyph's chip (Toast). Save… shows the save panel, Send via iMessage… a new message in Messages, Send To… the system's share menu at the field, and Retake goes back to choosing.
- **Edit** (#673). Marks on what's chosen before another row takes it, as Arc's Edit opens the picture for markup. Arc's markup isn't recorded, so ours (*interim*): Edit puts a bar in the menu's panel where the menu was, 8 under the ring at its left, or over it where the page has no room under it, inside the page. It holds a Pen, an Arrow and a Box (Lucide's pencil, arrow-up-right and square), seven colours (red first, then orange, yellow, green, blue, black and white, each a 16 pt dot), Undo and Done, each a 28 pt button 4 apart, the tool and colour chosen on the Space's accent. A drag on what's chosen draws, inside it: the pen where the pointer goes, the arrow straight with a 14 pt head at ±30° where it's let go, the box from corner to corner, each 4 pt wide with round ends; a click is a pen's dot, and no arrow or box. The pointer is a crosshair. ⌘Z or Undo takes the last mark back. Done or ↩ keeps the marks and brings the menu back, whose rows take the picture with them drawn on it at its scale; Esc takes back that Edit's marks and brings the menu back, and a press off what's chosen does nothing. Retake drops them. No Text tool yet, as a field over a picture needs the IME surface of its own.
- **Full page.** The toast says "Capture in Progress", then the picture of the whole page, past its view, is copied and the toast is the one after Copy.
- **Portrait Mode.** Arc's Capture in Portrait Mode (Arc's 2023 release notes; no capture, so its sizes aren't measured) asks what to put the page on, then copies the page's view on it with its window's corners and a shadow, and shows the toast after Copy. Arc also shows it in its Developer Mode toolbar; ilo's keeps one button, DevTools, so Portrait Mode is in the command bar there (#2124, #673).
  - Arc's offers its desktop picture first, white, or a colour from a slider. Ours offers the desktop's picture (the Mac and Windows; not Linux, whose sandbox can't read it, #2682), white, and the theme picker's nine colours. It's read at Capture; one that can't be, as Windows Spotlight's, gives white.
  - The card hangs under the URL field, where a page's question hangs. It brings a hidden sidebar out, and has Not Now, Capture, × and Escape.
  - The background chosen is kept in `Portrait Background` in the profile directory, and the next card starts on it.
  - ⌥ on Site Controls' Capture Full Page captures on the kept background without the card, as Arc's ⌥-click skips its panel. A Little ilo window has no URL field, so it captures at once.
  - Ours (*interim*): the page sits 56 pt from the picture's edges, its shadow 12 pt down with a 40 pt blur at 0.3 (`[capture.portrait]`), and the swatches are 18 pt circles, the chosen one ringed in the Space's accent. Arc puts its mark on the picture; ours puts nothing on it.
- **Pictures.** At the display's scale: what's chosen from the compositor's last frame, the full page from DevTools' screenshot beyond the view.
- **Agents.** No agent path. A capture shows whatever the page holds, which may be what decision 3 keeps from agents, and the pasteboard and share menu take it out of the browser. Agents read pages through their own tool, with its permission rule and activity log.
- Ours (*interim*, as no capture shows them): Send To… opens the system's share menu, where Arc's field lists people and apps of its own; a press is a drag past 4 pt; a click away from the menu leaves; the toast stays 5 s, and while the pointer is over it, with the ladder's 0.20 over a chip's white 0.1 under the pointer and 0.16 pressed; the file is "Capture yyyy-MM-dd at HH.mm.ss.png"; the pasteboard gets PNG only; the camera, Lucide's, has no flash dot; a drag's rectangle has the ring; Arc's choice of a larger element than the one under the pointer, the pinch zoom and the full page's flow aren't copied; a failure only logs.

### Import from other browsers

The user runs it; there is no agent path, because it reads the user's other browsers' data. It never decrypts another browser's saved passwords or cookies, from Chrome's family, Firefox (NSS) or Safari (the Keychain): passwords come from a CSV file the user exports from the other browser, behind that browser's own sign-in, through Chromium's password importer, and cookies don't come, so the user signs in again.

- **From Arc.** Arc Spaces become our Spaces and Arc profiles become our profiles (decision 5; Ardia maps Spaces to profiles). Favorites come in as favorites and each Space's pinned tabs as its pinned tabs (Dia 1.52 imports Arc this way, P§20).
  - Custom tab names come along.
  - Each Space's theme comes from Arc's `windowTheme`: colours in extended sRGB, a third overlay colour, intensity, noise, texture, appearance, translucency style, and separate light and dark looks (Arc 1.167 schema, P§20).
  - Arc's Air Traffic Control rules come in as link rules (Link rules).
  - Ours, as Arc's importer isn't recorded: a Space of the same name in the same profile takes its pinned tabs, and a profile of the same name as Arc's (its Local State name) takes its Spaces, so a rerun adds nothing. Folders come as folders, nested, and only http(s) and file tabs come. A pinned split view comes as a split view, with the name the user gave it, its icon if it's an emoji, as only an emoji is a split's icon here, and stacked if Arc's is vertical (Convert to Vertical Split View). Its tabs that don't come leave it, one left comes as a tab, and past the third, as a split here holds three, the rest come after it as tabs. A split among the favorites comes as its tabs, as an import can't make a favorite split. A rerun adds only the tabs that aren't in the split's place, as a split if two or more (#1597). Each Space's Today tabs, Arc's `unpinned` container, come as its Today tabs, in Arc's order, under Open tabs, a switch of their own, on by default as every switch is; a split view among them comes as its tabs, as the import adds Today tabs one by one, and one the Space has pinned or open already doesn't come. With Spaces switched off, a Space with Today tabs still comes for them, without its pinned tabs or link rules, as a Today tab belongs to a Space. Arc's Chromium session isn't read, as its sidebar is what it had open. Its archived tabs (`StorableArchiveItems.json`) stay in Arc. Arc's colours, placed freely on its picker, become our theme's colours with its count, intensity, noise, texture and appearance, each after the first at its own hue and lightness in the field, at the first's saturation, which is the theme's, its third colour also coming exactly, and its light and dark looks become the theme's two looks. Arc's grain, sand, denim and tweed become our Noise, Dust, Weave and Fleck, and a texture it names otherwise becomes Noise. The appearance is Arc's `windowAppearance` where it names one; with none, a theme with both looks is automatic and a theme of one look takes its `translucencyStyle`. A theme forced dark takes its dark look, and a basic colour comes without noise. Tabs come in unloaded, loading when first shown.
- **From Chrome** (P§20).
  - It brings bookmarks, history, open tabs, addresses and cards, and passwords from the user's exported file.
  - The source's Web Store extensions are listed with links to the store and never installed silently. Extensions, a switch of its own for Chrome's family and Arc, reads them from each profile's `Extensions` folder, by the names in their manifests, and the summary counts them. They're listed, by name, under the import's summary and under the browsers in Settings › Import & Export until each is added or the app quits, each with Add…, which opens its Web Store page in a tab behind the one shown and takes it off the list. Themes, apps and extensions from outside the Web Store stay out.
  - An extension pinned in the other browser's toolbar keeps its pin: its row says so, and once it's added from the list it's pinned here, once (owner, 2026-10-29).
  - Another ad blocker, as uBlock Origin is built in (Ad blocking), stays off the list (uBlock Origin, AdBlock, Adblock Plus, AdGuard, Ghostery, and others by their names), and one line says so once, with Add Anyway, which adds it as Add… does (owner, 2026-10-29).
  - Full Disk Access, ours, where macOS asks for it: Safari's folder, which macOS keeps from other apps. As Safari is picked, before its choices, the import says why, gives the three steps (System Settings › Privacy & Security › Full Disk Access, turn on ilo, come back), "Waiting for access…", and Open System Settings, which opens that list. It reads again each time the user comes back to the app, showing the choices once it can, and else "Still no access. If you turned it on, quit ilo and open it again." Only the Mac has the step; the other browsers, and every browser on Windows and Linux, go straight to their choices.
  - Running the import again adds nothing new.
- **From the rest of Chrome's family** (Chrome, Dia, Edge, Brave, Vivaldi, Opera, Chromium, Helium), ours, as Arc's importer isn't measured: Arc has no bookmarks bar, so a browser's bookmarks land in a Space named after it, one for each of its profiles when it has more ("Chrome – Work"), in the profile matched as Arc's are. The bar's bookmarks are its first pinned tabs and folders, or a folder of the bar's name once it holds more than 12, as more would push Today off the sidebar; then the other bookmarks and the phone's, each in a folder of its name. Bookmarklets and the browser's own pages stay out, and so do folders left empty. Arc's own bookmarks don't come, as Arc doesn't show them. Open tabs, a switch of their own, come from each profile's last session, its newest whole `Sessions/Session_*` as Chromium picks it, as that Space's Today tabs, unloaded, window by window in the browser's order; popups' and apps' tabs stay out, and so does one the Space has pinned or open already. Chromium writes the session encrypted too, which is never read, so a browser that writes only that copy brings none; its `Tabs_*` are closed tabs, not open ones. Addresses, a switch of their own and Arc's too, come from each profile's `Web Data`, the user's own and their account's, field by field as Chromium keeps them, into the matched profile's, but for one it has. Cards, encrypted beside them, never come, as the import never decrypts.
- **Choosing folders** (owner, 2026-10-29): under the switches, while bookmarks come, Choose folders opens a box of what they come in as, each with a checkbox, all ticked: the bar's own bookmarks by the bar's name (Bookmarks Bar, Bookmarks Toolbar, Favorites Bar), each of its folders, or its folder once it's too long, and the other top folders, once each however many profiles have one. An unticked one stays out. Import with none opened brings them all, so the simple path stays one click.
- **From Firefox and its family** (Firefox, Zen, LibreWolf), ours: the same, from each profile's `places.sqlite`, its default the one Firefox starts with, which goes to the profile this browser started with. The toolbar is the bar; the menu, the other bookmarks and the phone's come in folders of Firefox's names for them, and tags stay out.
- **From Safari**, ours: the same, its Favorites Bar as the bar, its menu with the bookmarks outside any folder in a Bookmarks Menu folder, the user's own top folders after it, and the Reading List, a switch of its own, in a Reading List folder.
- **A bookmarks file**, ours: the first row of a box under the browsers', Bookmarks, "A bookmarks file, in HTML, which every browser exports." and Choose File…, for a browser not found here or another machine's. It reads the Netscape format every browser exports into a Space named Bookmarks, in the profile the browser started with, as a browser's own bookmarks come: the bar's (`PERSONAL_TOOLBAR_FOLDER`) first, then those outside any folder in a folder named after the file's heading, as Firefox's menu, then the other folders; a file with no bar has those outside any folder as its bar. Running it again adds nothing new, and a file that isn't one says so.
- **Passwords**, from a file, ours: the box's second row, Passwords, "Export them from <the browser used last> as a file, then choose it here." and Choose File…, which opens the system's open panel; under the box a grey line for each browser found, once however many times it's installed, else for the four most people come from on that system (the Mac's Arc, Chrome, Firefox and Safari; Windows' Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Arc; Linux's Firefox, Chrome, Chromium and Brave), on where its export is (Chrome's family at its password-manager/settings › Export passwords, Firefox's family at about:logins › ••• › Export Passwords…, Safari's in the Passwords app, File › Export All Passwords to File…). Chromium's importer reads the file away from the browser, as Chrome's password manager's Import passwords does, into the profile of the Space the front window shows, else the one the browser started with, never an agent's. A login the profile holds with another password keeps it, where Chrome's page asks which to replace. The end says how many came in, and to which profile when there are more than one, how many conflicted and how many rows weren't logins, with Delete File before Done, as the file holds the passwords in plain text; the browser keeps no copy. Nothing of it reaches agents: not the file, its rows or its counts.
